# 21. Customer → Orders

Backend interview me realistic example:

```java
class Customer {

    String name;

    List<Order> orders;
}
```

Question:

> All customers ke all orders find karo.

```java
List<Order> orders =
        customers.stream()
                 .flatMap(customer ->
                         customer.getOrders().stream())
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Mental pattern:

```text
Customer
   ↓
List<Order>
   ↓
flatMap
   ↓
Order
```

---

# 22. Order → Items

```java
class Order {

    long orderId;

    List<Item> items;
}
```

Question:

> All orders ke all items find karo.

```java
List<Item> items =
        orders.stream()
              .flatMap(order ->
                      order.getItems().stream())
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 23. Customer → Orders → Items

Ab advanced scenario:

```text
Customer
   ↓
Orders
   ↓
Items
```

Nested:

```text
Customer
 ↓
List<Order>
 ↓
Each Order
 ↓
List<Item>
```

Code:

```java
List<Item> items =
        customers.stream()

                 .flatMap(customer ->
                         customer.getOrders().stream())

                 .flatMap(order ->
                         order.getItems().stream())

                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 Multiple `flatMap()` ka perfect example.

---

# 24. Visualize Multiple FlatMaps

Initially:

```text
List<Customer>
```

First flatMap:

```text
List<Customer>
      ↓
flatMap(Customer → Orders)
      ↓
Stream<Order>
```

Second flatMap:

```text
Stream<Order>
      ↓
flatMap(Order → Items)
      ↓
Stream<Item>
```

Finally:

```text
Stream<Item>
      ↓
collect
      ↓
List<Item>
```

---

# 25. String Sentence Example

Suppose:

```java
List<String> sentences = Arrays.asList(
        "Java is powerful",
        "Spring Boot is easy",
        "Java Stream API"
);
```

Question:

> Saare individual words chahiye.

Each sentence:

```text
"Java is powerful"
        ↓
split()
        ↓
["Java","is","powerful"]
```

Har sentence ek array produce karta hai.

So `flatMap()`.

```java
List<String> words =
        sentences.stream()
                 .flatMap(sentence ->
                         Arrays.stream(sentence.split(" ")))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
Java
is
powerful
Spring
Boot
is
easy
Java
Stream
API
```

---

# 26. Unique Words From Sentences

```java
List<String> words =
        sentences.stream()
                 .flatMap(sentence ->
                         Arrays.stream(sentence.split(" ")))
                 .distinct()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 27. Unique Words Sorted

```java
List<String> words =
        sentences.stream()
                 .flatMap(sentence ->
                         Arrays.stream(sentence.split(" ")))
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 28. Word Frequency From Multiple Sentences

🔥 Ab previous modules combine karte hain.

Question:

> Multiple sentences me har word kitni baar aya hai?

```java
Map<String, Long> frequency =
        sentences.stream()
                 .flatMap(sentence ->
                         Arrays.stream(sentence.split(" ")))
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Function.identity(),
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

Pipeline:

```text
Sentences
    ↓
flatMap
    ↓
Words
    ↓
groupingBy
    ↓
counting
    ↓
Frequency
```

---

# 29. FlatMap With Arrays

Suppose:

```java
List<String[]> list = Arrays.asList(
        new String[]{"Java", "Spring"},
        new String[]{"AWS", "Docker"},
        new String[]{"Kafka", "Redis"}
);
```

Flatten:

```java
List<String> result =
        list.stream()
            .flatMap(Arrays::stream)
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 30. `flatMap()` Kis Functional Interface Ko Use Karta Hai?

Conceptually:

```java
Function<T, Stream<R>>
```

`map()` me function:

```text
T → R
```

`flatMap()` me:

```text
T → Stream<R>
```

Then `flatMap()` saare returned streams ko ek stream me flatten karta hai.

---

# ⭐ map() vs flatMap() — Interview Table

| `map()`                        | `flatMap()`                              |
| ------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- |
| Transform                      | Transform + Flatten                      |
| One element → one result       | One element → multiple elements possible |
| `T → R`                        | `T → Stream<R>`                          |
| Nested structure reh sakta hai | Nested structure flatten hota hai        |
| `Stream<List<T>>` possible     | `Stream<T>` mil sakta hai                |

---

# Sabse Easy Example

Suppose Employee:

```java
Employee::getName
```

returns:

```text
String
```

Use:

```java
map(Employee::getName)
```

Because:

```text
Employee → String
```

But:

```java
Employee::getSkills
```

returns:

```text
List<String>
```

Agar hume **all skills individually** chahiye:

```java
flatMap(emp -> emp.getSkills().stream())
```

Because:

```text
Employee
   ↓
Multiple Skills
```

---

# 🧠 Sabse Important Decision Rule

Question dekho aur pucho:

> **Ek input object ke andar multiple values hain kya?**

Example:

```text
Employee → Multiple Skills
Customer → Multiple Orders
Order → Multiple Items
Department → Multiple Employees
Sentence → Multiple Words
List → Multiple Elements
```

YES?

Think:

```java
flatMap()
```

---

# `map()` Kab?

```text
Employee → Name
Employee → Salary
Employee → Age
Product  → Price
Student  → Marks
```

One object → one value.

```java
map()
```

---

# `flatMap()` Kab?

```text
Employee → Skills
Customer → Orders
Order → Items
Department → Employees
Sentence → Words
```

One object → multiple values.

```java
flatMap()
```

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. `flatMap()` kya karta hai?

**Answer:**

`flatMap()` har stream element ko ek stream me transform karta hai aur phir multiple streams ko flatten karke ek single stream banata hai.

Simple:

```text
Nested Data → Single Stream
```

---

### Q2. `map()` aur `flatMap()` me difference?

**Hindi-English Interview Answer:**

`map()` one-to-one transformation ke liye use hota hai, whereas `flatMap()` one-to-many nested data ko transform aur flatten karke single stream banata hai.

---

### Q3. Employee ke names extract karne hain. `map()` ya `flatMap()`?

```java
map(Employee::getName)
```

Because:

```text
Employee → One Name
```

---

### Q4. Employee ke all skills extract karne hain?

```java
flatMap(emp -> emp.getSkills().stream())
```

Because:

```text
Employee → Multiple Skills
```

---

### Q5. `flatMap()` intermediate hai ya terminal?

**Answer:** Intermediate Operation.

---

### Q6. Nested list ko single list me kaise convert karoge?

```java
nestedList.stream()
          .flatMap(List::stream)
          .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

### Q7. `flatMap()` ka common return flow kya hai?

```text
Stream<List<T>>
      ↓
flatMap()
      ↓
Stream<T>
```

---

# Practice Questions — Khud Solve Karo

1. Flatten `List<List<Integer>>`.
2. Flatten `List<List<String>>`.
3. Flatten nested numbers and find even numbers.
4. Flatten nested numbers and remove duplicates.
5. Flatten nested numbers, remove duplicates and sort.
6. Get all skills from employees.
7. Get unique employee skills.
8. Get unique skills sorted alphabetically.
9. Count unique skills.
10. Find how many employees collectively have `"Java"` in their skill lists.
11. Get all projects from employees.
12. Get all employees from departments.
13. Get all orders from customers.
14. Get all items from orders.
15. Get all items from customers → orders → items.
16. Split multiple sentences into individual words.
17. Find unique words from multiple sentences.
18. Find word frequency across multiple sentences.
19. Find all unique product categories across nested orders.
20. Find total price of all items from all orders.

---

# 🔥 Question 20 Ko Decode Karo

> Find total price of all items from all orders.

Direct code mat likho.

Requirement:

```text
Orders
   ↓
Each Order has Items
   ↓
flatMap()
   ↓
Items
   ↓
Price chahiye
   ↓
map()
   ↓
Total chahiye
   ↓
reduce()
```

Code:

```java
double total =
        orders.stream()
              .flatMap(order ->
                      order.getItems().stream())
              .map(Item::getPrice)
              .reduce(0.0, Double::sum);
```

Dekha?

Tumne alag-alag modules ratte nahi.

Tumne requirement ko patterns me toda:

```text
Nested   → flatMap()
Convert  → map()
Combine  → reduce()
```

---

# 🎯 MODULE 1–11 Master Logic Map

Ab tumhare paas ye mental dictionary honi chahiye:

| Requirement        | Java 8 Pattern     |
| ------------------ | ------------------ |
| Select / Condition | `filter()`         |
| Transform          | `map()`            |
| Nested → Flat      | `flatMap()`        |
| Arrange            | `sorted()`         |
| Remove Duplicate   | `distinct()`       |
| Highest / Lowest   | `max()/min()`      |
| How Many           | `count()`          |
| Multiple Groups    | `groupingBy()`     |
| Two Groups         | `partitioningBy()` |
| Many → One         | `reduce()`         |

## Final Memory Trick

```text
FILTER      → Chuno
MAP         → Badlo
FLATMAP     → Nested kholo
SORTED      → Arrange karo
DISTINCT    → Duplicate hatao
MAX/MIN     → Highest/Lowest
COUNT       → Gino
GROUPING    → Groups banao
PARTITION   → 2 groups banao
REDUCE      → Sabko combine karo
```

`flatMap()` ke liye bas ek line strongly yaad rakho:

> **"Ek object ke andar multiple objects hain aur mujhe sab individually chahiye → flatMap()."**
=====================================================================================================
Nahi — yahi important confusion hai. `map()` aur `filter()` **sirf Stream ke methods nahi hain**.

`Stream` aur `Optional` **dono ke paas apna-apna `map()` aur `filter()` method hai**.

Aapke example me:

```java
Optional<String> optional = Optional.of("java");

Optional<String> result =
        optional.map(String::toUpperCase);
```

Yahan `map()` **Stream wala map nahi hai**. Ye `Optional.map()` hai.

### Stream me

```java
list.stream()
    .map(String::toUpperCase)
    .filter(s -> s.length() > 3);
```

Yahan:

```text
map()    → Stream ka intermediate operation
filter() → Stream ka intermediate operation
```

### Optional me

```java
Optional<String> optional = Optional.of("java");

optional.map(String::toUpperCase);
optional.filter(s -> s.length() > 3);
```

Yahan:

```text
map()    → Optional class ka method
filter() → Optional class ka method
```

Isko aise yaad rakho:

```text
Stream
 ├── map()
 ├── filter()
 └── flatMap()

Optional
 ├── map()
 ├── filter()
 └── flatMap()
```

**Same method names hain, lekin classes alag hain aur purpose bhi thoda alag hai.**

Aapka ye statement:

> "`map()` intermediate operation hai"

**Stream ke context me correct hai.**

Lekin:

```java
optional.map(...)
```

ko hum **Stream intermediate operation nahi bolenge**, kyunki yahan koi Stream hai hi nahi.

Aur ek important difference:

```java
Stream<String> stream
        ↓ map()
Stream<String>
```

whereas:

```java
Optional<String> optional
        ↓ map()
Optional<String>
```

So **kis object par method call ho raha hai, pehle wo dekho**:

```java
list.stream().map(...)  // Stream.map()

optional.map(...)       // Optional.map()
```

Ye distinction interview ke liye bhi important hai.
====================================================================================================================
# MODULE 12 : Optional Pattern — Java 8

Ab tak humne kai jagah likha:

```java
employees.stream()
         .max(Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary))
         .orElse(null);
```

Aur:

```java
list.stream()
    .findFirst()
    .orElse(null);
```

Ye jo **`Optional`** aa raha tha, ab usko properly samjhenge.

> ⭐ **Optional ka main purpose: value present bhi ho sakti hai aur absent bhi. Null handling ko safer aur readable banana.**

---

# 1. Optional Ki Need Kyu Hai?

Traditional Java:

```java
Employee emp = findEmployee();

System.out.println(emp.getName());
```

Agar:

```java
emp == null
```

to:

```text
NullPointerException
```

mil sakta hai.

Traditional handling:

```java
Employee emp = findEmployee();

if (emp != null) {
    System.out.println(emp.getName());
}
```

Java 8 ne `Optional<T>` introduce kiya.

Concept:

```text
Optional<Employee>
       ↓
Value ho sakti hai
       OR
Value nahi ho sakti
```

---

# 2. Optional Kya Hai?

`Optional<T>` ek **container/wrapper** hai jo maximum ek value hold karta hai.

```text
Optional
   ↓
┌───────────────┐
│ Employee      │  ← Value Present
└───────────────┘
```

ya:

```text
Optional
   ↓
┌───────────────┐
│     Empty     │  ← Value Absent
└───────────────┘
```

---

# 3. Optional Kaha Milta Hai?

Package:

```java
java.util.Optional
```

Import:

```java
import java.util.Optional;
```

---

# 4. Optional Create Karne Ke 3 Important Methods

```java
Optional.of()
Optional.ofNullable()
Optional.empty()
```

Ye tino bahut important hain.

---

# 5. `Optional.of()`

Jab tum sure ho ki value **null nahi hai**.

```java
String name = "Vishnu";

Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of(name);
```

Result:

```text
Optional[Vishnu]
```

### Lekin:

```java
String name = null;

Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of(name);
```

Result:

```text
NullPointerException
```

### Memory

```text
of()
 ↓
Value MUST be non-null
```

---

# 6. `Optional.ofNullable()`

Value null bhi ho sakti hai.

```java
String name = "Vishnu";

Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.ofNullable(name);
```

Result:

```text
Optional[Vishnu]
```

Agar:

```java
String name = null;
```

then:

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.ofNullable(name);
```

Result:

```text
Optional.empty
```

Exception nahi.

### Memory Trick

```text
ofNullable()
     ↓
Null allowed
```

---

# 7. `Optional.empty()`

Manually empty Optional banana ho:

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.empty();
```

Result:

```text
Optional.empty
```

---

# `of()` vs `ofNullable()` vs `empty()`

| Method              | Null Allowed? | Purpose                  |
| ------------------- | ------------: | ------------------------ |
| `of(value)`         |             ❌ | Value definitely present |
| `ofNullable(value)` |             ✅ | Value may be null        |
| `empty()`           |             — | No value                 |

---

# 8. `isPresent()`

Check karta hai Optional ke andar value hai ya nahi.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("Java");

if (optional.isPresent()) {
    System.out.println(optional.get());
}
```

Output:

```text
Java
```

Concept:

```text
Optional
   ↓
Value present?
   ↓
true / false
```

---

# 9. `get()`

Optional ke andar ki value retrieve karta hai.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("Java");

String value = optional.get();
```

Output:

```text
Java
```

⚠️ Lekin blindly `get()` use mat karo.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.empty();

optional.get();
```

Exception:

```text
NoSuchElementException
```

---

# 10. `orElse()`

Agar value hai:

```text
value return karo
```

Agar value nahi:

```text
default value return karo
```

Example:

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.ofNullable(null);

String result =
        optional.orElse("Default");

System.out.println(result);
```

Output:

```text
Default
```

### Pattern

```text
Optional
    ↓
Value present?
   /       \
 Yes        No
 ↓           ↓
Value      Default
```

---

# 11. `orElse()` With Employee

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .filter(e -> e.getId() == 101)
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

Meaning:

```text
Employee mila?
     ↓
YES → Employee

NO  → null
```

Ye pattern tum already previous modules me use kar rahe the.

---

# 12. `orElseGet()`

Default value dynamically create karni ho:

```java
String result =
optional.orElseGet(() -> "Default");
```

Employee:

```java
Employee emp =
optional.orElseGet(() -> new Employee());
```

---

# 13. `orElse()` vs `orElseGet()`

Basic difference:

```java
optional.orElse(defaultValue);
```

vs

```java
optional.orElseGet(() -> defaultValue);
```

`orElseGet()` **Supplier** leta hai.

```text
Supplier
   ↓
No Input
   ↓
Output
```

Example:

```java
() -> new Employee()
```

---

# 14. Important Difference — Eager vs Lazy

Suppose:

```java
public static String getDefaultName() {
    System.out.println("Method called");
    return "Default";
}
```

Now:

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("Java");
```

### `orElse()`

```java
String result =
        optional.orElse(getDefaultName());
```

`getDefaultName()` evaluate ho sakta hai even though Optional me `"Java"` already hai.

### `orElseGet()`

```java
String result =
        optional.orElseGet(() -> getDefaultName());
```

Supplier tabhi execute hota hai jab Optional empty ho.

### Interview Memory

```text
orElse()
   ↓
Default value eager evaluation


orElseGet()
   ↓
Default supplier lazy evaluation
```

Agar default create karna expensive hai, `orElseGet()` useful hai.

---

# 15. `orElseThrow()`

Value nahi mili to exception throw karo.

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .filter(e -> e.getId() == 101)
         .findFirst()
         .orElseThrow(
             () -> new RuntimeException(
                 "Employee not found"
             )
         );
```

Flow:

```text
Employee mila?
     ↓
 YES           NO
 ↓              ↓
Employee      Exception
```

---

# 16. Real Spring Boot Pattern

Service layer me common pattern:

```java
Employee employee =
employeeRepository.findById(id)
        .orElseThrow(() ->
                new RuntimeException(
                        "Employee not found"
                ));
```

Repository:

```java
Optional<Employee> findById(Long id);
```

Isliye Spring Data JPA me `Optional` bahut common milega.

---

# 17. `ifPresent()`

Agar value present hai tabhi action perform karo.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("Java");

optional.ifPresent(
        System.out::println
);
```

Output:

```text
Java
```

Agar Optional empty hai:

```java
Optional.empty()
```

to kuch execute nahi hoga.

---

# 18. Traditional vs `ifPresent()`

Traditional:

```java
if (name != null) {
    System.out.println(name);
}
```

Optional:

```java
optional.ifPresent(
        System.out::println
);
```

---

# 19. Optional + `filter()`

Optional ke andar ki value par condition laga sakte ho.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("Java");

optional
    .filter(name -> name.length() > 3)
    .ifPresent(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
Java
```

Agar:

```java
.filter(name -> name.length() > 10)
```

condition false hui to:

```text
Optional.empty
```

---

# 20. Optional `filter()` Ko Samjho

```text
Optional[Java]
      ↓
filter(length > 3)
      ↓
true
      ↓
Optional[Java]
```

Agar false:

```text
Optional[Java]
      ↓
filter(length > 10)
      ↓
false
      ↓
Optional.empty
```

---

# 21. Optional + `map()`

Optional ke andar ki value transform karni ho.

```java
Optional<String> optional =
        Optional.of("java");

Optional<String> result =
        optional.map(String::toUpperCase);
```

Result:

```text
Optional[JAVA]
```

Concept:

```text
Optional[java]
      ↓
map()
      ↓
Optional[JAVA]
```

---

# 22. Employee Name Using Optional Map

Suppose:

```java
Optional<Employee> employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .findFirst();
```

Sirf name chahiye:

```java
Optional<String> name =
        employee.map(Employee::getName);
```

Flow:

```text
Optional<Employee>
       ↓
map(getName)
       ↓
Optional<String>
```

---

# 23. Employee Name With Default

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .findFirst()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .orElse("Employee Not Found");
```

Ye bahut clean pattern hai.

```text
findFirst()
    ↓
Optional<Employee>
    ↓
map(Name)
    ↓
Optional<String>
    ↓
orElse()
    ↓
String
```

---

# 24. `findFirst()` + Optional

Input:

```java
List<Integer> list =
        Arrays.asList(10,20,30,40);
```

Code:

```java
Optional<Integer> result =
        list.stream()
            .findFirst();
```

Output:

```text
Optional[10]
```

Agar list:

```java
Collections.emptyList()
```

to:

```text
Optional.empty
```

Isi wajah se `findFirst()` Optional return karta hai.

---

# 25. `max()` + Optional

Tumne Module 6 me padha:

```java
Optional<Integer> max =
        list.stream()
            .max(Integer::compare);
```

Kyun Optional?

Because:

```text
List empty ho sakti hai.
```

Empty list:

```java
List<Integer> list =
        Collections.emptyList();
```

Maximum kya hoga?

Kuch nahi.

Isliye:

```text
Optional.empty
```

---

# 26. `min()` + Optional

Same concept:

```java
Optional<Integer> min =
        list.stream()
            .min(Integer::compare);
```

---

# 27. `reduce()` + Optional

Module 10:

```java
Optional<Integer> result =
        list.stream()
            .reduce(Integer::sum);
```

Identity nahi di hai.

Agar stream empty hai to result nahi milega.

Isliye Optional.

---

# 28. Optional Kab-Kab Dikhega?

Tumhare current Java 8 modules me especially:

```text
findFirst()
     ↓
Optional


findAny()
     ↓
Optional


max()
     ↓
Optional


min()
     ↓
Optional


reduce() without identity
     ↓
Optional
```

Ye connection yaad rakho.

---

# 29. `findAny()`

```java
Optional<Employee> emp =
employees.stream()
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .findAny();
```

Koi ek matching Employee mil sakta hai.

---

# 30. `get()` vs `orElse()`

Bad/unsafe style:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .findFirst()
         .get();
```

Agar empty:

```text
NoSuchElementException
```

Better:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

Aur business requirement me often better:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .findFirst()
         .orElseThrow(() ->
             new RuntimeException(
                 "Employee not found"
             ));
```

---

# 31. `map()` + `orElse()`

Question:

> Find first IT employee name. Agar employee nahi mila to `"Not Found"` return karo.

Requirement todho:

```text
IT employee
    ↓
filter()

First
    ↓
findFirst()

Name
    ↓
map()

Absent?
    ↓
orElse()
```

Code:

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .findFirst()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .orElse("Not Found");
```

🔥 Ye real interview aur project dono me useful pattern hai.

---

# 32. Highest Paid Employee Name

Question:

> Highest salary employee ka name find karo. Employee nahi hai to `"No Employee"`.

Logic:

```text
Employee List
      ↓
max(Salary)
      ↓
Optional<Employee>
      ↓
map(Name)
      ↓
Optional<String>
      ↓
orElse()
```

Code:

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .max(Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .orElse("No Employee");
```

---

# 33. Lowest Paid Active Employee

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .min(Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .orElse(null);
```

Pattern:

```text
filter
  ↓
min
  ↓
Optional
  ↓
orElse
```

---

# 34. Optional Chaining

Optional ka powerful part ye hai ki baar-baar `null` check ki jagah operations chain kar sakte ho.

```java
String result =
Optional.ofNullable(name)
        .filter(n -> !n.isEmpty())
        .map(String::trim)
        .map(String::toUpperCase)
        .orElse("UNKNOWN");
```

Flow:

```text
Nullable Value
      ↓
ofNullable
      ↓
filter
      ↓
trim
      ↓
uppercase
      ↓
orElse
```

---

# 35. Nested Null Problem

Suppose:

```java
Employee employee = getEmployee();

String city =
employee.getAddress()
        .getCity();
```

Problem:

```text
employee null?

OR

address null?
```

NPE ho sakta hai.

Optional style:

```java
String city =
Optional.ofNullable(employee)
        .map(Employee::getAddress)
        .map(Address::getCity)
        .orElse("Unknown");
```

Flow:

```text
Employee
   ↓
Employee exists?
   ↓
Address
   ↓
Address exists?
   ↓
City
   ↓
orElse("Unknown")
```

---

# 36. Optional `map()` vs Stream `map()`

Naam same hai but context different hai.

### Stream map

```java
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
```

Works on:

```text
Many Employees
```

### Optional map

```java
optionalEmployee
        .map(Employee::getName)
```

Works on:

```text
Zero or One Employee
```

Memory:

```text
Stream<T>
   ↓
Many values


Optional<T>
   ↓
Zero or One value
```

---

# 37. Optional `flatMap()`

Ye thoda advanced hai.

Suppose method:

```java
Optional<Address> getAddress()
```

Agar tum:

```java
optionalEmployee.map(Employee::getAddress)
```

karoge to nested result ban sakta hai:

```text
Optional<Optional<Address>>
```

Us nested Optional ko flatten karne ke liye:

```java
optionalEmployee.flatMap(Employee::getAddress)
```

Result:

```text
Optional<Address>
```

Same concept jo Stream `flatMap()` me tha:

```text
Nested
  ↓
Flatten
```

---

# 38. Stream flatMap vs Optional flatMap

Stream:

```text
Stream<Stream<T>>
       ↓
flatMap
       ↓
Stream<T>
```

Optional:

```text
Optional<Optional<T>>
        ↓
flatMap
        ↓
Optional<T>
```

Core idea same:

> **Nested wrapper ko flatten karo.**

---

# 39. Optional Important Methods

| Method          | Purpose                    |
| --------------- | -------------------------- |
| `of()`          | Non-null value se Optional |
| `ofNullable()`  | Nullable value se Optional |
| `empty()`       | Empty Optional             |
| `isPresent()`   | Value hai?                 |
| `get()`         | Value nikalo               |
| `ifPresent()`   | Value ho to action         |
| `orElse()`      | Default value              |
| `orElseGet()`   | Lazy default               |
| `orElseThrow()` | Missing ho to exception    |
| `filter()`      | Condition                  |
| `map()`         | Transform                  |
| `flatMap()`     | Nested Optional flatten    |

---

# 40. Golden Rule

Agar tum dekho:

```text
Value may or may not exist
```

Think:

```java
Optional<T>
```

Agar dekho:

```text
Value nahi mili to default
```

Think:

```java
orElse()
```

Agar:

```text
Value nahi mili to object lazily create karo
```

Think:

```java
orElseGet()
```

Agar:

```text
Value nahi mili to exception
```

Think:

```java
orElseThrow()
```

Agar:

```text
Value ho to kuch karo
```

Think:

```java
ifPresent()
```

Agar:

```text
Optional ke andar value transform karo
```

Think:

```java
map()
```

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. Optional kya hai?

**Hindi-English Answer:**

`Optional` Java 8 me introduced ek container class hai jo value ko represent karta hai jo present bhi ho sakti hai aur absent bhi. Iska main purpose null handling ko explicit aur safer banana hai.

---

### Q2. Optional kis package me hai?

```java
java.util.Optional
```

---

### Q3. `Optional.of()` aur `ofNullable()` me difference?

```text
of()
↓
null allowed nahi


ofNullable()
↓
null allowed
```

`Optional.of(null)` → `NullPointerException`

`Optional.ofNullable(null)` → `Optional.empty()`

---

### Q4. `get()` safe hai?

Blindly use karna safe nahi.

Empty Optional par:

```java
optional.get();
```

`NoSuchElementException` aa sakta hai.

---

### Q5. `orElse()` kya karta hai?

Value present ho to wahi return karta hai, otherwise default value return karta hai.

```java
optional.orElse("Default")
```

---

### Q6. `orElse()` vs `orElseGet()`?

**Answer:**

`orElse()` direct default value leta hai, jabki `orElseGet()` `Supplier` leta hai aur default value tab lazily generate karta hai jab Optional empty ho.

---

### Q7. `orElseThrow()`?

Optional empty ho to specified exception throw karta hai.

```java
.orElseThrow(() ->
    new RuntimeException("Not Found"))
```

---

### Q8. `findFirst()` Optional kyu return karta hai?

Because stream empty ho sakti hai. Agar element nahi mila to null ke bajay `Optional.empty()` represent kiya ja sakta hai.

---

### Q9. `max()` aur `min()` Optional kyu return karte hain?

Because empty stream ka maximum/minimum exist nahi karta.

---

### Q10. Optional `map()` kya karta hai?

Agar value present hai to usko transform karta hai. Agar Optional empty hai to result bhi empty rehta hai.

---

# 🧠 Logic Building Practice

Question:

> Find active IT employee with highest salary and return his name. Agar employee nahi hai to `"Not Found"`.

Direct code nahi.

Requirement todho:

```text
Employee List
      ↓
Active
      ↓
filter()

IT
      ↓
filter()

Highest Salary
      ↓
max()

Ab kya mila?
      ↓
Optional<Employee>

Name chahiye
      ↓
map()

Value nahi?
      ↓
orElse()
```

Code:

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .max(Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .orElse("Not Found");
```

Ye ratne ki zarurat nahi.

Requirement ne khud methods bataye:

```text
Active       → filter
IT           → filter
Highest      → max
Name         → map
May not exist → Optional
Default      → orElse
```

---

# Practice Questions — Khud Solve Karo

1. Create Optional using `of()`.
2. Create Optional using `ofNullable()`.
3. Create empty Optional.
4. Check value using `isPresent()`.
5. Print value using `ifPresent()`.
6. Return default using `orElse()`.
7. Return lazy default using `orElseGet()`.
8. Throw exception using `orElseThrow()`.
9. Find first employee safely.
10. Find first IT employee safely.
11. Find first IT employee name or `"Not Found"`.
12. Find highest salary employee safely.
13. Find highest salary employee name.
14. Find youngest employee or default.
15. Find active employee with minimum salary.
16. Convert Optional String to uppercase.
17. Filter Optional String based on length.
18. Get Employee → Address → City safely.
19. Use `flatMap()` with nested Optional.
20. Find highest-paid active IT employee name or `"Not Found"`.

---

# ⭐ Modules 1–12 Master Logic Map

| Requirement         | Pattern            |
| ------------------- | ------------------ |
| Select              | `filter()`         |
| Transform           | `map()`            |
| Nested → Flat       | `flatMap()`        |
| Arrange             | `sorted()`         |
| Remove Duplicate    | `distinct()`       |
| Highest / Lowest    | `max()/min()`      |
| How Many            | `count()`          |
| Multiple Groups     | `groupingBy()`     |
| Two Groups          | `partitioningBy()` |
| Many → One          | `reduce()`         |
| Value may be absent | `Optional`         |

## Final Optional Memory Trick

```text
Optional
   ↓
"Value milegi ya nahi pata nahi"


Value hai?
→ isPresent()


Value ho to action?
→ ifPresent()


Default chahiye?
→ orElse()


Lazy default?
→ orElseGet()


Nahi mili to error?
→ orElseThrow()


Value change?
→ map()


Nested Optional?
→ flatMap()
```

### Ek line strongly yaad rakho:

> **`Stream` = 0 to many values handle karta hai; `Optional` = 0 or 1 value handle karta hai.**

Ye concept clear ho gaya to `findFirst()`, `max()`, `min()`, `reduce()` aur Spring Data JPA ke `findById()` ko samajhna bahut easy ho jayega.
=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 13 : Collectors Pattern — Java 8 Stream API

Ab tak tum `collect()` ke andar bahut saare Collectors already use kar chuke ho:

```java
Collectors.toList()
Collectors.toSet()
Collectors.groupingBy()
Collectors.partitioningBy()
Collectors.counting()
Collectors.mapping()
Collectors.averagingDouble()
Collectors.summingDouble()
Collectors.joining()
```

Ab in sabko **ek proper pattern** ki tarah samjhte hain.

> ⭐ **Collector ka simple meaning: Stream ke final data ko collect/aggregate karke List, Set, Map, String, count, sum, average etc. result banana.**

---

# 1. `collect()` Kya Hai?

Suppose:

```java
List<Integer> list =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6);
```

Even numbers ki List chahiye:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list.stream()
            .filter(i -> i % 2 == 0)
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[2, 4, 6]
```

Flow:

```text
List
 ↓
stream()
 ↓
filter()
 ↓
2 4 6
 ↓
collect()
 ↓
List<Integer>
```

---

# 2. `collect()` vs `Collectors`

Ye confusion clear rakho.

### `collect()`

Stream ka **terminal operation** hai.

```java
stream.collect(...)
```

### `Collectors`

Ek utility class hai jo ready-made collectors provide karti hai.

```java
Collectors.toList()
Collectors.toSet()
Collectors.groupingBy()
```

### Memory

```text
collect()
   ↓
"Collect karo"

Collectors
   ↓
"Kaise collect karna hai?"
```

Example:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toList())
```

Meaning:

```text
collect
  ↓
Data collect karo

toList
  ↓
List ke form me
```

---

# 3. Collectors Package

```java
java.util.stream.Collectors
```

Import:

```java
import java.util.stream.Collectors;
```

---

# 4. `Collectors.toList()`

Stream ko List me collect karta hai.

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list.stream()
            .filter(i -> i > 10)
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Return:

```java
List<Integer>
```

---

# 5. Employee Names To List

Question:

> Employee objects me se names ki List banao.

Requirement:

```text
Employee
   ↓
Name chahiye
   ↓
map()

List chahiye
   ↓
toList()
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 6. Active Employee Names

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Pattern:

```text
Select     → filter
Transform  → map
Store      → collect(toList)
```

---

# 7. `Collectors.toSet()`

Duplicates nahi chahiye aur Set result chahiye:

```java
Set<String> departments =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .collect(Collectors.toSet());
```

Output concept:

```text
[IT, HR, Sales]
```

---

# 8. `distinct()` vs `toSet()`

Dono duplicates remove karne me help kar sakte hain, but purpose different hai.

### `distinct()`

Stream operation:

```java
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getDepartment)
         .distinct()
```

Result abhi bhi:

```text
Stream<String>
```

### `toSet()`

Terminal collection:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toSet())
```

Result:

```text
Set<String>
```

### Memory

```text
distinct()
→ Stream me duplicate remove


toSet()
→ Final result Set banana
```

---

# 9. `Collectors.joining()`

Strings ko combine karke ek String banana.

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList("Vishnu", "Rahul", "Amit");

String result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));

System.out.println(result);
```

Output:

```text
Vishnu, Rahul, Amit
```

---

# 10. `joining()` Ke 3 Forms

### Without delimiter

```java
Collectors.joining()
```

Output:

```text
JavaSpringBoot
```

### With delimiter

```java
Collectors.joining(", ")
```

Output:

```text
Java, Spring, Boot
```

### Delimiter + Prefix + Suffix

```java
Collectors.joining(", ", "[", "]")
```

Output:

```text
[Java, Spring, Boot]
```

Easy:

```text
joining(delimiter, prefix, suffix)
```

---

# 11. Employee Names As Single String

Question:

> All employee names comma-separated chahiye.

```java
String names =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
```

Flow:

```text
Employee
   ↓
map(Name)
   ↓
String Stream
   ↓
joining()
   ↓
Single String
```

---

# 12. `Collectors.counting()`

Elements count karta hai.

```java
Long count =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.counting());
```

But simple total count ke liye:

```java
long count = employees.stream().count();
```

zyada straightforward hai.

`Collectors.counting()` ki real power downstream collector ke form me dikhti hai.

---

# 13. Department-wise Count

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

Output:

```text
IT    → 5
HR    → 3
Sales → 4
```

Pattern:

```text
groupingBy()
    +
counting()
```

---

# 14. `Collectors.summingInt()`

Integer values ka sum.

Suppose:

```java
class Product {
    String name;
    int quantity;
}
```

Total quantity:

```java
int total =
        products.stream()
                .collect(Collectors.summingInt(
                        Product::getQuantity
                ));
```

---

# 15. `summingDouble()`

Employee total salary:

```java
double totalSalary =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.summingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee
    ↓
getSalary
    ↓
summingDouble
    ↓
Total Salary
```

---

# 16. Department-wise Total Salary

Ab collectors combine:

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.summingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

Output concept:

```text
IT    → 450000
HR    → 230000
Sales → 310000
```

---

# 17. `Collectors.averagingInt()`

Example Student:

```java
class Student {
    String name;
    int marks;
}
```

Average marks:

```java
double average =
        students.stream()
                .collect(Collectors.averagingInt(
                        Student::getMarks
                ));
```

Return:

```java
Double
```

---

# 18. `averagingDouble()`

Employee average salary:

```java
double average =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.averagingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

---

# 19. Department-wise Average Salary

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.averagingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

Pattern:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy

Average Salary
      ↓
averagingDouble
```

---

# 20. `Collectors.maxBy()`

Highest salary Employee:

```java
Optional<Employee> employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.maxBy(
                         Comparator.comparing(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

Normally simple max ke liye:

```java
employees.stream()
         .max(Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary));
```

easier hai.

But `maxBy()` downstream grouping me powerful hai.

---

# 21. Department-wise Highest Salary

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                                 Comparator.comparing(
                                         Employee::getSalary
                                 )
                         )
                 ));
```

Pattern:

```text
groupingBy
   +
maxBy
```

---

# 22. `Collectors.minBy()`

```java
Optional<Employee> employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.minBy(
                         Comparator.comparing(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 23. Department-wise Lowest Salary

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.minBy(
                                 Comparator.comparing(
                                         Employee::getSalary
                                 )
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 24. `Collectors.mapping()`

Ye collector initially confusing lag sakta hai.

Suppose:

> Department-wise complete Employee objects nahi, **sirf names** chahiye.

Without mapping:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>>
```

But requirement:

```java
Map<String, List<String>>
```

Use:

```java
Collectors.mapping()
```

Code:

```java
Map<String, List<String>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getName,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 25. `mapping()` Ko Samjho

```java
Collectors.mapping(
        Employee::getName,
        Collectors.toList()
)
```

Meaning:

```text
Employee
   ↓
getName
   ↓
String
   ↓
toList
```

And outer:

```text
Department
    ↓
groupingBy
```

Complete:

```text
Employee
    ↓
Group by Department
    ↓
Each Employee → Name
    ↓
Names → List
```

Result:

```text
IT    → [Vishnu, Rahul]
HR    → [Amit, Sneha]
Sales → [Raj]
```

---

# 26. `Collectors.toMap()`

🔥 Important.

Stream elements ko Map me convert karta hai.

Suppose:

```text
Employee ID → Employee Name
```

Code:

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getName
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
101 → Vishnu
102 → Rahul
103 → Amit
```

---

# 27. `toMap()` Ka Input Kya Hai?

Basic form:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
        keyMapper,
        valueMapper
)
```

### First Function

```java
Employee::getId
```

Jo return karega wo:

```text
KEY
```

### Second Function

```java
Employee::getName
```

Jo return karega wo:

```text
VALUE
```

### Memory Trick

```java
toMap(KEY, VALUE)
```

Example:

```java
toMap(
    Employee::getId,       // KEY
    Employee::getName      // VALUE
)
```

---

# 28. Employee ID → Employee Object

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Function.identity()
                 ));
```

`Function.identity()` ka meaning:

```text
Jo Employee input mila
wahi Employee return karo
```

So:

```text
ID → Employee
```

---

# 29. `toMap()` Duplicate Key Problem

Bahut important interview point.

Suppose:

```java
Map<String, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Function.identity()
                 ));
```

Problem:

Multiple employees same department me hain:

```text
Vishnu → IT
Rahul  → IT
```

Key dono ki:

```text
IT
```

Duplicate key aa sakti hai aur `IllegalStateException` ho sakta hai.

---

# 30. Duplicate Key Handle Karna

`toMap()` ka third argument:

```java
mergeFunction
```

Example:

```java
Map<String, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Function.identity(),
                         (oldValue, newValue) -> oldValue
                 ));
```

Meaning:

```text
Duplicate key?
    ↓
Old employee rakho
```

Ya:

```java
(oldValue, newValue) -> newValue
```

Meaning:

```text
Latest/new value rakho
```

---

# 31. `toMap()` Pattern

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    keyMapper,
    valueMapper,
    mergeFunction
)
```

Memory:

```text
1st → Key kya hai?

2nd → Value kya hai?

3rd → Same key aaye to kya karna hai?
```

---

# 32. `groupingBy()` vs `toMap()`

Ye bahut important difference hai.

Suppose:

```text
Department → Employees
```

Ek department ke multiple employees hain.

Use:

```java
groupingBy()
```

Result:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>>
```

Suppose:

```text
Employee ID → Employee
```

ID unique hai.

Use:

```java
toMap()
```

Result:

```java
Map<Integer, Employee>
```

### Memory

```text
One Key → Multiple Values
        ↓
groupingBy()


One Key → One Value
        ↓
toMap()
```

---

# 33. `Collectors.partitioningBy()`

Module 9 me padha:

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         Employee::isActive
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → Active
false → Inactive
```

Ye bhi `Collectors` class ka hi method hai.

---

# 34. `Collectors.groupingBy()`

Module 8:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment
                 ));
```

Again, `groupingBy()` bhi Collector hai.

---

# 35. `Collectors.collectingAndThen()`

Ye advanced but interview-useful collector hai.

Meaning:

> Pehle collect karo, phir final result par ek aur operation perform karo.

Syntax:

```java
Collectors.collectingAndThen(
        collector,
        finisher
)
```

---

# 36. Example: Collect Then Make Unmodifiable

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.collectingAndThen(
                         Collectors.toList(),
                         Collections::unmodifiableList
                 ));
```

Flow:

```text
Employee Names
      ↓
toList()
      ↓
List
      ↓
unmodifiableList()
      ↓
Final List
```

---

# 37. Department-wise Highest Salary Without Optional

Earlier:

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>>
```

mil raha tha.

Question:

> Mujhe `Optional<Employee>` nahi chahiye. Direct Employee chahiye.

Use:

```java
Map<String, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.collectingAndThen(
                                 Collectors.maxBy(
                                         Comparator.comparing(
                                                 Employee::getSalary
                                         )
                                 ),
                                 Optional::get
                         )
                 ));
```

Result:

```java
Map<String, Employee>
```

⚠️ `Optional::get` tab safe hai jab har created group non-empty ho, jo normal `groupingBy()` output groups ke case me hota hai.

---

# 38. `summarizingInt()`

🔥 Powerful Collector.

Suppose student marks ke liye ek saath chahiye:

* Count
* Sum
* Min
* Average
* Max

Instead of separate calculations:

```java
IntSummaryStatistics stats =
        students.stream()
                .collect(Collectors.summarizingInt(
                        Student::getMarks
                ));
```

Then:

```java
stats.getCount();
stats.getSum();
stats.getMin();
stats.getAverage();
stats.getMax();
```

---

# 39. Employee Salary Statistics

For `double` salary:

```java
DoubleSummaryStatistics stats =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.summarizingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

Now:

```java
stats.getCount();
stats.getSum();
stats.getMin();
stats.getAverage();
stats.getMax();
```

### One collector:

```text
           summarizingDouble
                  ↓
       ┌──────────┼──────────┐
       ↓          ↓          ↓
     Count       Sum        Min
       ↓          ↓          ↓
    Average                 Max
```

---

# 40. Department-wise Salary Statistics

Advanced:

```java
Map<String, DoubleSummaryStatistics> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.summarizingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

Now each department ke liye:

```text
Count
Sum
Min
Average
Max
```

sab available.

---

# 41. Most Important Collectors Table

| Collector             | Kaam                      |
| --------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `toList()`            | List banana               |
| `toSet()`             | Set banana                |
| `toMap()`             | Map banana                |
| `joining()`           | Strings combine           |
| `counting()`          | Count                     |
| `summingInt()`        | Integer sum               |
| `summingLong()`       | Long sum                  |
| `summingDouble()`     | Double sum                |
| `averagingInt()`      | Integer average           |
| `averagingLong()`     | Long average              |
| `averagingDouble()`   | Double average            |
| `maxBy()`             | Maximum                   |
| `minBy()`             | Minimum                   |
| `groupingBy()`        | Multiple groups           |
| `partitioningBy()`    | True/False groups         |
| `mapping()`           | Downstream transformation |
| `collectingAndThen()` | Collect + final transform |
| `summarizingInt()`    | Statistics                |
| `summarizingDouble()` | Statistics                |

---

# 42. Collector Combination Pattern

Collectors ki real power tab aati hai jab collectors combine hote hain.

### Group + Count

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    counting()
)
```

### Group + Sum

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    summingDouble(Employee::getSalary)
)
```

### Group + Average

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    averagingDouble(Employee::getSalary)
)
```

### Group + Max

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    maxBy(Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary))
)
```

### Group + Mapping

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    mapping(Employee::getName, toList())
)
```

---

# 43. Logic Building — Collector Kaise Choose Kare?

Question aaya:

> **"Get all active IT employee names as a sorted List."**

Requirement todo:

```text
Active
  ↓
filter

IT
  ↓
filter

Name
  ↓
map

Sorted
  ↓
sorted

Final List
  ↓
Collectors.toList
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .sorted()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 44. Scenario: Unique Departments as Set

Question:

> Get all departments as Set.

Think:

```text
Employee
   ↓
Department
   ↓
map()

Set
 ↓
toSet()
```

```java
Set<String> departments =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .collect(Collectors.toSet());
```

---

# 45. Scenario: Employee Names Comma-Separated

Think:

```text
Employee
   ↓
Name
   ↓
map()

Single String
   ↓
joining()
```

```java
String result =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
```

---

# 46. Scenario: Department → Count

Think:

```text
Department-wise
      ↓
groupingBy

How many
      ↓
counting
```

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 47. Scenario: Department → Names

Think:

```text
Department-wise
      ↓
groupingBy

Complete Employee nahi
Name chahiye
      ↓
mapping

Multiple Names
      ↓
toList
```

```java
Map<String, List<String>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getName,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 48. Scenario: ID → Name

Question:

> Employee ID ko key aur name ko value bana ke Map create karo.

Immediately:

```text
Key → ID
Value → Name
One-to-One
     ↓
toMap()
```

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getName
                 ));
```

---

# 49. Scenario: Active vs Inactive Count

Think:

```text
2 groups
   ↓
partitioningBy

Count
   ↓
counting
```

```java
Map<Boolean, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         Employee::isActive,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 50. Scenario: Department Salary Full Statistics

Question:

> Department-wise salary count, total, minimum, average aur maximum sab chahiye.

Don't write five separate pipelines.

Think:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy

All Statistics
      ↓
summarizingDouble
```

```java
Map<String, DoubleSummaryStatistics> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.summarizingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 🔥 `collect()` vs `reduce()`

Module 10 ke saath difference clear rakho.

### `reduce()`

Generally:

```text
Many values
    ↓
Single value
```

Example:

```java
int sum =
        list.stream()
            .reduce(0, Integer::sum);
```

### `collect()`

Generally mutable/result containers ya structured aggregation:

```text
Stream
 ↓
List
Set
Map
Grouped Map
Statistics
String
```

Example:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

### Memory

```text
reduce()
→ values combine karo

collect()
→ result structure/aggregation banao
```

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. `collect()` kya hai?

`collect()` Stream API ka terminal operation hai jo stream elements ko final result/container me accumulate karta hai.

---

### Q2. `Collectors` kya hai?

`Collectors` `java.util.stream` package ki utility class hai jo predefined Collector implementations provide karti hai, jaise `toList()`, `toSet()`, `groupingBy()`, `joining()` etc.

---

### Q3. `collect()` aur `Collectors` me difference?

**Interview Answer:**

`collect()` Stream ka terminal operation hai, whereas `Collectors` utility class hai jo `collect()` ke liye predefined collectors provide karti hai.

---

### Q4. `toList()` aur `toSet()` difference?

`toList()` result ko List me collect karta hai. `toSet()` result ko Set me collect karta hai, jisme duplicate elements nahi hote.

---

### Q5. `joining()` kab use karoge?

Multiple strings ko ek single String me combine karna ho.

```java
Collectors.joining(", ")
```

---

### Q6. `toMap()` kitne important inputs leta hai?

Basic:

```java
toMap(
    keyMapper,
    valueMapper
)
```

Duplicate key handle karne ke liye:

```java
toMap(
    keyMapper,
    valueMapper,
    mergeFunction
)
```

---

### Q7. `groupingBy()` vs `toMap()`?

Simple answer:

```text
One key → Multiple values
        → groupingBy()

One key → One value
        → toMap()
```

---

### Q8. `mapping()` kyu use hota hai?

Group ke andar complete object ki jagah specific property collect karni ho.

Example:

```text
Department → Employee Names
```

---

### Q9. `summarizingDouble()` kya deta hai?

Ek saath:

```text
Count
Sum
Min
Average
Max
```

---

# 🧠 Collectors Logic Building Formula

Ab jab bhi collector ka question aaye, ye question pucho:

### 1. Final result kis form me chahiye?

```text
List?
→ toList()

Set?
→ toSet()

Map?
→ toMap()

String?
→ joining()
```

### 2. Aggregation chahiye?

```text
Count?
→ counting()

Sum?
→ summing()

Average?
→ averaging()

Max?
→ maxBy()

Min?
→ minBy()

All statistics?
→ summarizing()
```

### 3. Group chahiye?

```text
Multiple categories?
→ groupingBy()

Exactly 2 categories?
→ partitioningBy()
```

### 4. Group ke andar specific field chahiye?

```text
→ mapping()
```

---

Ab ye khud solve karo:

1. Convert even numbers to `List`.
2. Convert unique departments to `Set`.
3. Get employee names as `List`.
4. Get active employee names as `List`.
5. Join all employee names with comma.
6. Join names with `[ ]`.
7. Calculate total employee salary using `summingDouble()`.
8. Calculate average salary.
9. Find maximum salary employee using `maxBy()`.
10. Find minimum salary employee using `minBy()`.
11. Count employees department-wise.
12. Calculate total salary department-wise.
13. Calculate average salary department-wise.
14. Find highest paid employee department-wise.
15. Find lowest paid employee department-wise.
16. Create `EmployeeId → EmployeeName` Map.
17. Create `EmployeeId → Employee` Map.
18. Handle duplicate keys in `toMap()`.
19. Create `Department → Employee Names`.
20. Count active vs inactive employees.
21. Calculate complete salary statistics.
22. Calculate department-wise salary statistics.
23. Get unique skills from all employees into a Set.
24. Join unique employee skills with comma.
25. Create `Department → Count of Active Employees`.
