# ⭐ MODULE 1–13 Master Logic Map

| Requirement            | Pattern                    |
| ---------------------- | -------------------------- |
| Select                 | `filter()`                 |
| Transform              | `map()`                    |
| Nested → Flat          | `flatMap()`                |
| Arrange                | `sorted()`                 |
| Remove Duplicate       | `distinct()`               |
| Highest / Lowest       | `max()/min()`              |
| How Many               | `count()`                  |
| Multiple Groups        | `groupingBy()`             |
| Two Groups             | `partitioningBy()`         |
| Many → One             | `reduce()`                 |
| Value may not exist    | `Optional`                 |
| Final result structure | `collect()` / `Collectors` |

## ⭐ Collector Memory Map

```text
"List chahiye"
       ↓
toList()

"Set chahiye"
       ↓
toSet()

"Map chahiye"
       ↓
toMap()

"String banana hai"
       ↓
joining()

"Count chahiye"
       ↓
counting()

"Total chahiye"
       ↓
summing()

"Average chahiye"
       ↓
averaging()

"Highest chahiye"
       ↓
maxBy()

"Lowest chahiye"
       ↓
minBy()

"Category/Department wise"
       ↓
groupingBy()

"True/False groups"
       ↓
partitioningBy()

"Group ke andar sirf ek field"
       ↓
mapping()

"Count + Sum + Min + Avg + Max"
       ↓
summarizing()
```

### Sabse important line:

> **Stream operations (`filter`, `map`, `sorted`) decide karte hain data ke saath kya karna hai; `Collectors` decide karta hai final result kis form me banana hai.**
=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 14 : Comparator Pattern — Java 8

Ab Comparator Pattern ka main goal hai:

> **Objects ko kisi field ke basis par sort/compare karna — salary, age, name, department — aur multiple fields par sorting karna.**

Ye Module 4 `Sorting` ka advanced part samjho.

---

# 1. Comparator Kya Hai?

Suppose Employee:

```java
class Employee {

    private int id;
    private String name;
    private String department;
    private double salary;
    private int age;

    // getters/setters
}
```

Data:

```text
Vishnu   IT      70000   30
Rahul    HR      50000   28
Amit     IT      90000   32
Sneha    Sales   60000   27
```

Question:

> Employees ko salary ke according sort karo.

Java ko kaise pata chalega ki Employee object me **kis field ko compare karna hai?**

```text
name?
salary?
age?
department?
```

Yaha `Comparator` ka use hota hai.

---

# 2. Comparator Ka Basic Mental Model

```text
Two Objects
    ↓
Compare particular field
    ↓
Which comes first?
```

Example:

```text
Employee1 salary = 50000
Employee2 salary = 70000

        ↓ compare

50000 < 70000

        ↓

Employee1 first
```

---

# 3. Comparator Functional Interface

Package:

```java
java.util.Comparator
```

Comparator ek functional interface hai.

Important method:

```java
int compare(T o1, T o2);
```

Concept:

```text
Two Objects
    ↓
compare()
    ↓
int result
```

---

# 4. `compare()` Return Value

Ye interview me important hai.

```text
Negative → o1 pehle
Zero     → equal according to comparator
Positive → o2 pehle
```

Example:

```java
Comparator<Integer> comparator =
        (a, b) -> a.compareTo(b);
```

Suppose:

```text
a = 10
b = 20
```

Result negative.

Meaning:

```text
10 comes before 20
```

---

# 5. Basic Integer Sorting

```java
List<Integer> list =
        Arrays.asList(5, 2, 8, 1, 9);

list.stream()
    .sorted()
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
1
2
5
8
9
```

Yaha explicit Comparator ki zarurat nahi because Integer ka natural ordering already available hai.

---

# 6. Descending Integer Sorting

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
9
8
5
2
1
```

---

# 7. String Ascending

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList("Vishnu", "Amit", "Rahul", "Sneha");

names.stream()
     .sorted()
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
Amit
Rahul
Sneha
Vishnu
```

---

# 8. String Descending

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 9. Employee Salary Sorting — Traditional Lambda

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted((e1, e2) ->
                 Double.compare(
                         e1.getSalary(),
                         e2.getSalary()))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Meaning:

```text
Employee1
   ↓
salary

vs

Employee2
   ↓
salary
```

---

# 10. `Comparator.comparing()`

Java 8 me clean approach:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

### Is line ko samjho

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

Question:

**`comparing()` ko input kya diya?**

```java
Employee::getSalary
```

Ye Employee leta hai aur comparison key return karta hai:

```text
Employee
   ↓
getSalary()
   ↓
Salary
   ↓
Compare salary
```

## ⭐ Golden Rule

> `Comparator.comparing()` ke andar jo function doge, **us function se return hone wali value comparison key banegi.**

Exactly grouping ke concept jaisa:

```java
groupingBy(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Department → grouping key.

Whereas:

```java
comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

Salary → comparison key.

---

# 11. Employee Name Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee
   ↓
getName()
   ↓
Name becomes comparison key
```

---

# 12. Employee Age Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 13. Employee Department Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getDepartment))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 14. Salary Descending

Most common interview pattern:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                       .reversed())
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Memory:

```text
comparing()
    ↓
Ascending

comparing(...).reversed()
    ↓
Descending
```

---

# 15. Name Descending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName)
                       .reversed())
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 16. `comparingInt()`

Agar property primitive `int` hai:

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(Employee::getAge)
```

Example:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 17. `comparingDouble()`

Salary `double` hai:

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
```

Full:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Ye:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

se boxing avoid kar sakta hai when getter returns primitive `double`.

---

# 18. Important Comparing Methods

```java
Comparator.comparing()
Comparator.comparingInt()
Comparator.comparingLong()
Comparator.comparingDouble()
```

Memory:

| Field         | Prefer              |
| ------------- | ------------------- |
| String/Object | `comparing()`       |
| `int`         | `comparingInt()`    |
| `long`        | `comparingLong()`   |
| `double`      | `comparingDouble()` |

---

# 19. Multiple Field Sorting — `thenComparing()`

🔥 Very important.

Question:

> Employees ko department-wise sort karo. Same department me salary-wise sort karo.

Requirement:

```text
First
↓
Department

Same Department?
↓
Salary
```

Code:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 20. `thenComparing()` Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?

Suppose:

```text
Vishnu   IT   70000
Amit     IT   50000
Rahul    HR   60000
```

First:

```text
Department
```

So:

```text
HR
IT
IT
```

IT me two employees hain.

Tie:

```text
Vishnu → IT
Amit   → IT
```

Ab:

```java
thenComparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

apply hoga.

Result:

```text
HR → Rahul  60000

IT → Amit   50000
IT → Vishnu 70000
```

---

# 21. Three-Level Sorting

Question:

> Department → Age → Name

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getAge)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getName))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Pattern:

```text
Primary Sort
     ↓
Department

Tie?
 ↓
Age

Tie?
 ↓
Name
```

---

# 22. Department Ascending + Salary Descending

Very common interview scenario.

Requirement:

```text
Department
↓
Ascending

Salary
↓
Descending
```

Code:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                             Employee::getSalary)
                               .reversed()
                 )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 23. Don't Make This Mistake

Suppose requirement:

> Department ascending, salary descending.

Agar tum:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getDepartment)
          .thenComparing(Employee::getSalary)
          .reversed()
```

likhoge, to `.reversed()` **poore comparator chain ko reverse** karega.

Matlab:

```text
Department bhi reverse
Salary bhi reverse
```

But hume sirf salary descending chahiye.

Correct:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getDepartment)
          .thenComparing(
              Comparator.comparingDouble(
                  Employee::getSalary)
                  .reversed()
          )
```

🔥 Important interview trap.

---

# 24. Sort By String Length

Question:

> Strings ko length ke according sort karo.

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(
             String::length))
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Input:

```text
Java
C
Python
Go
```

Output:

```text
C
Go
Java
Python
```

---

# 25. String Length Descending

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(
             String::length)
             .reversed())
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 26. Length Same Ho To Alphabetical

Question:

> String length ascending. Same length ho to alphabetically.

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(String::length)
                   .thenComparing(
                       Comparator.naturalOrder()))
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Example:

```text
Raj
Amit
Java
Rahul
Ajay
```

Length 4:

```text
Ajay
Amit
Java
```

Alphabetically arrange honge.

---

# 27. `naturalOrder()`

Natural/default ordering:

```java
Comparator.naturalOrder()
```

Example:

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.naturalOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Numbers:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

Strings:

```text
A B C D
```

---

# 28. `reverseOrder()`

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

Example:

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 29. `reversed()` vs `reverseOrder()`

### `reverseOrder()`

Natural ordering ko reverse karta hai:

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

### `reversed()`

Existing Comparator ko reverse karta hai:

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getSalary)
          .reversed()
```

Memory:

```text
reverseOrder()
→ Natural ordering ka reverse


reversed()
→ Maine jo comparator banaya uska reverse
```

---

# 30. Comparator + `max()`

Comparator sirf `sorted()` ke liye nahi hai.

Highest salary:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .orElse(null);
```

Notice:

```text
max()
   ↓
"Compare kis basis par?"
   ↓
Comparator
```

---

# 31. Comparator + `min()`

Youngest employee:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .min(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .orElse(null);
```

---

# 32. Longest Name Using Comparator

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 String::length))
         .orElse("");
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee
   ↓
map(Name)
   ↓
Compare Length
   ↓
max()
```

---

# 33. Shortest Name

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .min(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 String::length))
         .orElse("");
```

---

# 34. Top 3 Highest Paid Employees

Question:

> Top 3 salary employees.

Think:

```text
Highest first
     ↓
Salary Descending

First 3
     ↓
limit(3)
```

Code:

```java
List<Employee> result =
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .limit(3)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 35. Second Highest Salary Employee

```java
Employee employee =
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .skip(1)
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

Pipeline:

```text
Salary Descending
       ↓
skip first
       ↓
findFirst
       ↓
Second Employee
```

⚠️ Ye **second employee after sorting** hai. Agar duplicate salaries hain aur interviewer specifically **second distinct highest salary** poochta hai, logic alag hoga.

---

# 36. Second Distinct Highest Salary

Suppose salaries:

```text
90000
90000
80000
70000
```

Second highest salary should be:

```text
80000
```

Not:

```text
90000
```

Code:

```java
Double salary =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getSalary)
         .distinct()
         .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
         .skip(1)
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

### Logic

```text
Employee
   ↓
map Salary
   ↓
distinct
   ↓
descending sort
   ↓
skip highest
   ↓
find first
```

🔥 Very common interview question.

---

# 37. Highest Salary Department Wise

Comparator + grouping combination:

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
employees.stream()
         .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
             Employee::getDepartment,
             Collectors.maxBy(
                 Comparator.comparingDouble(
                     Employee::getSalary)
             )
         ));
```

Pattern:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy()

Highest
      ↓
maxBy()

Compare what?
      ↓
Salary Comparator
```

---

# 38. Comparator With Null Values

Suppose employee name null ho sakta hai.

Direct:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getName)
```

comparison me problem create kar sakta hai.

Java provides:

```java
Comparator.nullsFirst()
Comparator.nullsLast()
```

---

# 39. `nullsFirst()`

Null values pehle:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName,
                 Comparator.nullsFirst(
                     Comparator.naturalOrder()
                 )
             )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Concept:

```text
null
null
Amit
Rahul
Vishnu
```

---

# 40. `nullsLast()`

Null values last:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName,
                 Comparator.nullsLast(
                     Comparator.naturalOrder()
                 )
             )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Concept:

```text
Amit
Rahul
Vishnu
null
null
```

---

# 41. Comparator vs Comparable

🔥 Interview me definitely important.

## Comparable

Class khud define karti hai ki uska **natural/default ordering** kya hai.

Method:

```java
compareTo()
```

Interface:

```java
Comparable<T>
```

Example concept:

```java
class Employee implements Comparable<Employee> {

    @Override
    public int compareTo(Employee other) {
        return this.id - other.id;
    }
}
```

---

## Comparator

External/custom sorting rule.

Method:

```java
compare()
```

Interface:

```java
Comparator<T>
```

Java 8:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

---

# 42. Comparable vs Comparator Table

| Comparable                | Comparator                      |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| `java.lang`               | `java.util`                     |
| `compareTo()`             | `compare()`                     |
| Natural/default sorting   | Custom sorting                  |
| Class implements it       | Separate comparator             |
| Usually one natural order | Multiple sorting rules possible |

Example:

Employee ka default ID sorting:

```text
Comparable
```

But:

```text
Salary sorting
Name sorting
Age sorting
Department sorting
```

ke liye:

```text
Comparator
```

bahut useful hai.

---

# 43. Old Style vs Java 8 Comparator

Old style:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        new Comparator<Employee>() {

            @Override
            public int compare(
                    Employee e1,
                    Employee e2) {

                return Double.compare(
                        e1.getSalary(),
                        e2.getSalary());
            }
        };
```

Lambda:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        (e1, e2) ->
            Double.compare(
                e1.getSalary(),
                e2.getSalary());
```

Java 8 cleaner:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        Comparator.comparingDouble(
                Employee::getSalary);
```

### Evolution

```text
Anonymous Class
      ↓
Lambda
      ↓
Comparator.comparing()
```

---

# 44. `Comparator.comparing()` Input Ko Strong Karo

Tumhare logic development ke liye ye bahut important hai.

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getName)
```

Ask:

> Employee me se **kis value ke basis par compare karna hai?**

Answer:

```text
Name
```

So:

```java
Employee::getName
```

---

Question:

> Salary basis?

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
```

Question:

> Age basis?

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(
        Employee::getAge)
```

Question:

> String length?

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(
        String::length)
```

## Golden Rule

```text
Comparator.comparing(
    "jis field/value par comparison chahiye"
)
```

---

# 45. Logic Building — Scenario 1

Question:

> Active IT employees ko salary descending order me sort karo.

Requirement todho:

```text
Active
  ↓
filter()

IT
  ↓
filter()

Salary order
  ↓
Comparator

Descending
  ↓
reversed()
```

Code:

```java
List<Employee> result =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 46. Logic Building — Scenario 2

Question:

> Active IT employees ke names salary descending order me chahiye.

Careful.

Agar pehle:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

kar diya, salary information chali jayegi.

So pehle Employee ko salary se sort karo.

Then name extract karo.

```text
Employee
   ↓
filter Active
   ↓
filter IT
   ↓
sort Salary
   ↓
map Name
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 **Operation order** yaha important hai.

---

# 47. Logic Building — Scenario 3

Question:

> Find top 3 unique highest salaries of active IT employees.

Decode:

```text
Active
↓
filter

IT
↓
filter

Salary
↓
map

Unique salary
↓
distinct

Highest first
↓
sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 3
↓
limit
```

Code:

```java
List<Double> salaries =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .map(Employee::getSalary)
         .distinct()
         .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
         .limit(3)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Ye modules combine kar raha hai:

```text
Filter
+
Map
+
Distinct
+
Comparator
+
Limit
+
Collectors
```

---

# 48. Most Important Comparator Methods

| Method                  | Use                     |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------- |
| `comparing()`           | Object/property compare |
| `comparingInt()`        | `int` field             |
| `comparingLong()`       | `long` field            |
| `comparingDouble()`     | `double` field          |
| `reversed()`            | Comparator reverse      |
| `thenComparing()`       | Second-level sorting    |
| `thenComparingInt()`    | Second `int` field      |
| `thenComparingLong()`   | Second `long` field     |
| `thenComparingDouble()` | Second `double` field   |
| `naturalOrder()`        | Natural ascending       |
| `reverseOrder()`        | Natural descending      |
| `nullsFirst()`          | Null first              |
| `nullsLast()`           | Null last               |

---

# 49. Interview Questions

### Q1. Comparator kya hai?

**Hindi-English Answer:**

`Comparator` Java ka functional interface hai jo two objects ko custom criteria ke basis par compare/sort karne ke liye use hota hai.

---

### Q2. Comparator ka main abstract method?

```java
int compare(T o1, T o2);
```

---

### Q3. `Comparator.comparing()` kya karta hai?

Object me se comparison key extract karke us key ke basis par Comparator create karta hai.

Example:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getName)
```

Name comparison key hai.

---

### Q4. Descending kaise karoge?

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
        .reversed()
```

---

### Q5. Multiple fields se sort kaise karoge?

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getDepartment)
        .thenComparing(
        Employee::getName)
```

---

### Q6. Comparable vs Comparator?

**Answer:**

`Comparable` natural/default sorting define karta hai using `compareTo()`, whereas `Comparator` custom/multiple sorting rules define karta hai using `compare()`.

---

### Q7. `reversed()` vs `reverseOrder()`?

`reversed()` existing Comparator ko reverse karta hai, while `reverseOrder()` natural ordering ka reverse Comparator deta hai.

---

### Q8. Primitive fields ke liye kya use kar sakte hain?

```java
comparingInt()
comparingLong()
comparingDouble()
```

---

### Q9. Null values ko kaise handle karoge?

```java
Comparator.nullsFirst()
```

or:

```java
Comparator.nullsLast()
```

---

18. Find lowest salary.
19. Find oldest employee.
20. Find youngest employee.
21. Find longest employee name.
22. Find shortest employee name.
23. Find top 3 highest paid employees.
24. Find second highest paid employee.
25. Find second **distinct** highest salary.
26. Find top 3 distinct salaries.
27. Find highest-paid active employee.
28. Find highest-paid IT employee.
29. Sort active IT employees salary descending.
30. Department-wise highest salary employee.

32. Sort null names with null last.

34. Sort strings length first, then alphabetical.
35. Find top 3 unique salaries of active IT employees.

# 🧠 Comparator Logic Building Formula

Jab bhi sorting question aaye, ye **4 questions** pucho:

### 1. Kis object ko sort kar raha hu?

```text
Employee?
String?
Integer?
Product?
```

### 2. Kis field/value ke basis par?

```text
Salary?
Age?
Name?
Length?
```

Then:

```java
Comparator.comparing(...)
```

### 3. Ascending ya Descending?

```text
Ascending
→ normal comparator

Descending
→ .reversed()
```

### 4. Tie hua to next field kya?

```text
thenComparing()
```

---

# ⭐ Modules 1–14 Master Logic Map

| Requirement         | Pattern                    |
| ------------------- | -------------------------- |
| Select              | `filter()`                 |
| Transform           | `map()`                    |
| Nested → Flat       | `flatMap()`                |
| Arrange             | `sorted()` + `Comparator`  |
| Custom comparison   | `Comparator.comparing()`   |
| Multiple-field sort | `thenComparing()`          |
| Remove Duplicate    | `distinct()`               |
| Highest / Lowest    | `max()/min()` + Comparator |
| How Many            | `count()`                  |
| Multiple Groups     | `groupingBy()`             |
| Two Groups          | `partitioningBy()`         |
| Many → One          | `reduce()`                 |
| Value may be absent | `Optional`                 |
| Final result        | `Collectors`               |

## Final Comparator Memory Trick

```text
"Salary ke basis par"
        ↓
comparing(Employee::getSalary)


"Salary descending"
        ↓
comparing(...).reversed()


"Department, then Salary"
        ↓
comparing(Department)
.thenComparing(Salary)


"Highest Salary"
        ↓
max(Comparator...)


"Lowest Salary"
        ↓
min(Comparator...)
```

### ⭐ Ek line strongly yaad rakho:

> **`Comparator.comparing(X)` me `X` jo value return karega, wahi comparison/sorting key banegi.**

Is concept ko strong kar loge to Employee, Product, Student ya kisi bhi custom object ka sorting question naya hone ke baad bhi tum khud solve kar paoge.

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 14 : Comparator Pattern — Java 8

Ab Comparator Pattern ka main goal hai:

> **Objects ko kisi field ke basis par sort/compare karna — salary, age, name, department — aur multiple fields par sorting karna.**

Ye Module 4 `Sorting` ka advanced part samjho.

---

# 1. Comparator Kya Hai?

Suppose Employee:

```java
class Employee {

    private int id;
    private String name;
    private String department;
    private double salary;
    private int age;

    // getters/setters
}
```

Data:

```text
Vishnu   IT      70000   30
Rahul    HR      50000   28
Amit     IT      90000   32
Sneha    Sales   60000   27
```

Question:

> Employees ko salary ke according sort karo.

Java ko kaise pata chalega ki Employee object me **kis field ko compare karna hai?**

```text
name?
salary?
age?
department?
```

Yaha `Comparator` ka use hota hai.

---

# 2. Comparator Ka Basic Mental Model

```text
Two Objects
    ↓
Compare particular field
    ↓
Which comes first?
```

Example:

```text
Employee1 salary = 50000
Employee2 salary = 70000

        ↓ compare

50000 < 70000

        ↓

Employee1 first
```

---

# 3. Comparator Functional Interface

Package:

```java
java.util.Comparator
```

Comparator ek functional interface hai.

Important method:

```java
int compare(T o1, T o2);
```

Concept:

```text
Two Objects
    ↓
compare()
    ↓
int result
```

---

# 4. `compare()` Return Value

Ye interview me important hai.

```text
Negative → o1 pehle
Zero     → equal according to comparator
Positive → o2 pehle
```

Example:

```java
Comparator<Integer> comparator =
        (a, b) -> a.compareTo(b);
```

Suppose:

```text
a = 10
b = 20
```

Result negative.

Meaning:

```text
10 comes before 20
```

---

# 5. Basic Integer Sorting

```java
List<Integer> list =
        Arrays.asList(5, 2, 8, 1, 9);

list.stream()
    .sorted()
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
1
2
5
8
9
```

Yaha explicit Comparator ki zarurat nahi because Integer ka natural ordering already available hai.

---

# 6. Descending Integer Sorting

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
9
8
5
2
1
```

---

# 7. String Ascending

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList("Vishnu", "Amit", "Rahul", "Sneha");

names.stream()
     .sorted()
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
Amit
Rahul
Sneha
Vishnu
```

---

# 8. String Descending

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 9. Employee Salary Sorting — Traditional Lambda

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted((e1, e2) ->
                 Double.compare(
                         e1.getSalary(),
                         e2.getSalary()))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Meaning:

```text
Employee1
   ↓
salary

vs

Employee2
   ↓
salary
```

---

# 10. `Comparator.comparing()`

Java 8 me clean approach:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

### Is line ko samjho

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

Question:

**`comparing()` ko input kya diya?**

```java
Employee::getSalary
```

Ye Employee leta hai aur comparison key return karta hai:

```text
Employee
   ↓
getSalary()
   ↓
Salary
   ↓
Compare salary
```

## ⭐ Golden Rule

> `Comparator.comparing()` ke andar jo function doge, **us function se return hone wali value comparison key banegi.**

Exactly grouping ke concept jaisa:

```java
groupingBy(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Department → grouping key.

Whereas:

```java
comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

Salary → comparison key.

---

# 11. Employee Name Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee
   ↓
getName()
   ↓
Name becomes comparison key
```

---

# 12. Employee Age Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 13. Employee Department Ascending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getDepartment))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 14. Salary Descending

Most common interview pattern:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                       .reversed())
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Memory:

```text
comparing()
    ↓
Ascending

comparing(...).reversed()
    ↓
Descending
```

---

# 15. Name Descending

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName)
                       .reversed())
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 16. `comparingInt()`

Agar property primitive `int` hai:

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(Employee::getAge)
```

Example:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 17. `comparingDouble()`

Salary `double` hai:

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
```

Full:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Ye:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

se boxing avoid kar sakta hai when getter returns primitive `double`.

---

# 18. Important Comparing Methods

```java
Comparator.comparing()
Comparator.comparingInt()
Comparator.comparingLong()
Comparator.comparingDouble()
```

Memory:

| Field         | Prefer              |
| ------------- | ------------------- |
| String/Object | `comparing()`       |
| `int`         | `comparingInt()`    |
| `long`        | `comparingLong()`   |
| `double`      | `comparingDouble()` |

---

# 19. Multiple Field Sorting — `thenComparing()`

🔥 Very important.

Question:

> Employees ko department-wise sort karo. Same department me salary-wise sort karo.

Requirement:

```text
First
↓
Department

Same Department?
↓
Salary
```

Code:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getSalary))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 20. `thenComparing()` Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?

Suppose:

```text
Vishnu   IT   70000
Amit     IT   50000
Rahul    HR   60000
```

First:

```text
Department
```

So:

```text
HR
IT
IT
```

IT me two employees hain.

Tie:

```text
Vishnu → IT
Amit   → IT
```

Ab:

```java
thenComparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

apply hoga.

Result:

```text
HR → Rahul  60000

IT → Amit   50000
IT → Vishnu 70000
```

---

# 21. Three-Level Sorting

Question:

> Department → Age → Name

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getAge)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Employee::getName))
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Pattern:

```text
Primary Sort
     ↓
Department

Tie?
 ↓
Age

Tie?
 ↓
Name
```

---

# 22. Department Ascending + Salary Descending

Very common interview scenario.

Requirement:

```text
Department
↓
Ascending

Salary
↓
Descending
```

Code:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                     Employee::getDepartment)
                 .thenComparing(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                             Employee::getSalary)
                               .reversed()
                 )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 23. Don't Make This Mistake

Suppose requirement:

> Department ascending, salary descending.

Agar tum:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getDepartment)
          .thenComparing(Employee::getSalary)
          .reversed()
```

likhoge, to `.reversed()` **poore comparator chain ko reverse** karega.

Matlab:

```text
Department bhi reverse
Salary bhi reverse
```

But hume sirf salary descending chahiye.

Correct:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getDepartment)
          .thenComparing(
              Comparator.comparingDouble(
                  Employee::getSalary)
                  .reversed()
          )
```

🔥 Important interview trap.

---

# 24. Sort By String Length

Question:

> Strings ko length ke according sort karo.

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(
             String::length))
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Input:

```text
Java
C
Python
Go
```

Output:

```text
C
Go
Java
Python
```

---

# 25. String Length Descending

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(
             String::length)
             .reversed())
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 26. Length Same Ho To Alphabetical

Question:

> String length ascending. Same length ho to alphabetically.

```java
names.stream()
     .sorted(
         Comparator.comparingInt(String::length)
                   .thenComparing(
                       Comparator.naturalOrder()))
     .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Example:

```text
Raj
Amit
Java
Rahul
Ajay
```

Length 4:

```text
Ajay
Amit
Java
```

Alphabetically arrange honge.

---

# 27. `naturalOrder()`

Natural/default ordering:

```java
Comparator.naturalOrder()
```

Example:

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.naturalOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Numbers:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

Strings:

```text
A B C D
```

---

# 28. `reverseOrder()`

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

Example:

```java
list.stream()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 29. `reversed()` vs `reverseOrder()`

### `reverseOrder()`

Natural ordering ko reverse karta hai:

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

### `reversed()`

Existing Comparator ko reverse karta hai:

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getSalary)
          .reversed()
```

Memory:

```text
reverseOrder()
→ Natural ordering ka reverse


reversed()
→ Maine jo comparator banaya uska reverse
```

---

# 30. Comparator + `max()`

Comparator sirf `sorted()` ke liye nahi hai.

Highest salary:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
         .orElse(null);
```

Notice:

```text
max()
   ↓
"Compare kis basis par?"
   ↓
Comparator
```

---

# 31. Comparator + `min()`

Youngest employee:

```java
Employee emp =
employees.stream()
         .min(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 Employee::getAge))
         .orElse(null);
```

---

# 32. Longest Name Using Comparator

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 String::length))
         .orElse("");
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee
   ↓
map(Name)
   ↓
Compare Length
   ↓
max()
```

---

# 33. Shortest Name

```java
String name =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .min(
             Comparator.comparingInt(
                 String::length))
         .orElse("");
```

---

# 34. Top 3 Highest Paid Employees

Question:

> Top 3 salary employees.

Think:

```text
Highest first
     ↓
Salary Descending

First 3
     ↓
limit(3)
```

Code:

```java
List<Employee> result =
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .limit(3)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 35. Second Highest Salary Employee

```java
Employee employee =
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .skip(1)
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

Pipeline:

```text
Salary Descending
       ↓
skip first
       ↓
findFirst
       ↓
Second Employee
```

⚠️ Ye **second employee after sorting** hai. Agar duplicate salaries hain aur interviewer specifically **second distinct highest salary** poochta hai, logic alag hoga.

---

# 36. Second Distinct Highest Salary

Suppose salaries:

```text
90000
90000
80000
70000
```

Second highest salary should be:

```text
80000
```

Not:

```text
90000
```

Code:

```java
Double salary =
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getSalary)
         .distinct()
         .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
         .skip(1)
         .findFirst()
         .orElse(null);
```

### Logic

```text
Employee
   ↓
map Salary
   ↓
distinct
   ↓
descending sort
   ↓
skip highest
   ↓
find first
```

🔥 Very common interview question.

---

# 37. Highest Salary Department Wise

Comparator + grouping combination:

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
employees.stream()
         .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
             Employee::getDepartment,
             Collectors.maxBy(
                 Comparator.comparingDouble(
                     Employee::getSalary)
             )
         ));
```

Pattern:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy()

Highest
      ↓
maxBy()

Compare what?
      ↓
Salary Comparator
```

---

# 38. Comparator With Null Values

Suppose employee name null ho sakta hai.

Direct:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getName)
```

comparison me problem create kar sakta hai.

Java provides:

```java
Comparator.nullsFirst()
Comparator.nullsLast()
```

---

# 39. `nullsFirst()`

Null values pehle:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName,
                 Comparator.nullsFirst(
                     Comparator.naturalOrder()
                 )
             )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Concept:

```text
null
null
Amit
Rahul
Vishnu
```

---

# 40. `nullsLast()`

Null values last:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparing(
                 Employee::getName,
                 Comparator.nullsLast(
                     Comparator.naturalOrder()
                 )
             )
         )
         .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Concept:

```text
Amit
Rahul
Vishnu
null
null
```

---

# 41. Comparator vs Comparable

🔥 Interview me definitely important.

## Comparable

Class khud define karti hai ki uska **natural/default ordering** kya hai.

Method:

```java
compareTo()
```

Interface:

```java
Comparable<T>
```

Example concept:

```java
class Employee implements Comparable<Employee> {

    @Override
    public int compareTo(Employee other) {
        return this.id - other.id;
    }
}
```

---

## Comparator

External/custom sorting rule.

Method:

```java
compare()
```

Interface:

```java
Comparator<T>
```

Java 8:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary)
```

---

# 42. Comparable vs Comparator Table

| Comparable                | Comparator                      |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| `java.lang`               | `java.util`                     |
| `compareTo()`             | `compare()`                     |
| Natural/default sorting   | Custom sorting                  |
| Class implements it       | Separate comparator             |
| Usually one natural order | Multiple sorting rules possible |

Example:

Employee ka default ID sorting:

```text
Comparable
```

But:

```text
Salary sorting
Name sorting
Age sorting
Department sorting
```

ke liye:

```text
Comparator
```

bahut useful hai.

---

# 43. Old Style vs Java 8 Comparator

Old style:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        new Comparator<Employee>() {

            @Override
            public int compare(
                    Employee e1,
                    Employee e2) {

                return Double.compare(
                        e1.getSalary(),
                        e2.getSalary());
            }
        };
```

Lambda:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        (e1, e2) ->
            Double.compare(
                e1.getSalary(),
                e2.getSalary());
```

Java 8 cleaner:

```java
Comparator<Employee> comparator =
        Comparator.comparingDouble(
                Employee::getSalary);
```

### Evolution

```text
Anonymous Class
      ↓
Lambda
      ↓
Comparator.comparing()
```

---

# 44. `Comparator.comparing()` Input Ko Strong Karo

Tumhare logic development ke liye ye bahut important hai.

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getName)
```

Ask:

> Employee me se **kis value ke basis par compare karna hai?**

Answer:

```text
Name
```

So:

```java
Employee::getName
```

---

Question:

> Salary basis?

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
```

Question:

> Age basis?

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(
        Employee::getAge)
```

Question:

> String length?

```java
Comparator.comparingInt(
        String::length)
```

## Golden Rule

```text
Comparator.comparing(
    "jis field/value par comparison chahiye"
)
```

---

# 45. Logic Building — Scenario 1

Question:

> Active IT employees ko salary descending order me sort karo.

Requirement todho:

```text
Active
  ↓
filter()

IT
  ↓
filter()

Salary order
  ↓
Comparator

Descending
  ↓
reversed()
```

Code:

```java
List<Employee> result =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 46. Logic Building — Scenario 2

Question:

> Active IT employees ke names salary descending order me chahiye.

Careful.

Agar pehle:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

kar diya, salary information chali jayegi.

So pehle Employee ko salary se sort karo.

Then name extract karo.

```text
Employee
   ↓
filter Active
   ↓
filter IT
   ↓
sort Salary
   ↓
map Name
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed())
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 **Operation order** yaha important hai.

---

# 47. Logic Building — Scenario 3

Question:

> Find top 3 unique highest salaries of active IT employees.

Decode:

```text
Active
↓
filter

IT
↓
filter

Salary
↓
map

Unique salary
↓
distinct

Highest first
↓
sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 3
↓
limit
```

Code:

```java
List<Double> salaries =
employees.stream()
         .filter(Employee::isActive)
         .filter(e ->
             "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
         .map(Employee::getSalary)
         .distinct()
         .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
         .limit(3)
         .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Ye modules combine kar raha hai:

```text
Filter
+
Map
+
Distinct
+
Comparator
+
Limit
+
Collectors
```

---

# 48. Most Important Comparator Methods

| Method                  | Use                     |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------- |
| `comparing()`           | Object/property compare |
| `comparingInt()`        | `int` field             |
| `comparingLong()`       | `long` field            |
| `comparingDouble()`     | `double` field          |
| `reversed()`            | Comparator reverse      |
| `thenComparing()`       | Second-level sorting    |
| `thenComparingInt()`    | Second `int` field      |
| `thenComparingLong()`   | Second `long` field     |
| `thenComparingDouble()` | Second `double` field   |
| `naturalOrder()`        | Natural ascending       |
| `reverseOrder()`        | Natural descending      |
| `nullsFirst()`          | Null first              |
| `nullsLast()`           | Null last               |

---

# 49. Interview Questions

### Q1. Comparator kya hai?

**Hindi-English Answer:**

`Comparator` Java ka functional interface hai jo two objects ko custom criteria ke basis par compare/sort karne ke liye use hota hai.

---

### Q2. Comparator ka main abstract method?

```java
int compare(T o1, T o2);
```

---

### Q3. `Comparator.comparing()` kya karta hai?

Object me se comparison key extract karke us key ke basis par Comparator create karta hai.

Example:

```java
Comparator.comparing(Employee::getName)
```

Name comparison key hai.

---

### Q4. Descending kaise karoge?

```java
Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary)
        .reversed()
```

---

### Q5. Multiple fields se sort kaise karoge?

```java
Comparator.comparing(
        Employee::getDepartment)
        .thenComparing(
        Employee::getName)
```

---

### Q6. Comparable vs Comparator?

**Answer:**

`Comparable` natural/default sorting define karta hai using `compareTo()`, whereas `Comparator` custom/multiple sorting rules define karta hai using `compare()`.

---

### Q7. `reversed()` vs `reverseOrder()`?

`reversed()` existing Comparator ko reverse karta hai, while `reverseOrder()` natural ordering ka reverse Comparator deta hai.

---

### Q8. Primitive fields ke liye kya use kar sakte hain?

```java
comparingInt()
comparingLong()
comparingDouble()
```

---

### Q9. Null values ko kaise handle karoge?

```java
Comparator.nullsFirst()
```

or:

```java
Comparator.nullsLast()
```

---

15. Age ascending + salary descending.

18. Find lowest salary.
19. Find oldest employee.
20. Find youngest employee.
21. Find longest employee name.
22. Find shortest employee name.
23. Find top 3 highest paid employees.
24. Find second highest paid employee.
25. Find second **distinct** highest salary.
26. Find top 3 distinct salaries.
27. Find highest-paid active employee.
28. Find highest-paid IT employee.
29. Sort active IT employees salary descending.
30. Department-wise highest salary employee.

32. Sort null names with null last.

# 🧠 Comparator Logic Building Formula

Jab bhi sorting question aaye, ye **4 questions** pucho:

### 1. Kis object ko sort kar raha hu?

```text
Employee?
String?
Integer?
Product?
```

### 2. Kis field/value ke basis par?

```text
Salary?
Age?
Name?
Length?
```

Then:

```java
Comparator.comparing(...)
```

### 3. Ascending ya Descending?

```text
Ascending
→ normal comparator

Descending
→ .reversed()
```

### 4. Tie hua to next field kya?

```text
thenComparing()
```

---

# ⭐ Modules 1–14 Master Logic Map

| Requirement         | Pattern                    |
| ------------------- | -------------------------- |
| Select              | `filter()`                 |
| Transform           | `map()`                    |
| Nested → Flat       | `flatMap()`                |
| Arrange             | `sorted()` + `Comparator`  |
| Custom comparison   | `Comparator.comparing()`   |
| Multiple-field sort | `thenComparing()`          |
| Remove Duplicate    | `distinct()`               |
| Highest / Lowest    | `max()/min()` + Comparator |
| How Many            | `count()`                  |
| Multiple Groups     | `groupingBy()`             |
| Two Groups          | `partitioningBy()`         |
| Many → One          | `reduce()`                 |
| Value may be absent | `Optional`                 |
| Final result        | `Collectors`               |

## Final Comparator Memory Trick

```text
"Salary ke basis par"
        ↓
comparing(Employee::getSalary)


"Salary descending"
        ↓
comparing(...).reversed()


"Department, then Salary"
        ↓
comparing(Department)
.thenComparing(Salary)


"Highest Salary"
        ↓
max(Comparator...)


"Lowest Salary"
        ↓
min(Comparator...)
```

### ⭐ Ek line strongly yaad rakho:

> **`Comparator.comparing(X)` me `X` jo value return karega, wahi comparison/sorting key banegi.**

Is concept ko strong kar loge to Employee, Product, Student ya kisi bhi custom object ka sorting question naya hone ke baad bhi tum khud solve kar paoge.

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 15 : String Programs — Java 8

Ab String Programs ko **program-by-program ratne ke bajay patterns** me samjhenge.

String questions me mostly ye thinking repeat hoti hai:

```text
String
  ↓
characters / words me todo
  ↓
filter / map / grouping / sorting
  ↓
required result
```

Java 8 me String ke liye sabse powerful starting point hai:

```java
str.chars()
```

---

# 1. String ko Characters ke Stream me Convert Karna

Suppose:

```java
String str = "java";
```

Agar:

```java
str.chars()
```

karte ho, return hota hai:

```java
IntStream
```

Direct `Stream<Character>` nahi.

Isliye commonly:

```java
str.chars()
   .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
```

Mental model:

```text
"java"
   ↓
chars()
   ↓
IntStream
   ↓
mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
   ↓
j a v a
```

Ye String programs ka **base pattern** hai.

---

# 2. Print All Characters

```java
String str = "java";

str.chars()
   .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
   .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
j
a
v
a
```

---

# 3. Count Total Characters

```java
long count = str.chars().count();

System.out.println(count);
```

For:

```text
java
```

Output:

```text
4
```

Pattern:

```text
How many?
   ↓
count()
```

---

# 4. Count Vowels

Question:

> String me kitne vowels hain?

Logic:

```text
Characters
   ↓
Vowel select
   ↓
Count
```

Code:

```java
long count =
        str.toLowerCase()
           .chars()
           .filter(c -> "aeiou".indexOf(c) != -1)
           .count();
```

Ye line important hai:

```java
"aeiou".indexOf(c) != -1
```

Meaning:

Agar character vowels ke andar mila:

```text
index >= 0
```

to vowel hai.

---

# 5. Count Consonants

```java
long count =
        str.toLowerCase()
           .chars()
           .filter(Character::isLetter)
           .filter(c -> "aeiou".indexOf(c) == -1)
           .count();
```

Logic:

```text
Letter hona chahiye
       +
Vowel nahi hona chahiye
       =
Consonant
```

---

# 6. Count Digits

Input:

```java
String str = "java123spring45";
```

Code:

```java
long count =
        str.chars()
           .filter(Character::isDigit)
           .count();
```

Output:

```text
5
```

---

# 7. Count Alphabets

```java
long count =
        str.chars()
           .filter(Character::isLetter)
           .count();
```

---

# 8. Count Spaces

```java
long count =
        str.chars()
           .filter(Character::isWhitespace)
           .count();
```

---

# 9. Count Uppercase Characters

```java
long count =
        str.chars()
           .filter(Character::isUpperCase)
           .count();
```

---

# 10. Count Lowercase Characters

```java
long count =
        str.chars()
           .filter(Character::isLowerCase)
           .count();
```

## First String Pattern

```text
Count something
      ↓
chars()
      ↓
filter(condition)
      ↓
count()
```

Examples:

```text
Vowels       → filter(vowel)
Digits       → filter(isDigit)
Letters      → filter(isLetter)
Uppercase    → filter(isUpperCase)
Whitespace   → filter(isWhitespace)
```

---

# 11. Find Duplicate Characters

🔥 Very common interview question.

Input:

```text
programming
```

Hume characters ko group karna hai:

```text
p → 1
r → 2
o → 1
g → 2
a → 1
m → 2
i → 1
n → 1
```

Then:

```text
count > 1
```

duplicate.

Code:

```java
Map<Character, Long> frequency =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   Collectors.counting()
           ));
```

Duplicates:

```java
frequency.entrySet()
         .stream()
         .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
         .forEach(e ->
                 System.out.println(
                         e.getKey() + " = " + e.getValue()));
```

---

# 12. Duplicate Character Logic

Question ko todho:

```text
Character-wise
      ↓
groupingBy()

Kitni baar?
      ↓
counting()

Duplicate?
      ↓
count > 1
```

Pattern:

```java
groupingBy(
    Function.identity(),
    counting()
)
```

🔥 Ye String frequency programs ka master pattern hai.

---

# 13. Character Frequency

Question:

> Har character kitni baar aya hai?

```java
Map<Character, Long> frequency =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   Collectors.counting()
           ));
```

For:

```text
banana
```

Conceptually:

```text
b → 1
a → 3
n → 2
```

---

# 14. `Function.identity()` Yaha Kya Kar Raha Hai?

Input:

```text
a
```

Return:

```text
a
```

Input:

```text
b
```

Return:

```text
b
```

Matlab:

```java
Function.identity()
```

same as:

```java
c -> c
```

So:

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
        Function.identity(),
        Collectors.counting()
)
```

means:

> Same characters ko group karo aur count karo.

---

# 15. Find Unique Characters

Unique means:

```text
frequency == 1
```

```java
str.chars()
   .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
   .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
           Function.identity(),
           Collectors.counting()
   ))
   .entrySet()
   .stream()
   .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
   .forEach(e ->
           System.out.println(e.getKey()));
```

---

# 16. Remove Duplicate Characters

Input:

```text
banana
```

Expected:

```text
ban
```

Code:

```java
String result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .distinct()
           .map(String::valueOf)
           .collect(Collectors.joining());

System.out.println(result);
```

Output:

```text
ban
```

---

# 17. Is Program Ka Logic Samjho

```text
"banana"
    ↓
chars()
    ↓
b a n a n a
    ↓
distinct()
    ↓
b a n
    ↓
String::valueOf
    ↓
"b" "a" "n"
    ↓
joining()
    ↓
"ban"
```

Yaha multiple modules combine hue:

```text
String
+ map
+ distinct
+ collectors
```

---===========================================================================================================================
Haan. `groupingBy()` dekhne me confusing lagta hai, but isko **3 inputs wale formula** se yaad rakho.

Tumhara code:

```java
.collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
    Function.identity(),
    LinkedHashMap::new,
    Collectors.counting()
))
```

`groupingBy()` ka ye version **3 inputs** leta hai:

```java
groupingBy(
    classifier,
    mapFactory,
    downstreamCollector
)
```

Tumhare example me:

```text
Function.identity()     → KEY kya hogi?
LinkedHashMap::new      → Kaunsa Map banana hai?
Collectors.counting()   → VALUE kya hogi?
```

### 1. First input — `classifier`

```java
Function.identity()
```

Ye decide karta hai **GROUP/KEY kya hogi**.

Tumhare stream me:

```text
a
b
a
c
```

`Function.identity()` ka matlab:

```java
c -> c
```

jo aaya wahi return karo.

Isliye:

```text
a → key "a"
b → key "b"
c → key "c"
```

First parameter me koi bhi `Function` de sakte ho jo **key return kare**.

For example:

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Key:

```text
IT
HR
Finance
```

Ya:

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(String::length)
```

Key:

```text
3
4
5
```

**Rule: First parameter jo return karega, wahi KEY banegi.**

---

### 2. Second input — `mapFactory`

```java
LinkedHashMap::new
```

Ye decide karta hai:

> Result ke liye **kaunsa Map implementation** use karna hai?

Tum de sakte ho:

```java
HashMap::new
LinkedHashMap::new
TreeMap::new
```

Example:

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
    Function.identity(),
    TreeMap::new,
    Collectors.counting()
)
```

Difference:

```text
HashMap       → order guaranteed nahi
LinkedHashMap → insertion order maintain
TreeMap       → key sorted order
```

Tumhare question me `LinkedHashMap` important hai because tum baad me:

```java
.findFirst()
```

kar rahe ho.

Tumhe **first non-repeating character** chahiye, isliye original character order preserve karna useful hai.

---

### 3. Third input — `downstreamCollector`

```java
Collectors.counting()
```

Ye decide karta hai:

> Har group ke saath **VALUE me kya collect karna hai?**

Example:

```java
Collectors.counting()
```

Frequency count karega.

Input:

```text
banana
```

Result roughly:

```text
b = 1
a = 3
n = 2
```

Lekin third parameter sirf `counting()` nahi hota.

Bahut saare collectors aa sakte hain:

```java
Collectors.counting()
Collectors.toList()
Collectors.toSet()
Collectors.summingInt(...)
Collectors.averagingInt(...)
Collectors.mapping(...)
Collectors.maxBy(...)
Collectors.minBy(...)
Collectors.joining(...)
```

For example Employees ko department ke according group karo:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =
    employees.stream()
        .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
            Employee::getDepartment
        ));
```

Yaha internally default downstream collector `toList()` hota hai.

Salary sum chahiye:

```java
employees.stream()
    .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
        Employee::getDepartment,
        Collectors.summingDouble(Employee::getSalary)
    ));
```

Result:

```text
IT = 250000
HR = 180000
```

---

## `groupingBy()` ke main 3 forms

Ye interview ke liye important hai:

```java
// 1 parameter
groupingBy(classifier)
```

Example:

```java
groupingBy(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Result:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>>
```

---

```java
// 2 parameters
groupingBy(
    classifier,
    downstreamCollector
)
```

Example:

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    Collectors.counting()
)
```

Result:

```java
Map<String, Long>
```

---

```java
// 3 parameters
groupingBy(
    classifier,
    mapFactory,
    downstreamCollector
)
```

Example:

```java
groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    LinkedHashMap::new,
    Collectors.counting()
)
```

Result:

```java
Map<String, Long>
```

but Map implementation = `LinkedHashMap`.

### Shortcut yaad rakho

```text
groupingBy(
   KEY kis basis pe?,       ← classifier
   MAP kaunsa?,             ← mapFactory
   VALUE me kya chahiye?    ← downstream collector
)
```

Tumhare code me:

```java
groupingBy(
    Function.identity(),   // Character = KEY
    LinkedHashMap::new,    // LinkedHashMap use karo
    Collectors.counting()  // frequency = VALUE
)
```

So:

```text
"swiss"

s → 3
w → 1
i → 1
```

Aur baad ka:

```java
.filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
.findFirst()
```

pehla frequency `1` wala character nikal deta hai → **`w`**.

==============================================================================================================================

# 18. First Non-Repeated Character

🔥🔥 Extremely common interview question.

Input:

```text
swiss
```

Frequency:

```text
s → 3
w → 1
i → 1
```

First non-repeated:

```text
w
```

Important issue:

`groupingBy()` me order preserve karna padega.

Code:

```java
Character result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   LinkedHashMap::new,
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
           .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
           .findFirst()
           .orElse(null);
```

---

# 19. `LinkedHashMap` Kyu?

Because question hai:

> **FIRST** non-repeated character.

Hume original insertion order preserve karna hai.

```text
HashMap
→ order guarantee nahi

LinkedHashMap
→ insertion order preserve
```

So:

```java
LinkedHashMap::new
```

important hai.

---

# 20. First Repeated Character

Same pattern, condition change:

```java
Character result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   LinkedHashMap::new,
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
           .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
           .findFirst()
           .orElse(null);
```

Notice:

First non-repeated:

```java
e.getValue() == 1
```

First repeated:

```java
e.getValue() > 1
```

Pattern same.

---

# 21. Most Frequent Character

Input:

```text
banana
```

Frequency:

```text
b → 1
a → 3
n → 2
```

Maximum frequency:

```text
a
```

Code:

```java
Map.Entry<Character, Long> result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
           .orElse(null);
```

Output concept:

```text
a = 3
```

Logic:

```text
Character frequency
      ↓
entrySet()
      ↓
max by value
```

---

# 22. Reverse String Using Java 8

Simple practical solution:

```java
String result =
        new StringBuilder(str)
                .reverse()
                .toString();
```

Example:

```text
java
 ↓
avaj
```

Important:

> Har String question ko Stream se solve karna zaruri nahi. Interview me simplest correct solution often better hota hai.

---

# 23. Reverse Using IntStream

Java 8 Stream practice ke liye:

```java
String result =
        IntStream.range(0, str.length())
                 .mapToObj(i ->
                         String.valueOf(
                                 str.charAt(
                                     str.length() - 1 - i)))
                 .collect(Collectors.joining());
```

But practical interview me reverse ke liye:

```java
new StringBuilder(str).reverse()
```

simple hai.

---

# 24. Palindrome Check

Input:

```text
madam
```

Reverse:

```text
madam
```

Original == Reverse.

```java
boolean palindrome =
        str.equals(
            new StringBuilder(str)
                .reverse()
                .toString()
        );
```

Output:

```text
true
```

---

# 25. Palindrome Ignore Case

```java
boolean palindrome =
        str.equalsIgnoreCase(
            new StringBuilder(str)
                .reverse()
                .toString()
        );
```

---

# 26. Palindrome Using IntStream

Java 8 logic practice:

```java
boolean palindrome =
        IntStream.range(
                0,
                str.length() / 2
        )
        .allMatch(i ->
                str.charAt(i)
                ==
                str.charAt(
                    str.length() - 1 - i
                )
        );
```

Mental model:

```text
M A D A M
↑       ↑
compare

  ↑   ↑
 compare
```

Half string tak compare enough hai.

---

```java
String result =
        str.chars()

                   String.valueOf(
                           Character.toUpperCase((char) c)))
           .collect(Collectors.joining());
```

But obviously normal Java me:

```java
str.toUpperCase()
```

better hai.

Streams ka version logic practice ke liye hai.

# 34. Check Anagram

🔥 Common interview question.

Input:

```text
listen
silent
```

Dono characters sort karo:

```text
eilnst
eilnst
```

Same → Anagram.

Java 8:

```java
String s1 = "listen";
String s2 = "silent";

String sorted1 =
        s1.toLowerCase()
          .chars()
          .sorted()
          .mapToObj(c ->
                  String.valueOf((char)c))
          .collect(Collectors.joining());

String sorted2 =
        s2.toLowerCase()
          .chars()
          .sorted()
          .mapToObj(c ->
                  String.valueOf((char)c))
          .collect(Collectors.joining());

boolean anagram =
        sorted1.equals(sorted2);
```

---

# 35. Anagram Logic

Don't memorize code.

Think:

```text
Do both Strings contain
same characters
same number of times?
        ↓
Sort both
        ↓
Compare
```

Example:

```text
listen → eilnst
silent → eilnst

same
↓
Anagram
```

---

# 36. Count Words

Input:

```java
String sentence =
        "Java Spring Boot Microservices";
```

Code:

```java
long count =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .count();
```

Output:

```text
4
```

---

# 37. String Programs Ke 2 Main Starting Points

Character-level question:

```text
String
  ↓
chars()
```

Examples:

```text
vowels
duplicates
frequency
digits
palindrome characters
```

Word-level question:

```text
Sentence
   ↓
split()
   ↓
Arrays.stream()
```

Examples:

```text
word count
duplicate words
word frequency
longest word
shortest word
```

🔥 Ye decision bahut important hai.

---

# 38. Word Frequency

Input:

```text
java spring java boot spring java
```

Code:

```java
Map<String, Long> frequency =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

Result:

```text
java   → 3
spring → 2
boot   → 1
```

Same grouping pattern!

Character frequency:

```java
Stream<Character>
    ↓
groupingBy(identity, counting)
```

Word frequency:

```java
Stream<String>
    ↓
groupingBy(identity, counting)
```

---

# 39. Find Duplicate Words

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 40. Find Unique Words

Condition change:

```java
List<String> unique =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 41. Longest Word

Input:

```text
Java Spring Microservices AWS
```

Code:

```java
String longest =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .max(Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length))
              .orElse("");
```

Logic:

```text
Words
 ↓
Longest
 ↓
max()

Compare kis basis par?
 ↓
length
```

---

# 42. Shortest Word

```java
String shortest =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .min(Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length))
              .orElse("");
```

```java
List<String> result =
```

# 44. Sort Words By Length

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .sorted(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length))
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 45. Sort Words By Length Descending

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .sorted(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length)
                      .reversed())
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 46. Find Words Starting With A

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .filter(word ->
                      word.startsWith("A"))
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Case-insensitive:

```java
.filter(word ->
        word.toLowerCase().startsWith("a"))
```

```java
List<String> result =
```

```text
      ↓
```

# 49. Reverse Every Word

Input:

```text
java spring boot
```

Expected:

```text
avaj gnirps toob
```

Code:

```java
String result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .map(word ->
                      new StringBuilder(word)
                              .reverse()
                              .toString())
              .collect(Collectors.joining(" "));
```

Important difference:

### Reverse complete sentence

```text
java spring
↓
gnirps avaj
```

### Reverse each word

```text
java spring
↓
avaj gnirps
```

Requirement carefully read karo.

# 50. Reverse Word Order

Input:

```text
I love Java
```

Expected:

```text
Java love I
```

Code:

```java
String[] words = sentence.split("\\s+");

String result =
        IntStream.range(0, words.length)
                 .mapToObj(i ->
                         words[words.length - 1 - i])
                 .collect(Collectors.joining(" "));
```

# 51. Find First Non-Repeated Word

Input:

```text
java spring java boot spring aws
```

Frequency:

```text
java   → 2
spring → 2
boot   → 1
aws    → 1
```

First non-repeated:

```text
boot
```

Code:

```java
String result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      LinkedHashMap::new,
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(null);
```

Notice same exact pattern as character version.

---

# 52. Second Most Important String Pattern — Frequency

Character ho ya word, frequency pattern same hai:

```java
.collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
        Function.identity(),
        Collectors.counting()
))
```

Then requirement decide karegi:

```text
All frequency
→ directly Map


Duplicate
→ count > 1


Unique
→ count == 1


Most frequent
→ max(value)


Least frequent
→ min(value)


First unique
→ LinkedHashMap + count == 1 + findFirst
```

🔥 Ye ek pattern seekhne se bahut programs solve honge.

---

# 53. Find Most Frequent Word

```java
Map.Entry<String, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
              .orElse(null);
```

---

# 54. Find Longest Word Length

Agar word nahi, sirf maximum length chahiye:

```java
int maxLength =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .mapToInt(String::length)
              .max()
              .orElse(0);
```

Difference:

### Longest word

```java
.max(Comparator.comparingInt(String::length))
```

Return:

```text
String
```

### Longest word length

```java
.mapToInt(String::length)
.max()
```

Return:

```text
int
```

# 57. Group Words By Length

🔥 Good interview question.

Input:

```text
Java is very powerful
```

Expected concept:

```text
2 → [is]
4 → [Java, very]
8 → [powerful]
```

Code:

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      String::length
              ));
```

Think:

```text
Group kis basis par?
      ↓
Length

groupingBy(String::length)
```

Remember:

> `groupingBy()` me function jo return karega wahi key banegi.

# 58. Group Words By First Character

```java
Map<Character, List<String>> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      word -> word.charAt(0)
              ));
```

```text
First Character
```

because:

```java
word -> word.charAt(0)
```

return kar raha hai.

# 59. Count Words By Length

Question:

> Har length ke kitne words hain?

```java
Map<Integer, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      String::length,
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

Example:

```text
2 → 1
4 → 3
6 → 2
```

Pattern:

```text
Group by Length
      +
Count
```

# 60. Check If String Contains Only Digits

```java
boolean result =
        !str.isEmpty()
        &&
        str.chars()
           .allMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

Example:

```text
"12345" → true
"12a45" → false
```

---

# 61. Check If String Contains Only Alphabets

```java
boolean result =
        !str.isEmpty()
        &&
        str.chars()
           .allMatch(Character::isLetter);
```

---

# 62. Check If Any Digit Exists

```java
boolean result =
        str.chars()
           .anyMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

Mental pattern:

```text
Koi ek satisfy karta hai?
      ↓
anyMatch()
```

---

# 63. Check If No Digit Exists

```java
boolean result =
        str.chars()
           .noneMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

---

# 64. `anyMatch`, `allMatch`, `noneMatch`

Ye String programs me bahut useful hain:

```text
Koi ek?
→ anyMatch()

Sab?
→ allMatch()

Ek bhi nahi?
→ noneMatch()
```

Example:

```java
str.chars().anyMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

```java
str.chars().allMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

```java
str.chars().noneMatch(Character::isDigit);
```

---

# 65. Character With Highest Frequency

```java
Character result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
           .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
           .orElse(null);
```

Notice Optional Pattern bhi combine hua:

```text
max()
 ↓
Optional<Entry>
 ↓
map(getKey)
 ↓
orElse
```

---

# 66. Find Second Highest Frequency Character

Advanced pattern:

```java
Character result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .sorted(
               Map.Entry.<Character, Long>
                       comparingByValue()
                       .reversed()
           )
           .skip(1)
           .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
           .findFirst()
           .orElse(null);
```

But careful:

> Agar interviewer **second distinct highest frequency** bolta hai to duplicate frequency values ko separately handle karna padega.

---

# 67. Character Programs Master Pattern

Most character programs:

```java
str.chars()
```

Then requirement:

```text
Condition?
→ filter()


Character change?
→ map()


Character object chahiye?
→ mapToObj()


Duplicates remove?
→ distinct()


Sort?
→ sorted()


Count?
→ count()


Frequency?
→ groupingBy + counting


Single String result?
→ map(String::valueOf) + joining()
```

---

# 68. Word Programs Master Pattern

Most word programs:

```java
Arrays.stream(
    sentence.split("\\s+")
)
```

Then:

```text
Condition
→ filter()

Transform
→ map()

Sort
→ sorted()

Longest
→ max(comparing length)

Shortest
→ min(comparing length)

Frequency
→ groupingBy + counting

Duplicates
→ frequency > 1

Single String
→ joining()
```

---

# 69. Interview Questions

### Q1. Java 8 me String characters ko stream kaise karoge?

```java
str.chars()
```

Ye `IntStream` return karta hai.

---

### Q2. `chars()` `Stream<Character>` return karta hai?

**No.**

```java
str.chars()
```

returns:

```java
IntStream
```

Character objects chahiye:

```java
str.chars()
   .mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
```

---

### Q3. Character frequency kaise find karoge?

```java
str.chars()
   .mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
   .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
       Function.identity(),
       Collectors.counting()
   ));
```

---

### Q4. First non-repeated character me `LinkedHashMap` kyu?

Because original insertion order preserve karna hai.

---

### Q5. Duplicate characters ka logic?

```text
groupingBy character
      ↓
counting
      ↓
count > 1
```

---

### Q6. Unique character ka logic?

```text
count == 1
```

---

### Q7. Remove duplicate characters?

```java
str.chars()
   .distinct()
```

---

### Q8. Longest word kaise?

```java
Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
      .max(Comparator.comparingInt(
              String::length))
```

---

### Q9. Word frequency?

Same pattern:

```java
groupingBy(
    Function.identity(),
    counting()
)
```

---

### Q10. Palindrome ka logic?

Original string ko reverse string se compare karo, ya first-half characters ko corresponding last-half characters se compare karo.
