# 🔥 String Question Ko Kaise Decode Karna Hai

Suppose interviewer asks:

> **Find first non-repeated character from a String.**

Don't remember code.

Translate requirement:

```text
Character level question
        ↓
chars()

Har character kitni baar?
        ↓
groupingBy + counting

"First" important hai
        ↓
LinkedHashMap

Non-repeated
        ↓
count == 1

First result
        ↓
findFirst()

May not exist
        ↓
Optional / orElse
```

Code automatically build hoga:

```java
Character result =
        str.chars()
           .mapToObj(c -> (char)c)
           .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                   Function.identity(),
                   LinkedHashMap::new,
                   Collectors.counting()
           ))
           .entrySet()
           .stream()
           .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
           .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
           .findFirst()
           .orElse(null);
```

Yahi tumhara target hai: **code yaad nahi, requirement → pattern identify karna.**

---

# ⭐ MODULE 15 Master Cheat Sheet

```text
STRING QUESTION
      │
      ├── Character level?
      │        ↓
      │     str.chars()
      │
      └── Word level?
               ↓
       Arrays.stream(str.split("\\s+"))
```

Uske baad:

| Question me word  | Think                           |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------- |
| Select / only     | `filter()`                      |
| Convert/change    | `map()`                         |
| Duplicate remove  | `distinct()`                    |
| Count             | `count()`                       |
| Frequency         | `groupingBy()` + `counting()`   |
| Duplicate         | frequency `> 1`                 |
| Unique            | frequency `== 1`                |
| First             | `LinkedHashMap` + `findFirst()` |
| Highest frequency | `max(comparingByValue())`       |
| Lowest frequency  | `min(comparingByValue())`       |
| Longest           | `max(comparingInt(length))`     |
| Shortest          | `min(comparingInt(length))`     |
| Sort              | `sorted()`                      |
| Combine Strings   | `joining()`                     |
| Any satisfies     | `anyMatch()`                    |
| All satisfy       | `allMatch()`                    |
| None satisfy      | `noneMatch()`                   |

## ⭐ Final Memory Formula

```text
Character Question
      ↓
chars()

Word Question
      ↓
split() + Arrays.stream()

Condition
      ↓
filter()

Transformation
      ↓
map()

Frequency
      ↓
groupingBy(identity, counting)

First + Frequency
      ↓
LinkedHashMap

Highest / Lowest
      ↓
max() / min()

Final String
      ↓
joining()
```

**String programs me 50 alag logics nahi hain. Mostly 6–8 patterns baar-baar different requirements ke saath repeat hote hain.**

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 16 : Array Programs — Java 8

Array programs me bhi 50 alag logic ratne ki zarurat nahi hai.

Main starting point:

```java
int[] arr = {10, 20, 30, 40, 50};

Arrays.stream(arr)
```

> ⭐ **Array → `Arrays.stream()` → requirement ke according `filter / map / sorted / distinct / max / min / sum / grouping`.**

---

# 1. Array ko Stream me Convert Kaise Kare?

For primitive `int[]`:

```java
int[] arr = {10, 20, 30, 40, 50};

IntStream stream = Arrays.stream(arr);
```

Return:

```text
int[] 
 ↓
Arrays.stream()
 ↓
IntStream
```

For object array:

```java
String[] names = {"Java", "Spring", "Boot"};

Stream<String> stream = Arrays.stream(names);
```

---

# 2. Print All Array Elements

```java
int[] arr = {10, 20, 30, 40, 50};

Arrays.stream(arr)
      .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 3. Count Elements

```java
long count =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .count();
```

Or normally:

```java
int count = arr.length;
```

---

# 4. Find Even Numbers

Question:

> Array se even numbers nikalo.

Think:

```text
Array
 ↓
Select
 ↓
filter()
```

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .toArray();
```

---

# 5. Find Odd Numbers

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
              .toArray();
```

### Pattern

```text
Condition hai
    ↓
filter()
```

---

# 6. Numbers Greater Than 50

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n > 50)
              .toArray();
```

Again logic same:

```text
Even          → n % 2 == 0
Odd           → n % 2 != 0
Greater 50    → n > 50
Less 50       → n < 50

Method same:
filter()
```

---

# 7. Sum of Array

```java
int sum =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .sum();
```

Example:

```text
10 20 30

↓

60
```

---

# 8. Average of Array

```java
double average =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .average()
              .orElse(0.0);
```

Why `orElse()`?

Because:

```java
average()
```

returns:

```java
OptionalDouble
```

Array empty ho sakta hai.

```java
              .orElse(0);
```

# 10. Minimum Element

```java
int min =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .min()
              .orElse(0);
```

### Mental Pattern

```text
Total     → sum()
Average   → average()
Highest   → max()
Lowest    → min()
How many  → count()
```

# 17. Sum of Squares of Even Numbers

🔥 Modules combine.

Question:

> Even numbers ke square ka sum.

```text
Even
 ↓

Square
 ↓
map()

Total
 ↓
sum()
```

```java
int sum =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .map(n -> n * n)
              .sum();
```

Yahi logic building hai.

# 18. Sort Array Ascending

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .sorted()
              .toArray();
```

Input:

```text
5 2 8 1 9
```

Output:

```text
1 2 5 8 9
```

---

# 19. Sort Array Descending

Primitive `IntStream` me directly:

```java
.sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
```

nahi laga sakte.

Kyunki `IntStream` primitive stream hai.

Box karo:

```java
Integer[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
              .toArray(Integer[]::new);
```

Flow:

```text
int
 ↓
boxed()
 ↓
Integer
 ↓
Comparator
 ↓
Descending
```

---

# 20. `boxed()` Kya Karta Hai?

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

returns:

```text
IntStream
```

Then:

```java
.boxed()
```

converts:

```text
IntStream
   ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

Example:

```text
int     → Integer
10      → Integer(10)
20      → Integer(20)
```

Useful jab:

```text
Comparator
Collectors
groupingBy
```

jaise object-stream operations chahiye.

---

# 21. Remove Duplicate Elements

Input:

```text
1 2 2 3 3 4 5
```

Expected:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .distinct()
              .toArray();
```

Easy:

```text
Duplicate remove
      ↓
distinct()
```

---

# 22. Find Duplicate Elements

🔥 Common interview question.

Input:

```java
int[] arr = {1, 2, 3, 2, 4, 3, 5};
```

Frequency chahiye.

First box:

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

Result conceptually:

```text
1 → 1
2 → 2
3 → 2
4 → 1
5 → 1
```

Duplicates:

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[2, 3]
```

---

# 23. Duplicate Logic

Don't memorize code.

Question says:

> Duplicate elements.

Think:

```text
Har number kitni baar?
       ↓
groupingBy + counting

Duplicate means?
       ↓
count > 1
```

Exactly same pattern jo String duplicate characters me use kiya tha.

---

# 24. Frequency of Each Element

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

Example:

```text
10 → 2
20 → 3
30 → 1
```

🔥 Master frequency pattern:

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
        Function.identity(),
        Collectors.counting()
)
```

Ye:

```text
String characters
Words
List elements
Array elements
```

sab jagah same hai.

---

# 25. Find Unique Elements

Unique means:

```text
count == 1
```

```java
List<Integer> unique =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 26. `distinct()` vs Unique Elements

Important difference.

Input:

```text
1 2 2 3 4 4
```

### `distinct()`

```java
Arrays.stream(arr).distinct()
```

Result:

```text
1 2 3 4
```

Matlab:

> Har value ek baar rakho.

### Unique Elements

Frequency `== 1`:

```text
1 3
```

Matlab:

> Jo original array me sirf ek baar aaye.

🔥 **Distinct ≠ Unique**

```java
int first =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(0);
```

```java
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(0);
```

```text
Even
 ↓
filter

First
 ↓
findFirst

May not exist
 ↓
orElse
```

# 30. Second Highest Element

Suppose:

```text
10 50 30 80 40
```

Think:

```text
Highest first
    ↓
Descending

First nahi chahiye
    ↓
skip(1)

Next
    ↓
findFirst()
```

```java
Integer secondHighest =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
              .skip(1)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(null);
```

Result:

```text
50
```

# 31. Second Distinct Highest Element

Suppose:

```text
80 80 50 40
```

Second highest distinct:

```text
50
```

Not second element:

```text
80
```

Code:

```java
Integer secondHighest =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .distinct()
              .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
              .skip(1)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(null);
```

Pattern:

```text
Duplicate salary/value?
       ↓
distinct()

Descending
       ↓
sorted(reverse)

Skip highest
       ↓
skip(1)

Next
       ↓
findFirst()
```

# 37. Check If Number Exists

Question:

> Array me `50` hai kya?

```java
boolean exists =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .anyMatch(n -> n == 50);
```

Memory:

```text
Koi ek match?
    ↓
anyMatch()
```

---

# 38. Check All Numbers Positive

```java
boolean result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .allMatch(n -> n > 0);
```

---

# 39. Check If No Negative Number Exists

```java
boolean result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .noneMatch(n -> n < 0);
```

### Match Pattern

```text
Koi ek?
→ anyMatch()

Sab?
→ allMatch()

Ek bhi nahi?
→ noneMatch()
```

---

# 40. Count Even and Odd Numbers

Simple separately:

```java
long even =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .count();

long odd =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
              .count();
```

But Module 9 partitioning use karke:

```java
Map<Boolean, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                      n -> n % 2 == 0,
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → even count
false → odd count
```

---

# 41. Partition Even and Odd

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Integer>> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                      n -> n % 2 == 0
              ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → [2,4,6,8]
false → [1,3,5,7]
```

---

# 42. Group Numbers By Even/Odd

Technically grouping bhi kar sakte ho:

```java
Map<String, List<Integer>> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      n -> n % 2 == 0
                              ? "EVEN"
                              : "ODD"
              ));
```

But exactly two boolean groups ke liye:

```java
partitioningBy()
```

more natural hai.

---

# 43. Group Numbers By Remainder

Question:

> Numbers ko `% 3` remainder ke according group karo.

```java
Map<Integer, List<Integer>> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      n -> n % 3
              ));
```

Example:

```text
0 → [3,6,9]
1 → [1,4,7]
2 → [2,5,8]
```

Remember:

> `groupingBy()` me function jo return karega wahi key banega.

Here:

```java
n -> n % 3
```

returns:

```text
0 / 1 / 2
```

So wahi keys hain.

---

# 44. Count Frequency Using `toMap()`

Alternative:

```java
Map<Integer, Integer> frequency =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                      Function.identity(),
                      n -> 1,
                      Integer::sum
              ));
```

Understand:

```text
Key
 ↓
number itself

Value
 ↓
1

Same key again?
 ↓
Integer::sum
```

Example:

```text
2 comes 3 times

2 → 1
2 → 1
2 → 1

merge
 ↓
2 → 3
```

But beginners ke liye:

```java
groupingBy + counting
```

usually easier.

---

# 45. Product of All Elements

No direct `product()` method.

Use `reduce()`:

```java
int product =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .reduce(1, (a, b) -> a * b);
```

Pattern:

```text
Many values combine
       ↓
reduce()
```

---

# 46. Product of Even Numbers

```java
int product =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .reduce(1, (a, b) -> a * b);
```

---

# 47. Difference Between Max and Min

```java
IntSummaryStatistics stats =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .summaryStatistics();

int difference =
        stats.getMax() - stats.getMin();
```

---

# 48. Array Statistics

🔥 Useful built-in feature:

```java
IntSummaryStatistics stats =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .summaryStatistics();
```

Then:

```java
stats.getCount();
stats.getSum();
stats.getMin();
stats.getAverage();
stats.getMax();
```

One operation gives:

```text
Count
Sum
Min
Average
Max
```

---

# 49. Merge Two Arrays

Suppose:

```java
int[] arr1 = {1, 2, 3};
int[] arr2 = {4, 5, 6};
```

Use:

```java
int[] result =
        IntStream.concat(
                Arrays.stream(arr1),
                Arrays.stream(arr2)
        ).toArray();
```

Output:

```text
1 2 3 4 5 6
```

---

# 50. Merge Two Arrays and Remove Duplicates

```java
int[] result =
        IntStream.concat(
                Arrays.stream(arr1),
                Arrays.stream(arr2)
        )
        .distinct()
        .toArray();
```

---

# 51. Merge Two Arrays and Sort

```java
int[] result =
        IntStream.concat(
                Arrays.stream(arr1),
                Arrays.stream(arr2)
        )
        .sorted()
        .toArray();
```

---

# 52. Merge + Unique + Sorted

```java
int[] result =
        IntStream.concat(
                Arrays.stream(arr1),
                Arrays.stream(arr2)
        )
        .distinct()
        .sorted()
        .toArray();
```

Decode:

```text
Two arrays
   ↓
concat

Duplicate remove
   ↓
distinct

Arrange
   ↓
sorted
```

---

# 53. Find Common Elements Between Two Arrays

🔥 Common interview question.

```java
int[] arr1 = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5};
int[] arr2 = {3, 4, 5, 6, 7};
```

Simple Java 8:

```java
List<Integer> second =
        Arrays.stream(arr2)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.toList());

List<Integer> common =
        Arrays.stream(arr1)
              .boxed()
              .filter(second::contains)
              .distinct()
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[3, 4, 5]
```

For larger arrays, a `Set` is better for lookup:

```java
Set<Integer> second =
        Arrays.stream(arr2)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.toSet());

List<Integer> common =
        Arrays.stream(arr1)
              .boxed()
              .filter(second::contains)
              .distinct()
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 54. Elements Present in First But Not Second

```java
Set<Integer> second =
        Arrays.stream(arr2)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.toSet());

List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr1)
              .boxed()
              .filter(n -> !second.contains(n))
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
Second array contains?
       ↓
NO
       ↓
keep it
```

---

# 55. Check Two Arrays Equal

If order matters:

```java
boolean equal =
        Arrays.equals(arr1, arr2);
```

Example:

```text
[1,2,3]
[1,2,3]

true
```

But:

```text
[1,2,3]
[3,2,1]

false
```

---

# 56. Check Same Elements Ignoring Order

If duplicates/counts matter:

```java
boolean same =
        Arrays.equals(
                Arrays.stream(arr1).sorted().toArray(),
                Arrays.stream(arr2).sorted().toArray()
        );
```

Example:

```text
[1,2,3]
[3,1,2]

sort both

[1,2,3]
[1,2,3]

true
```

---

# 57. Reverse an Array

Streams don't have a direct `reverse()` operation.

Using `IntStream` indexes:

```java
int[] result =
        IntStream.range(0, arr.length)
                 .map(i ->
                         arr[arr.length - 1 - i])
                 .toArray();
```

Example:

```text
10 20 30 40

↓

40 30 20 10
```

---

# 58. How Reverse Logic Works

For:

```text
arr.length = 4
```

Index:

```text
i = 0
```

we access:

```text
arr[4 - 1 - 0]
= arr[3]
```

Then:

```text
i = 1
→ arr[2]

i = 2
→ arr[1]

i = 3
→ arr[0]
```

So reverse.

---

# 59. Find Missing Number — 1 to N

🔥 Very common.

Suppose:

```java
int[] arr = {1, 2, 3, 5};
```

Expected numbers:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

Missing:

```text
4
```

Using sum formula:

```java
int n = 5;

int expected =
        n * (n + 1) / 2;

int actual =
        Arrays.stream(arr).sum();

int missing =
        expected - actual;
```

### Logic

```text
Expected Sum
     -
Actual Sum
     =
Missing Number
```

⚠️ Ye approach tab suitable hai jab expected range `1..n` ho aur exactly one number missing ho.

---

# 60. Find Missing Numbers From a Range

Suppose multiple numbers missing hain:

```java
int[] arr = {1, 2, 4, 6, 7};
```

Range:

```text
1 to 7
```

Expected missing:

```text
3, 5
```

Code:

```java
Set<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.toSet());

List<Integer> missing =
        IntStream.rangeClosed(1, 7)
                 .filter(n -> !numbers.contains(n))
                 .boxed()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
Generate expected range
       ↓
1 2 3 4 5 6 7

Check original contains?
       ↓
No
       ↓
Missing
```

---

# 61. Find Numbers Appearing More Than Once

Frequency approach:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same as duplicate program.

---

# 62. Find Number With Highest Frequency

```java
Map.Entry<Integer, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
              .orElse(null);
```

Example:

```text
1 2 2 3 3 3

↓

3 = 3 times
```

---

# 63. Find Number With Lowest Frequency

```java
Map.Entry<Integer, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
              .orElse(null);
```

If multiple numbers have same minimum frequency, this returns one of them; tie behavior depends on encounter/map iteration order.

---

# 64. Sort Elements By Frequency

Advanced:

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));

List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .sorted(
                  Comparator.comparingLong(
                      frequency::get)
                      .reversed()
              )
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Input:

```text
1 2 2 3 3 3
```

Output concept:

```text
3 3 3 2 2 1
```

---

# 65. Find Positive Numbers

```java
int[] positive =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n > 0)
              .toArray();
```

# 66. Find Negative Numbers

```java
int[] negative =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n < 0)
              .toArray();
```

---

# 67. Count Positive, Negative and Zero

```java
long positive =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n > 0)
              .count();

long negative =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n < 0)
              .count();

long zero =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n == 0)
              .count();
```

# 68. Find Prime Numbers

Question:

> Array me se prime numbers nikalo.

First prime logic:

```text
Prime:
number > 1

2 se sqrt(number) tak
kisi se divisible nahi
```

Helper:

```java
public static boolean isPrime(int n) {

    if (n <= 1) {
        return false;
    }

    return IntStream.rangeClosed(
                    2,
                    (int) Math.sqrt(n))
            .noneMatch(i -> n % i == 0);
}
```

Then:

```java
int[] primes =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(Main::isPrime)
              .toArray();
```

---

# 69. Why `noneMatch()` in Prime?

Suppose:

```text
n = 7
```

Check:

```text
2 divides 7? NO
```

No divisor found.

Meaning:

```text
None of the possible divisors divide n
```

Hence:

```java
.noneMatch(i -> n % i == 0)
```

---

# 70. Count Prime Numbers

```java
long count =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(Main::isPrime)
              .count();
```

Pattern:

```text
Prime?
 ↓
filter

How many?
 ↓
count
```

---

# 71. Sum of Prime Numbers

```java
int sum =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(Main::isPrime)
              .sum();
```

Same filter, terminal operation changed.

---

# 72. Find Numbers Starting With 1

🔥 Famous Java 8 interview question.

Input:

```java
int[] arr =
        {10, 15, 20, 25, 100, 145};
```

Expected:

```text
10
15
100
145
```

This is not mathematical comparison.

It's a **String property**.

So convert:

```text
Number
 ↓
String
 ↓
startsWith("1")
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .filter(n ->
                      String.valueOf(n)
                            .startsWith("1"))
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 73. Why Convert Number to String?

Requirement:

> Starts with `1`

`startsWith()` String concept hai.

So:

```text
Integer
  ↓
String.valueOf()
  ↓
String
  ↓
startsWith()
```

🔥 Requirement ke data type ko identify karna logic building ka important part hai.

---

# 74. Find Palindrome Numbers

Suppose:

```text
121
345
1331
```

Convert number to String:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .filter(n -> {
                  String s = String.valueOf(n);

                  return s.equals(
                          new StringBuilder(s)
                                  .reverse()
                                  .toString()
                  );
              })
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

```text
String
```

# 89. Remove Null Values From Object Array

Suppose:

```java
String[] names = {
        "Java",
        null,
        "Spring",
        null,
        "AWS"
};
```

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(names)
              .filter(Objects::nonNull)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Memory:

```text
Non-null only
    ↓
Objects::nonNull
```

---

# 90. Remove Null and Empty Strings

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(names)
              .filter(Objects::nonNull)
              .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Order important:

```text
First
↓
null remove

Then
↓
isEmpty()
```

Otherwise null par `isEmpty()` call karoge to NPE aa sakta hai.

# 91. 2D Array Flatten

🔥 Module 11 `flatMap` connection.

Suppose:

```java
int[][] arr = {
        {1, 2, 3},
        {4, 5, 6},
        {7, 8, 9}
};
```

Structure:

```text
[
 [1,2,3],
 [4,5,6],
 [7,8,9]
]
```

Flatten:

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
              .toArray();
```

Output:

```text
1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9
```

---

# 92. Why `flatMapToInt()`?

Outer stream:

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

where `arr` is `int[][]` gives elements of type:

```text
int[]
```

Each:

```java
Arrays::stream
```

returns:

```text
IntStream
```

So:

```text
Stream<int[]>
     ↓
flatMapToInt()
     ↓
IntStream
```

---

# 93. Find Even Numbers From 2D Array

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .toArray();
```

Pipeline:

```text
2D Array
   ↓
flatten
   ↓
filter even
```

---

# 94. Sum of 2D Array

```java
int sum =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
              .sum();
```

---

# 95. Maximum From 2D Array

```java
int max =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
              .max()
              .orElse(0);
```

---

# 96. Unique Sorted Elements From 2D Array

```java
int[] result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
              .distinct()
              .sorted()
              .toArray();
```

This combines:

```text
flatMap
+
distinct
+
sorting
```

# 97. Array Programs Me `map()` vs `mapToInt()`

Suppose:

```java
String[] names;
```

String → uppercase:

```java
.map(String::toUpperCase)
```

because:

```text
String → String
```

But:

```java
.mapToInt(String::length)
```

because:

```text
String → int
```

### Memory

```text
Object → Object
      ↓
map()


Object → int
      ↓
mapToInt()


Object → long
      ↓
mapToLong()


Object → double
      ↓
mapToDouble()
```

---

# 98. `int[]` vs `Integer[]`

Important interview/basic concept.

```java
int[]
```

Primitive array.

```java
Integer[]
```

Wrapper object array.

For:

```java
int[] arr;
```

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

returns:

```java
IntStream
```

For:

```java
Integer[] arr;
```

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

returns:

```java
Stream<Integer>
```

That's why sometimes `boxed()` chahiye and sometimes nahi.

---

# 99. When Do We Need `boxed()`?

Suppose:

```java
int[] arr;
```

Basic numerical operations:

```text
filter
map
sum
average
min
max
sorted
distinct
```

ke liye boxing ki zarurat nahi.

But agar chahiye:

```text
List<Integer>
Map<Integer, Long>
Comparator<Integer>
groupingBy()
Collectors
```

then often:

```java
.boxed()
```

use karte hain.

### Memory

```text
int[]
 ↓
IntStream

Need objects/Collectors?
 ↓
boxed()

Stream<Integer>
```

---

# 100. Array Master Decision Pattern

Question aaya:

> Find top 3 distinct even numbers in descending order.

Code mat socho.

Requirement todho:

```text
Array
 ↓
Arrays.stream()

Even
 ↓
filter()

Distinct
 ↓
distinct()

Descending
 ↓
Need Comparator
 ↓
boxed()
 ↓
sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 3
 ↓
limit(3)

List
 ↓
collect(toList)
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
              .distinct()
              .boxed()
              .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
              .limit(3)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 **Ye tumhara actual goal hai: requirement → operations identify karna.**

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. Primitive int array ko Stream me kaise convert karoge?

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

For `int[]`, ye `IntStream` return karta hai.

---

### Q2. `int[]` ke `Arrays.stream()` ka return type?

```java
IntStream
```

---

### Q3. `Integer[]` ka return type?

```java
Stream<Integer>
```

---

### Q4. `boxed()` kya karta hai?

Primitive stream ko wrapper object stream me convert karta hai.

```text
IntStream
   ↓
boxed()
   ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

---

### Q5. Duplicate elements kaise find karoge?

```text
Array
 ↓
boxed()
 ↓
groupingBy(identity)
 ↓
counting()
 ↓
count > 1
```

---

### Q6. `distinct()` aur unique elements me difference?

`distinct()` har different value ko ek baar rakhta hai.

Unique element wo hai jiski original frequency exactly `1` hai.

---

### Q7. Second distinct highest kaise find karoge?

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
      .boxed()
      .distinct()
      .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
      .skip(1)
      .findFirst();
```

---

### Q8. Array frequency ka common pattern?

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
      .boxed()
      .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
              Function.identity(),
              Collectors.counting()
      ));
```

---

### Q9. Two arrays merge kaise karoge?

Primitive arrays ke liye:

```java
IntStream.concat(
        Arrays.stream(arr1),
        Arrays.stream(arr2)
)
```

---

### Q10. 2D int array flatten kaise karoge?

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
      .flatMapToInt(Arrays::stream)
```

# ⭐ MODULE 16 — Master Cheat Sheet

```text
ARRAY QUESTION
     ↓
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

Then identify requirement:

| Requirement       | Think                                     |
| ----------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| Condition         | `filter()`                                |
| Transform         | `map()`                                   |
| Total             | `sum()`                                   |
| Average           | `average()`                               |
| Highest           | `max()`                                   |
| Lowest            | `min()`                                   |
| Count             | `count()`                                 |
| Remove duplicates | `distinct()`                              |
| Sort ascending    | `sorted()`                                |
| Sort descending   | `boxed()` + Comparator                    |
| Frequency         | `boxed()` + `groupingBy()` + `counting()` |
| Duplicate         | frequency `> 1`                           |
| Unique            | frequency `== 1`                          |
| First             | `findFirst()`                             |
| Second            | `skip(1)`                                 |
| Top N             | `limit(n)`                                |
| Exists?           | `anyMatch()`                              |
| All satisfy?      | `allMatch()`                              |
| None satisfy?     | `noneMatch()`                             |
| Two groups        | `partitioningBy()`                        |
| Merge arrays      | `IntStream.concat()`                      |
| 2D → 1D           | `flatMapToInt()`                          |
| Many → one        | `reduce()`                                |
| Statistics        | `summaryStatistics()`                     |

# ⭐ Array Logic Formula

Whenever array question comes:

```text
1. Input kya hai?
   ↓
   int[] / Integer[] / String[] / 2D array

2. Stream kaise milega?
   ↓
   Arrays.stream()

3. Selection hai?
   ↓
   filter()

4. Transformation hai?
   ↓
   map()

5. Duplicate issue hai?
   ↓
   distinct() OR groupingBy + counting()

6. Ordering hai?
   ↓
   sorted() / Comparator

7. Position hai?
   ↓
   skip() / limit() / findFirst()

8. Final answer kya chahiye?
   ↓
   sum / count / max / min / List / Array / Map
```

### Sabse important connection:

```text
"Duplicate hatao"
        ↓
distinct()

"Duplicate find karo"
        ↓
groupingBy + counting + count > 1

"Unique find karo"
        ↓
groupingBy + counting + count == 1
```

**Ye difference strong rakho. Array programs ka bahut bada portion isi pattern se solve ho jayega.**

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 17 : List Programs — Java 8

Ab **List Programs** karte hain. Array ke baad List aur easy lagega because:

```java
list.stream()
```

directly `Stream<T>` deta hai.

Main target wahi hai:

> **Program ratna nahi hai. Question dekhkar decide karna hai: `filter`, `map`, `distinct`, `sorted`, `groupingBy`, `max/min`, `skip/limit` etc.**

---

# 1. List Ka Basic Setup

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(10, 20, 30, 40, 50);
```

Stream:

```java
numbers.stream()
```

Flow:

```text
List<Integer>
      ↓
stream()
      ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

Array me:

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

List me:

```java
list.stream()
```

---

# 2. Print All Elements

```java
numbers.stream()
       .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 3. Count Elements

```java
long count =
        numbers.stream()
               .count();
```

Normal way:

```java
numbers.size();
```

---

# 4. Find Even Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
Even chahiye
    ↓
Condition
    ↓
filter()
```

---

# 5. Find Odd Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 6. Numbers Greater Than 50

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 50)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same pattern:

```text
Even       → filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
Odd        → filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
> 50       → filter(n -> n > 50)
< 50       → filter(n -> n < 50)
Positive   → filter(n -> n > 0)
Negative   → filter(n -> n < 0)
```

---

# 7. Find Positive Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 8. Find Negative Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n < 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 9. Sum of List

For `List<Integer>`:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

### Why `mapToInt()`?

Because:

```java
numbers.stream()
```

gives:

```text
Stream<Integer>
```

But `sum()` primitive stream `IntStream` me directly available hai.

So:

```text
Stream<Integer>
      ↓
mapToInt()
      ↓
IntStream
      ↓
sum()
```

---

# 10. Alternative Sum Using Reduce

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(0, Integer::sum);
```

or:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(0, (a, b) -> a + b);
```

---

# 11. Average of List

```java
double average =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .average()
               .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 12. Maximum Number

Two common approaches.

### Using `max() + Comparator`

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .max(Integer::compareTo)
               .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .max(Comparator.naturalOrder())
               .orElse(null);
```

### Using `mapToInt()`

```java
int max =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .max()
               .orElse(0);
```

---

# 13. Minimum Number

```java
Integer min =
        numbers.stream()
               .min(Comparator.naturalOrder())
               .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
int min =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .min()
               .orElse(0);
```

---

# 14. Sum of Even Numbers

Requirement:

```text
Even
 ↓
filter()

Sum
 ↓
mapToInt()
 ↓
sum()
```

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 15. Sum of Odd Numbers

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 16. Square Every Number

Input:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

Expected:

```text
1 4 9 16 25
```

Transformation hai:

```text
map()
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 17. Cube Every Number

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 18. Double Every Number

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * 2)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

### Golden Rule

```text
Element select/remove karna?
        ↓
filter()

Element change karna?
        ↓
map()
```

---

# 19. Square of Even Numbers

Question:

> Even numbers ke square chahiye.

Decode:

```text
Even
 ↓
filter()

Square
 ↓
map()
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 20. Sum of Squares of Even Numbers

Ab same question me final result `List` nahi, **sum** hai.

```text
Even
 ↓
filter

Square
 ↓
map

Sum
 ↓
mapToInt + sum
```

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

Alternative:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .mapToInt(n -> n * n)
               .sum();
```

Second version cleaner hai.

---

# 21. Sort Ascending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Or:

```java
.sorted(Comparator.naturalOrder())
```

---

# 22. Sort Descending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Array me `int[]` ke saath boxing problem thi.

List me:

```text
List<Integer>
   ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

already object stream hai.

Isliye direct:

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

use kar sakte ho.

---

# 23. Remove Duplicates

Input:

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5);
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
```

---

# 24. Find Frequency of Every Element

🔥 Important pattern.

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

Input:

```text
1 2 2 3 3 3
```

Result:

```text
1 → 1
2 → 2
3 → 3
```

---

# 25. `Function.identity()` Kya Hai?

```java
Function.identity()
```

same as:

```java
n -> n
```

So:

```java
groupingBy(Function.identity())
```

means:

> Number ko usi number ke basis par group karo.

Example:

```text
1 → group 1
2 → group 2
2 → group 2
3 → group 3
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.counting()
```

count karega.

---

# 26. Find Duplicate Elements

Question:

> List me duplicate numbers find karo.

Think:

```text
Har number kitni baar?
       ↓
groupingBy + counting

Duplicate?
       ↓
count > 1
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 27. Find Unique Elements

Unique means:

```text
Frequency exactly 1
```

```java
List<Integer> unique =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 28. Distinct vs Duplicate vs Unique

Input:

```text
[1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 4]
```

### Distinct

```java
distinct()
```

Result:

```text
[1, 2, 3, 4]
```

Meaning:

> Har different value ek baar.

### Duplicate

```text
frequency > 1
```

Result:

```text
[2, 4]
```

### Unique

```text
frequency == 1
```

Result:

```text
[1, 3]
```

🔥 Ye difference interview me strong rakho.

```java
Integer first =

               .orElse(null);
```

```text
Even
 ↓
filter

First
 ↓
findFirst

Not available?
 ↓
orElse
```

# 32. Second Highest Number

Input:

```text
[10, 50, 20, 80, 40]
```

Think:

```text
Highest first
 ↓
Descending

First nahi chahiye
 ↓
skip(1)

Next
 ↓
findFirst
```

Code:

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

Output:

```text
50
```

# 33. Second Distinct Highest

Suppose:

```text
[80, 80, 50, 40]
```

Second distinct highest:

```text
50
```

Code:

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

### Pattern

```text
Distinct
   ↓
Descending
   ↓
Skip Highest
   ↓
Next
```

```java
Integer result =
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
```

```text
Highest first
 ↓
descending

How many?
 ↓
limit(3)
```

# 39. Check Number Exists

Question:

> List me 50 exist karta hai?

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .anyMatch(n -> n == 50);
```

# 40. Check All Numbers Positive

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .allMatch(n -> n > 0);
```

---

# 41. Check No Negative Number

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .noneMatch(n -> n < 0);
```

Memory:

```text
Koi ek?
→ anyMatch()

Sab?
→ allMatch()

Ek bhi nahi?
→ noneMatch()
```

# 42. Partition Even and Odd

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0
               ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → even
false → odd
```

---

# 43. Count Even and Odd

```java
Map<Boolean, Long> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0,
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

---

# 44. Group Numbers By Even/Odd Name

```java
Map<String, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0
                               ? "EVEN"
                               : "ODD"
               ));
```

---

# 45. Group Numbers By Remainder `% 3`

```java
Map<Integer, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       n -> n % 3
               ));
```

Input:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6,7]
```

Result:

```text
0 → [3,6]
1 → [1,4,7]
2 → [2,5]
```

Why keys `0,1,2`?

Because:

```java
n -> n % 3
```

ye values return karta hai.

> ⭐ `groupingBy()` ke andar function jo return karega, wahi Map ki key banegi.

---

# 46. Product of All Numbers

```java
int product =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(1, (a, b) -> a * b);
```

Or:

```java
int product =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(1, Math::multiplyExact);
```

Simple interview answer me first wala easier hai.

---

# 47. Prime Numbers

Helper:

```java
public static boolean isPrime(int n) {

    if (n <= 1) {
        return false;
    }

    return IntStream.rangeClosed(
                    2,
                    (int) Math.sqrt(n))
            .noneMatch(i -> n % i == 0);
}
```

Then:

```java
List<Integer> primes =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 48. Count Prime Numbers

```java
long count =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .count();
```

---

# 49. Sum Prime Numbers

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

Notice same filter:

```java
.filter(Main::isPrime)
```

Final requirement badla:

```text
List chahiye  → collect()
Count chahiye → count()
Sum chahiye   → mapToInt().sum()
```

🔥 Ye bahut important logic-development point hai.

---

# 50. Numbers Starting With 1

Famous Java 8 question.

Input:

```text
[10, 15, 20, 125, 45, 100]
```

Expected:

```text
[10, 15, 125, 100]
```

"Starts with" String operation hai.

So:

```text
Integer
  ↓
String
  ↓
startsWith()
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n ->
                       String.valueOf(n)
                             .startsWith("1"))
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 51. Numbers Ending With 5

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n ->
                       String.valueOf(n)
                             .endsWith("5"))
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Alternative mathematical:

```java
.filter(n -> Math.abs(n) % 10 == 5)
```

---

# 52. Palindrome Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> {

                   String s = String.valueOf(n);

                   return s.equals(
                           new StringBuilder(s)
                                   .reverse()
                                   .toString());
               })
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 53. Number With Highest Frequency

Input:

```text
[1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3]
```

Expected:

```text
3
```

First frequency:

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

Then maximum value:

```java
Integer result =
        frequency.entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 54. Number With Lowest Frequency

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 55. Merge Two Lists

```java
List<Integer> list1 =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3);

List<Integer> list2 =
        Arrays.asList(4, 5, 6);
```

Merge:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6]
```

---

# 56. Merge Two Lists + Remove Duplicates

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .distinct()
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 57. Merge + Unique + Sorted

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .distinct()
        .sorted()
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Requirement decode:

```text
Two Lists
 ↓
Stream.concat()

Duplicates remove
 ↓
distinct()

Ascending
 ↓
sorted()
```

---

# 58. Common Elements Between Two Lists

Suppose:

```java
List<Integer> list1 =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3, 4, 5);

List<Integer> list2 =
        Arrays.asList(3, 4, 5, 6, 7);
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> common =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(list2::contains)
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[3,4,5]
```

For larger lists, better lookup:

```java
Set<Integer> set2 =
        new HashSet<>(list2);

List<Integer> common =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(set2::contains)
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 59. Elements Present in First But Not Second

```java
Set<Integer> set2 =
        new HashSet<>(list2);

List<Integer> result =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(n -> !set2.contains(n))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Mental model:

```text
Second list contains n?
       ↓
No
       ↓
Keep it
```

---

# 60. Common Elements Logic

Question:

> Common elements?

Think:

```text
Take List1 elements
       ↓
Does List2 contain element?
       ↓
Yes
       ↓
filter()
```

So:

```java
.filter(list2::contains)
```

Question:

> List1 me hain but List2 me nahi?

Condition reverse:

```java
.filter(n -> !list2.contains(n))
```

---

# 61. Check Two Lists Equal

If order matters:

```java
boolean equal =
        list1.equals(list2);
```

Example:

```text
[1,2,3]
[1,2,3]
→ true

[1,2,3]
[3,2,1]
→ false
```

---

# 62. Check Same Elements Ignoring Order

If duplicate counts also matter:

```java
boolean same =
        list1.size() == list2.size()
        &&
        list1.stream()
             .sorted()
             .collect(Collectors.toList())
             .equals(
                 list2.stream()
                      .sorted()
                      .collect(Collectors.toList())
             );
```

---

# 63. Convert List to Set

```java
Set<Integer> set =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.toSet());
```

This naturally removes duplicates.

---

# 64. Convert List to Array

```java
Integer[] arr =
        numbers.stream()
               .toArray(Integer[]::new);
```

---

# 65. Convert List<Integer> to int[]

```java
int[] arr =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .toArray();
```

---

# 66. Reverse List Using Stream

```java
List<Integer> result =
        IntStream.range(0, numbers.size())
                 .mapToObj(i ->
                         numbers.get(
                                 numbers.size() - 1 - i))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

But normal Java me:

```java
List<Integer> copy =
        new ArrayList<>(numbers);

Collections.reverse(copy);
```

simpler hai.

> Stream use karna possible hai iska matlab har jagah Stream use karna best hai, aisa nahi hai.

---

# 67. Skip First 3 Elements

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 68. Get First 3 Elements

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 69. Get Elements 4 to 6

Think:

```text
First 3 skip
     ↓
Next 3 take
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip(3)
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This pattern pagination me useful hai.

---

# 70. Pagination Pattern

Suppose:

```java
int pageNumber = 2;
int pageSize = 5;
```

Zero-based page number assume karte hue:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip((long) pageNumber * pageSize)
               .limit(pageSize)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Concept:

```text
skip()
+
limit()
=
pagination-style slicing
```

---

# String List Programs

Ab:

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList(
                "Java",
                "Spring",
                "AWS",
                "Docker",
                "Kafka"
        );
```

---

# 71. Find Names Starting With A

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.startsWith("A"))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Case-insensitive:

```java
.filter(name ->
        name.toLowerCase().startsWith("a"))
```

---

# 72. Find Strings Ending With "a"

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.endsWith("a"))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 73. Strings With Length > 5

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.length() > 5)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 74. Convert All Strings to Uppercase

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::toUpperCase)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 75. Convert All Strings to Lowercase

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::toLowerCase)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 76. Get Length of Every String

Input:

```text
Java Spring AWS
```

Expected:

```text
4 6 3
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::length)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Notice:

```text
String
  ↓
map()
  ↓
Integer
```

`map()` input/output same type hona compulsory nahi hai.

```java
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 80. Sort Strings By Length Descending

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .sorted(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                     String::length)
                     .reversed())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 81. Same Length Ho To Alphabetical

🔥 Comparator module connection.

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .sorted(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                         String::length)
                     .thenComparing(
                         Comparator.naturalOrder())
             )
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
First
 ↓
Length

Same length?
 ↓
Alphabetical
```

```java
String longest =
        names.stream()
             .max(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                     String::length))
             .orElse("");
```

```java
String shortest =
        names.stream()
             .min(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                     String::length))
             .orElse("");
```

# 84. Total Length of All Strings

```java
int total =
        names.stream()
             .mapToInt(String::length)
             .sum();
```

---

# 85. Average String Length

```java
double average =
        names.stream()
             .mapToInt(String::length)
             .average()
             .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 86. Remove Duplicate Strings

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 87. Duplicate Strings

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 88. Duplicate Strings Ignoring Case

Input:

```text
Java
JAVA
Spring
java
AWS
```

First normalize:

```java
.map(String::toLowerCase)
```

Then frequency:

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::toLowerCase)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
JAVA / Java / java
       ↓
Normalize
       ↓
java / java / java
       ↓
Frequency
```

---

# 89. Group Strings By Length

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>> result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     String::length
             ));
```

Remember:

```java
String::length
```

return karta hai:

```text
Integer
```

So Map key:

```text
Integer
```

Result type:

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>>
```

# 90. Count Strings By Length

```java
Map<Integer, Long> result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     String::length,
                     Collectors.counting()
             ));
```

```java
Map<Character, List<String>> result =
        names.stream()
```

Function returns:

```text
Character
```

Therefore key:

```text
Character
```

# 93. Remove Null Values

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList(
                "Java",
                null,
                "Spring",
                null,
                "AWS"
        );
```

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 94. Remove Null and Empty Values

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Why null filter first?

Because:

```java
null.isEmpty()
```

will cause:

```text
NullPointerException
```

---

# 95. Remove Null, Empty and Blank-Like Strings — Java 8

Java 8 me `String.isBlank()` nahi hai.

So:

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .filter(s -> !s.trim().isEmpty())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This removes:

```text
null
""
"   "
```

# Nested List Programs — FlatMap

Ab important:

```java
List<List<Integer>> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(
                Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3),
                Arrays.asList(4, 5, 6),
                Arrays.asList(7, 8, 9)
        );
```

Structure:

```text
[
 [1,2,3],
 [4,5,6],
 [7,8,9]
]
```

---

# 96. Flatten Nested List

Question:

> `List<List<Integer>>` ko `List<Integer>` banao.

Nested → Flat.

Think:

```text
flatMap()
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9]
```

---

# 97. `flatMap()` Ka Logic

Outer stream:

```text
[1,2,3]
[4,5,6]
[7,8,9]
```

Each List par:

```java
List::stream
```

Then:

```text
Stream<Integer>
Stream<Integer>
Stream<Integer>

        ↓ flatMap

Stream<Integer>
```

Final:

```text
1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9
```

---

# 98. Find Even Numbers From Nested List

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Decode:

```text
Nested
 ↓
flatMap

Even
 ↓
filter
```

---

# 99. Sum Nested List

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 100. Unique Sorted Values From Nested List

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .distinct()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This combines:

```text
FlatMap
+
Distinct
+
Sorting
+
Collectors
```

```text
 ↓
```

# 117. Top 3 Highest Paid Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 118. Second Highest Paid Employee

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Remember:

> Ye second employee after sorting hai, **second distinct salary** nahi.

---

# 119. Second Distinct Highest Salary

```java
Double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Decode:

```text
Employee
 ↓
Salary only
 ↓
map()

Unique salaries
 ↓
distinct()

Highest first
 ↓
sorted(reverse)

Highest skip
 ↓
skip(1)

Next
 ↓
findFirst()
```

---

# 120. Group Employees By Department

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =

                         Employee::getDepartment
                 ));
```

```text
Group kis basis par?
       ↓
Department
```

So:

```java
Employee::getDepartment
```

return value becomes Map key.

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
```

# 122. Department-Wise Employee Names

```java
Map<String, List<String>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getName,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
IT → [Amit, Rahul]

```

This combines:

```text
Grouping
+
Mapping
```

```java
Map<String, Double> result =

                                 Employee::getSalary)
```

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
```

# 125. Department-Wise Highest Paid Employee

🔥 Very important interview question.

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                         )
                 ));
```

Logic:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy

Highest
      ↓
maxBy

Compare based on?
      ↓
Salary
```

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
```

```text
true  → active
false → inactive
```

# 128. Active IT Employee Names Sorted By Salary Descending

🔥 Real scenario question.

Don't jump to code.

Decode:

```text
Active
 ↓
filter

IT
 ↓
filter

Salary descending
 ↓
sorted comparator

Names only
 ↓
map

List
 ↓
collect
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 129. Why Sort Before `map(Employee::getName)`?

Suppose tum pehle:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

kar dete ho.

Ab stream me:

```text
Employee
```

nahi hai.

Ab:

```text
String Name
```

hai.

Salary information lost.

So:

```text
Employee
 ↓
filter
 ↓
sort by salary
 ↓
map name
```

correct order.

🔥 Stream question solve karte waqt **current stream me abhi kaunsa object hai** ye hamesha track karo.

# 135. Convert Employee List To Map — ID → Employee

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Function.identity()
                 ));
```

Think:

```text
Key?
 ↓
Employee ID

Value?
 ↓
Employee itself
```

```java
Employee::getId
```

and:

```java
Function.identity()
```

# 136. Employee ID → Name Map

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getName
                 ));
```

Mental model:

```text
Map<Key, Value>

Key
 ↓
ID

Value
 ↓
Name
```

---

# 137. Employee Name → Salary Map

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

⚠️ If duplicate employee names exist, `toMap()` can throw an exception unless you provide a merge function.

Example:

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary,
                         (oldValue, newValue) -> newValue
                 ));
```

---

# 138. `toMap()` Mental Pattern

Whenever interviewer says:

> Convert List to Map.

Immediately ask:

```text
Map key kya hai?
Map value kya hai?
```

Example:

```text
ID → Name
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    Employee::getId,
    Employee::getName
)
```

Example:

```text
Name → Salary
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    Employee::getName,
    Employee::getSalary
)
```

Don't memorize code.

---

# 139. List Master Pattern — Requirement Decode

Suppose interviewer asks:

> Find top 3 unique highest salaries of active IT employees.

Break:

```text
Active
 ↓
filter()

IT
 ↓
filter()

Employee → Salary
 ↓
map()

Unique
 ↓
distinct()

Highest first
 ↓
sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 3
 ↓
limit(3)

List
 ↓
collect()
```

Code:

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This single question combines:

```text
Filter
+
Map
+
Distinct
+
Sorting
+
Comparator
+
Limit
+
Collectors
```

---

# 140. Another Logic-Building Scenario

Question:

> Department-wise active employee count find karo.

Decode:

```text
Only Active
 ↓
filter()

Department wise
 ↓
groupingBy()

Count
 ↓
counting()
```

Code:

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

# 141. Another Scenario

Question:

> Salary > 50000 employees ke unique departments sorted chahiye.

Decode:

```text
Salary > 50000
 ↓
filter

Only department
 ↓
map

Unique
 ↓
distinct

Sorted
 ↓
sorted

List
 ↓
collect
```

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 50000)
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 142. Another Scenario

Question:

> Highest paid employee ka name chahiye.

Important.

Direct Employee return nahi chahiye.

Think:

```text
Highest Employee
      ↓
max(salary comparator)

Then Employee → Name
      ↓
Optional.map()

Fallback
      ↓
orElse
```

```java
String name =
        employees.stream()
                 .max(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary))
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .orElse("");
```

🔥 Module `Optional + Comparator + Map` combine hua.

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. List ko stream me kaise convert karoge?

```java
list.stream()
```

---

### Q2. Array aur List streaming me basic difference?

Array:

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

List:

```java
list.stream()
```

---

### Q3. `List<Integer>` ka stream return type?

```text
Stream<Integer>
```

---

### Q4. List me even numbers kaise?

```java
list.stream()
    .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
```

---

### Q5. List duplicates remove kaise?

```java
distinct()
```

---

### Q6. Duplicate elements find kaise?

```text
groupingBy(identity)
+
counting()
+
count > 1
```

---

### Q7. Unique elements?

```text
frequency == 1
```

---

### Q8. Second distinct highest?

```java
list.stream()
    .distinct()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .skip(1)
    .findFirst();
```

---

### Q9. Two lists merge kaise?

```java
Stream.concat(
    list1.stream(),
    list2.stream()
)
```

---

### Q10. Nested List flatten kaise?

```java
list.stream()
    .flatMap(List::stream)
```

---

### Q11. Employee list department-wise group kaise?

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment
)
```

---

### Q12. Department-wise count?

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    Collectors.counting()
)
```

---

### Q13. List ko Map me convert kaise?

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    keyFunction,
    valueFunction
)
```

---

### Q14. Highest salary employee?

```java
employees.stream()
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
```

---

### Q15. `map()` aur `flatMap()` difference?

```text
map()
→ One element ko another value me transform


flatMap()
→ Nested structure ko flatten
```

Example:

```text
Employee → Name
→ map()

List<List<Integer>> → List<Integer>
→ flatMap()
```

---
