# 🧠 MODULE 17 Master Logic Map

Question me word identify karo:

| Requirement      | Java 8 Pattern                |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------- |
| Select           | `filter()`                    |
| Transform        | `map()`                       |
| Nested → Flat    | `flatMap()`                   |
| Remove duplicate | `distinct()`                  |
| Frequency        | `groupingBy()` + `counting()` |
| Duplicate        | frequency `> 1`               |
| Unique           | frequency `== 1`              |
| Sort             | `sorted()`                    |
| Custom sort      | `Comparator`                  |
| Highest          | `max()`                       |
| Lowest           | `min()`                       |
| Count            | `count()`                     |
| Sum              | `mapToInt().sum()`            |
| Group            | `groupingBy()`                |
| Two groups       | `partitioningBy()`            |
| First            | `findFirst()`                 |
| Skip N           | `skip(n)`                     |
| Top N            | `limit(n)`                    |
| Exists           | `anyMatch()`                  |
| All satisfy      | `allMatch()`                  |
| None satisfy     | `noneMatch()`                 |
| Combine values   | `reduce()`                    |
| Join Strings     | `joining()`                   |
| List → Map       | `toMap()`                     |
| Maybe result     | `Optional`                    |

# ⭐ Most Important Logic Development Rule

Har pipeline ke baad khud se pucho:

> **"Ab mere Stream ke andar kya hai?"**

Example:

```java
employees.stream()
```

Now:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.filter(Employee::isActive)
```

Still:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.map(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Now:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.distinct()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.sorted()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toList())
```

Finally:

```text
List<String>
```

So visualize:

```text
List<Employee>
      ↓ stream
Employee
      ↓ filter active
Employee
      ↓ map department
String
      ↓ distinct
String
      ↓ sorted
String
      ↓ collect
List<String>
```

**Agar tum har operation ke baad current data type/value mentally track karna seekh gaye, to naye Java 8 programs banana bahut easy ho jayega.**

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 17 : List Programs — Java 8

Ab **List Programs** karte hain. Array ke baad List aur easy lagega because:

```java
list.stream()
```

directly `Stream<T>` deta hai.

Main target wahi hai:

> **Program ratna nahi hai. Question dekhkar decide karna hai: `filter`, `map`, `distinct`, `sorted`, `groupingBy`, `max/min`, `skip/limit` etc.**

---

# 1. List Ka Basic Setup

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(10, 20, 30, 40, 50);
```

Stream:

```java
numbers.stream()
```

Flow:

```text
List<Integer>
      ↓
stream()
      ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

Array me:

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

List me:

```java
list.stream()
```

---

# 2. Print All Elements

```java
numbers.stream()
       .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 3. Count Elements

```java
long count =
        numbers.stream()
               .count();
```

Normal way:

```java
numbers.size();
```

---

# 4. Find Even Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
Even chahiye
    ↓
Condition
    ↓
filter()
```

---

# 5. Find Odd Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 6. Numbers Greater Than 50

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 50)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same pattern:

```text
Even       → filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
Odd        → filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
> 50       → filter(n -> n > 50)
< 50       → filter(n -> n < 50)
Positive   → filter(n -> n > 0)
Negative   → filter(n -> n < 0)
```

---

# 7. Find Positive Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 8. Find Negative Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n < 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 9. Sum of List

For `List<Integer>`:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

### Why `mapToInt()`?

Because:

```java
numbers.stream()
```

gives:

```text
Stream<Integer>
```

But `sum()` primitive stream `IntStream` me directly available hai.

So:

```text
Stream<Integer>
      ↓
mapToInt()
      ↓
IntStream
      ↓
sum()
```

---

# 10. Alternative Sum Using Reduce

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(0, Integer::sum);
```

or:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(0, (a, b) -> a + b);
```

---

# 11. Average of List

```java
double average =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .average()
               .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 12. Maximum Number

Two common approaches.

### Using `max() + Comparator`

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .max(Integer::compareTo)
               .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .max(Comparator.naturalOrder())
               .orElse(null);
```

### Using `mapToInt()`

```java
int max =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .max()
               .orElse(0);
```

---

# 13. Minimum Number

```java
Integer min =
        numbers.stream()
               .min(Comparator.naturalOrder())
               .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
int min =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .min()
               .orElse(0);
```

---

# 14. Sum of Even Numbers

Requirement:

```text
Even
 ↓
filter()

Sum
 ↓
mapToInt()
 ↓
sum()
```

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 15. Sum of Odd Numbers

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 16. Square Every Number

Input:

```text
1 2 3 4 5
```

Expected:

```text
1 4 9 16 25
```

Transformation hai:

```text
map()
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 17. Cube Every Number

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 18. Double Every Number

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * 2)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

### Golden Rule

```text
Element select/remove karna?
        ↓
filter()

Element change karna?
        ↓
map()
```

---

# 19. Square of Even Numbers

Question:

> Even numbers ke square chahiye.

Decode:

```text
Even
 ↓
filter()

Square
 ↓
map()
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 20. Sum of Squares of Even Numbers

Ab same question me final result `List` nahi, **sum** hai.

```text
Even
 ↓
filter

Square
 ↓
map

Sum
 ↓
mapToInt + sum
```

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

Alternative:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .mapToInt(n -> n * n)
               .sum();
```

Second version cleaner hai.

---

# 21. Sort Ascending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Or:

```java
.sorted(Comparator.naturalOrder())
```

---

# 22. Sort Descending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Array me `int[]` ke saath boxing problem thi.

List me:

```text
List<Integer>
   ↓
Stream<Integer>
```

already object stream hai.

Isliye direct:

```java
Comparator.reverseOrder()
```

use kar sakte ho.

---

# 23. Remove Duplicates

Input:

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5);
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
```

---

# 24. Find Frequency of Every Element

🔥 Important pattern.

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

Input:

```text
1 2 2 3 3 3
```

Result:

```text
1 → 1
2 → 2
3 → 3
```

---

# 25. `Function.identity()` Kya Hai?

```java
Function.identity()
```

same as:

```java
n -> n
```

So:

```java
groupingBy(Function.identity())
```

means:

> Number ko usi number ke basis par group karo.

Example:

```text
1 → group 1
2 → group 2
2 → group 2
3 → group 3
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.counting()
```

count karega.

---

# 26. Find Duplicate Elements

Question:

> List me duplicate numbers find karo.

Think:

```text
Har number kitni baar?
       ↓
groupingBy + counting

Duplicate?
       ↓
count > 1
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 27. Find Unique Elements

Unique means:

```text
Frequency exactly 1
```

```java
List<Integer> unique =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 28. Distinct vs Duplicate vs Unique

Input:

```text
[1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 4]
```

### Distinct

```java
distinct()
```

Result:

```text
[1, 2, 3, 4]
```

Meaning:

> Har different value ek baar.

### Duplicate

```text
frequency > 1
```

Result:

```text
[2, 4]
```

### Unique

```text
frequency == 1
```

Result:

```text
[1, 3]
```

🔥 Ye difference interview me strong rakho.

```java
Integer first =
        numbers.stream()
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

# 30. First Even Number

```java
Integer firstEven =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

Logic:

```text
Even
 ↓
filter

First
 ↓
findFirst

Not available?
 ↓
orElse
```

# 31. First Number Greater Than 50

```java
               .orElse(null);
```

# 32. Second Highest Number

Input:

```text
[10, 50, 20, 80, 40]
```

Think:

```text
Highest first
 ↓
Descending

First nahi chahiye
 ↓
skip(1)

Next
 ↓
findFirst
```

Code:

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

Output:

```text
50
```

# 33. Second Distinct Highest

Suppose:

```text
[80, 80, 50, 40]
```

Second distinct highest:

```text
50
```

Code:

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

### Pattern

```text
Distinct
   ↓
Descending
   ↓
Skip Highest
   ↓
Next
```

```java
Integer result =
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
```

```text
Highest first
 ↓
descending

How many?
 ↓
limit(3)
```

# 39. Check Number Exists

Question:

> List me 50 exist karta hai?

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .anyMatch(n -> n == 50);
```

---

# 40. Check All Numbers Positive

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .allMatch(n -> n > 0);
```

---

# 41. Check No Negative Number

```java
boolean result =
        numbers.stream()
               .noneMatch(n -> n < 0);
```

Memory:

```text
Koi ek?
→ anyMatch()

Sab?
→ allMatch()

Ek bhi nahi?
→ noneMatch()
```

---

# 42. Partition Even and Odd

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0
               ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → even
false → odd
```

---

# 43. Count Even and Odd

```java
Map<Boolean, Long> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0,
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

---

# 44. Group Numbers By Even/Odd Name

```java
Map<String, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       n -> n % 2 == 0
                               ? "EVEN"
                               : "ODD"
               ));
```

---

# 45. Group Numbers By Remainder `% 3`

```java
Map<Integer, List<Integer>> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       n -> n % 3
               ));
```

Input:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6,7]
```

Result:

```text
0 → [3,6]
1 → [1,4,7]
2 → [2,5]
```

Why keys `0,1,2`?

Because:

```java
n -> n % 3
```

ye values return karta hai.

> ⭐ `groupingBy()` ke andar function jo return karega, wahi Map ki key banegi.

---

# 46. Product of All Numbers

```java
int product =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(1, (a, b) -> a * b);
```

Or:

```java
int product =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(1, Math::multiplyExact);
```

Simple interview answer me first wala easier hai.

---

# 47. Prime Numbers

Helper:

```java
public static boolean isPrime(int n) {

    if (n <= 1) {
        return false;
    }

    return IntStream.rangeClosed(
                    2,
                    (int) Math.sqrt(n))
            .noneMatch(i -> n % i == 0);
}
```

Then:

```java
List<Integer> primes =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 48. Count Prime Numbers

```java
long count =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .count();
```

---

# 49. Sum Prime Numbers

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(Main::isPrime)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

Notice same filter:

```java
.filter(Main::isPrime)
```

Final requirement badla:

```text
List chahiye  → collect()
Count chahiye → count()
Sum chahiye   → mapToInt().sum()
```

🔥 Ye bahut important logic-development point hai.

---

# 50. Numbers Starting With 1

Famous Java 8 question.

Input:

```text
[10, 15, 20, 125, 45, 100]
```

Expected:

```text
[10, 15, 125, 100]
```

"Starts with" String operation hai.

So:

```text
Integer
  ↓
String
  ↓
startsWith()
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n ->
                       String.valueOf(n)
                             .startsWith("1"))
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 51. Numbers Ending With 5

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n ->
                       String.valueOf(n)
                             .endsWith("5"))
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Alternative mathematical:

```java
.filter(n -> Math.abs(n) % 10 == 5)
```

---

# 52. Palindrome Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> {

                   String s = String.valueOf(n);

                   return s.equals(
                           new StringBuilder(s)
                                   .reverse()
                                   .toString());
               })
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 53. Number With Highest Frequency

Input:

```text
[1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3]
```

Expected:

```text
3
```

First frequency:

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

Then maximum value:

```java
Integer result =
        frequency.entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 54. Number With Lowest Frequency

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 55. Merge Two Lists

```java
List<Integer> list1 =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3);

List<Integer> list2 =
        Arrays.asList(4, 5, 6);
```

Merge:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6]
```

---

# 56. Merge Two Lists + Remove Duplicates

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .distinct()
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 57. Merge + Unique + Sorted

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .distinct()
        .sorted()
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Requirement decode:

```text
Two Lists
 ↓
Stream.concat()

Duplicates remove
 ↓
distinct()

Ascending
 ↓
sorted()
```

---

# 58. Common Elements Between Two Lists

Suppose:

```java
List<Integer> list1 =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3, 4, 5);

List<Integer> list2 =
        Arrays.asList(3, 4, 5, 6, 7);
```

Code:

```java
List<Integer> common =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(list2::contains)
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[3,4,5]
```

For larger lists, better lookup:

```java
Set<Integer> set2 =
        new HashSet<>(list2);

List<Integer> common =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(set2::contains)
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 59. Elements Present in First But Not Second

```java
Set<Integer> set2 =
        new HashSet<>(list2);

List<Integer> result =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(n -> !set2.contains(n))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Mental model:

```text
Second list contains n?
       ↓
No
       ↓
Keep it
```

---

# 60. Common Elements Logic

Question:

> Common elements?

Think:

```text
Take List1 elements
       ↓
Does List2 contain element?
       ↓
Yes
       ↓
filter()
```

So:

```java
.filter(list2::contains)
```

Question:

> List1 me hain but List2 me nahi?

Condition reverse:

```java
.filter(n -> !list2.contains(n))
```

---

# 61. Check Two Lists Equal

If order matters:

```java
boolean equal =
        list1.equals(list2);
```

Example:

```text
[1,2,3]
[1,2,3]
→ true

[1,2,3]
[3,2,1]
→ false
```

---

# 62. Check Same Elements Ignoring Order

If duplicate counts also matter:

```java
boolean same =
        list1.size() == list2.size()
        &&
        list1.stream()
             .sorted()
             .collect(Collectors.toList())
             .equals(
                 list2.stream()
                      .sorted()
                      .collect(Collectors.toList())
             );
```

---

# 63. Convert List to Set

```java
Set<Integer> set =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.toSet());
```

This naturally removes duplicates.

---

# 64. Convert List to Array

```java
Integer[] arr =
        numbers.stream()
               .toArray(Integer[]::new);
```

---

# 65. Convert List<Integer> to int[]

```java
int[] arr =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .toArray();
```

---

# 66. Reverse List Using Stream

```java
List<Integer> result =
        IntStream.range(0, numbers.size())
                 .mapToObj(i ->
                         numbers.get(
                                 numbers.size() - 1 - i))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

But normal Java me:

```java
List<Integer> copy =
        new ArrayList<>(numbers);

Collections.reverse(copy);
```

simpler hai.

> Stream use karna possible hai iska matlab har jagah Stream use karna best hai, aisa nahi hai.

---

# 67. Skip First 3 Elements

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 68. Get First 3 Elements

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 69. Get Elements 4 to 6

Think:

```text
First 3 skip
     ↓
Next 3 take
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip(3)
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This pattern pagination me useful hai.

---

# 70. Pagination Pattern

Suppose:

```java
int pageNumber = 2;
int pageSize = 5;
```

Zero-based page number assume karte hue:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .skip((long) pageNumber * pageSize)
               .limit(pageSize)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Concept:

```text
skip()
+
limit()
=
pagination-style slicing
```

# String List Programs

Ab:

```java
List<String> names =

                "Java",
                "Spring",
                "AWS",
                "Docker",
                "Kafka"
        );
```

---

# 71. Find Names Starting With A

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.startsWith("A"))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Case-insensitive:

```java
.filter(name ->
        name.toLowerCase().startsWith("a"))
```

---

# 72. Find Strings Ending With "a"

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.endsWith("a"))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 73. Strings With Length > 5

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(name ->
                     name.length() > 5)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

```java
List<String> result =

             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 75. Convert All Strings to Lowercase

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()

             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 76. Get Length of Every String

```text
Java Spring AWS
```

Expected:

```text
4 6 3
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::length)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Notice:

```text
String
  ↓
map()
  ↓
Integer
```

`map()` input/output same type hona compulsory nahi hai.

# 79. Sort Strings By Length

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .sorted(

             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 80. Sort Strings By Length Descending

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .sorted(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                     String::length)
                     .reversed())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 81. Same Length Ho To Alphabetical

🔥 Comparator module connection.

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .sorted(
                 Comparator.comparingInt(
                         String::length)
                     .thenComparing(
                         Comparator.naturalOrder())
             )
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
First
 ↓
Length

Same length?
 ↓
Alphabetical
```

```java
String longest =

        names.stream()
```

```java
int total =
        names.stream()
             .mapToInt(String::length)
             .sum();
```

```java
double average =
        names.stream()
             .mapToInt(String::length)
             .average()
             .orElse(0.0);
```

# 86. Remove Duplicate Strings

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 87. Duplicate Strings

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 88. Duplicate Strings Ignoring Case

Input:

```text
Java
JAVA
Spring
java
AWS
```

First normalize:

```java
.map(String::toLowerCase)
```

Then frequency:

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        names.stream()
             .map(String::toLowerCase)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
JAVA / Java / java
       ↓
Normalize
       ↓
java / java / java
       ↓
Frequency
```

---

# 89. Group Strings By Length

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>> result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     String::length
             ));
```

Remember:

```java
String::length
```

return karta hai:

```text
Integer
```

So Map key:

```text
Integer
```

Result type:

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>>
```

# 90. Count Strings By Length

```java
Map<Integer, Long> result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     String::length,
                     Collectors.counting()
             ));
```

# 91. Group Strings By First Character

```java
Map<Character, List<String>> result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     name -> name.charAt(0)
             ));
```

Function returns:

```text
Character
```

Therefore key:

```text
Character
```

# 92. Join All Strings

```java
String result =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
```

Output:

```text
Java, Spring, AWS, Docker, Kafka
```

---

# 93. Remove Null Values

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList(
                "Java",
                null,
                "Spring",
                null,
                "AWS"
        );
```

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 94. Remove Null and Empty Values

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Why null filter first?

Because:

```java
null.isEmpty()
```

will cause:

```text
NullPointerException
```

---

# 95. Remove Null, Empty and Blank-Like Strings — Java 8

Java 8 me `String.isBlank()` nahi hai.

So:

```java
List<String> result =
        names.stream()
             .filter(Objects::nonNull)
             .filter(s -> !s.trim().isEmpty())
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This removes:

```text
null
""
"   "
```

---

# Nested List Programs — FlatMap

Ab important:

```java
List<List<Integer>> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(
                Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3),
                Arrays.asList(4, 5, 6),
                Arrays.asList(7, 8, 9)
        );
```

Structure:

```text
[
 [1,2,3],
 [4,5,6],
 [7,8,9]
]
```

---

# 96. Flatten Nested List

Question:

> `List<List<Integer>>` ko `List<Integer>` banao.

Nested → Flat.

Think:

```text
flatMap()
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Output:

```text
[1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9]
```

---

# 97. `flatMap()` Ka Logic

Outer stream:

```text
[1,2,3]
[4,5,6]
[7,8,9]
```

Each List par:

```java
List::stream
```

Then:

```text
Stream<Integer>
Stream<Integer>
Stream<Integer>

        ↓ flatMap

Stream<Integer>
```

Final:

```text
1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9
```

---

# 98. Find Even Numbers From Nested List

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Decode:

```text
Nested
 ↓
flatMap

Even
 ↓
filter
```

---

# 99. Sum Nested List

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

---

# 100. Unique Sorted Values From Nested List

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .distinct()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This combines:

```text
FlatMap
+
Distinct
+
Sorting
+
Collectors
```

# Employee List Programs

Ab real interview scenarios ke liye:

```java
class Employee {

    private int id;
    private String name;
    private String department;
    private double salary;
    private int age;
    private boolean active;

    // getters/setters
}
```

Suppose:

```java
List<Employee> employees;
```

---

# 101. Find IT Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 102. Find Active Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 104. Employees Salary > 50000

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()

                         e.getSalary() > 50000)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 117. Top 3 Highest Paid Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 118. Second Highest Paid Employee

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Remember:

> Ye second employee after sorting hai, **second distinct salary** nahi.

---

# 119. Second Distinct Highest Salary

```java
Double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Decode:

```text
Employee
 ↓
Salary only
 ↓
map()

Unique salaries
 ↓
distinct()

Highest first
 ↓
sorted(reverse)

Highest skip
 ↓
skip(1)

Next
 ↓
findFirst()
```

---

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =

                         Employee::getDepartment
                 ));
```

```text
Group kis basis par?
       ↓
Department
```

So:

```java
Employee::getDepartment
```

return value becomes Map key.

```java
Map<String, Long> result =

```

# 122. Department-Wise Employee Names

```java
Map<String, List<String>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getName,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
IT → [Amit, Rahul]
HR → [Sneha, Raj]
```

This combines:

```text
Grouping
+
Mapping
```

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
```

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
```

# 125. Department-Wise Highest Paid Employee

🔥 Very important interview question.

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                         )
                 ));
```

Logic:

```text
Department wise
      ↓
groupingBy

Highest
      ↓
maxBy

Compare based on?
      ↓
Salary
```

# 126. Active vs Inactive Employees

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()

                         Employee::isActive
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → active
false → inactive
```

```java
                         Employee::isActive,
```

# 128. Active IT Employee Names Sorted By Salary Descending

🔥 Real scenario question.

Don't jump to code.

Decode:

```text
Active
 ↓
filter

IT
 ↓
filter

Salary descending
 ↓
sorted comparator

Names only
 ↓
map

List
 ↓
collect
```

Code:

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed())
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 129. Why Sort Before `map(Employee::getName)`?

Suppose tum pehle:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

kar dete ho.

Ab stream me:

```text
Employee
```

nahi hai.

Ab:

```text
String Name
```

hai.

Salary information lost.

So:

```text
Employee
 ↓
filter
 ↓
sort by salary
 ↓
map name
```

correct order.

🔥 Stream question solve karte waqt **current stream me abhi kaunsa object hai** ye hamesha track karo.

---

```java
List<List<Employee>> teams;
```

```java
List<Employee> allEmployees =
        teams.stream()
             .flatMap(List::stream)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```


---

# 134. Employee Names as Comma-Separated String

```java
String result =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
```

Output:

```text
Amit, Rahul, Sneha, Vishnu
```

---

# 135. Convert Employee List To Map — ID → Employee

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Function.identity()
                 ));
```

Think:

```text
Key?
 ↓
Employee ID

Value?
 ↓
Employee itself
```

So:

```java
Employee::getId
```

and:

```java
Function.identity()
```

---

# 136. Employee ID → Name Map

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getName
                 ));
```

Mental model:

```text
Map<Key, Value>

Key
 ↓
ID

Value
 ↓
Name
```

---

# 137. Employee Name → Salary Map

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

⚠️ If duplicate employee names exist, `toMap()` can throw an exception unless you provide a merge function.

Example:

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary,
                         (oldValue, newValue) -> newValue
                 ));
```

---

# 138. `toMap()` Mental Pattern

Whenever interviewer says:

> Convert List to Map.

Immediately ask:

```text
Map key kya hai?
Map value kya hai?
```

Example:

```text
ID → Name
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    Employee::getId,
    Employee::getName
)
```

Example:

```text
Name → Salary
```

Then:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    Employee::getName,
    Employee::getSalary
)
```

Don't memorize code.

---

# 139. List Master Pattern — Requirement Decode

Suppose interviewer asks:

> Find top 3 unique highest salaries of active IT employees.

Break:

```text
Active
 ↓
filter()

IT
 ↓
filter()

Employee → Salary
 ↓
map()

Unique
 ↓
distinct()

Highest first
 ↓
sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 3
 ↓
limit(3)

List
 ↓
collect()
```

Code:

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(
                             e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This single question combines:

```text
Filter
+
Map
+
Distinct
+
Sorting
+
Comparator
+
Limit
+
Collectors
```

---

# 140. Another Logic-Building Scenario

Question:

> Department-wise active employee count find karo.

Don't think code.

Decode:

```text
Only Active
 ↓
filter()

Department wise
 ↓
groupingBy()

Count
 ↓
counting()
```

Code:

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 141. Another Scenario

Question:

> Salary > 50000 employees ke unique departments sorted chahiye.

Decode:

```text
Salary > 50000
 ↓
filter

Only department
 ↓
map

Unique
 ↓
distinct

Sorted
 ↓
sorted

List
 ↓
collect
```

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 50000)
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 142. Another Scenario

Question:

> Highest paid employee ka name chahiye.

Important.

Direct Employee return nahi chahiye.

Think:

```text
Highest Employee
      ↓
max(salary comparator)

Then Employee → Name
      ↓
Optional.map()

Fallback
      ↓
orElse
```

```java
String name =
        employees.stream()
                 .max(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary))
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .orElse("");
```

🔥 Module `Optional + Comparator + Map` combine hua.

# Interview Questions

### Q1. List ko stream me kaise convert karoge?

```java
list.stream()
```

---

### Q2. Array aur List streaming me basic difference?

Array:

```java
Arrays.stream(arr)
```

List:

```java
list.stream()
```

---

### Q3. `List<Integer>` ka stream return type?

```text
Stream<Integer>
```

---

### Q4. List me even numbers kaise?

```java
list.stream()
    .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
```

---

### Q5. List duplicates remove kaise?

```java
distinct()
```

---

### Q6. Duplicate elements find kaise?

```text
groupingBy(identity)
+
counting()
+
count > 1
```

---

### Q7. Unique elements?

```text
frequency == 1
```

---

### Q8. Second distinct highest?

```java
list.stream()
    .distinct()
    .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
    .skip(1)
    .findFirst();
```

---

### Q9. Two lists merge kaise?

```java
Stream.concat(
    list1.stream(),
    list2.stream()
)
```

---

### Q10. Nested List flatten kaise?

```java
list.stream()
    .flatMap(List::stream)
```

---

### Q11. Employee list department-wise group kaise?

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment
)
```

---

### Q12. Department-wise count?

```java
Collectors.groupingBy(
    Employee::getDepartment,
    Collectors.counting()
)
```

---

### Q13. List ko Map me convert kaise?

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    keyFunction,
    valueFunction
)
```

---

### Q14. Highest salary employee?

```java
employees.stream()
         .max(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary))
```

---

### Q15. `map()` aur `flatMap()` difference?

```text
map()
→ One element ko another value me transform


flatMap()
→ Nested structure ko flatten
```

Example:

```text
Employee → Name
→ map()

List<List<Integer>> → List<Integer>
→ flatMap()
```

---

# 🧠 MODULE 17 Master Logic Map

Question me word identify karo:

| Requirement      | Java 8 Pattern                |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------- |
| Select           | `filter()`                    |
| Transform        | `map()`                       |
| Nested → Flat    | `flatMap()`                   |
| Remove duplicate | `distinct()`                  |
| Frequency        | `groupingBy()` + `counting()` |
| Duplicate        | frequency `> 1`               |
| Unique           | frequency `== 1`              |
| Sort             | `sorted()`                    |
| Custom sort      | `Comparator`                  |
| Highest          | `max()`                       |
| Lowest           | `min()`                       |
| Count            | `count()`                     |
| Sum              | `mapToInt().sum()`            |
| Group            | `groupingBy()`                |
| Two groups       | `partitioningBy()`            |
| First            | `findFirst()`                 |
| Skip N           | `skip(n)`                     |
| Top N            | `limit(n)`                    |
| Exists           | `anyMatch()`                  |
| All satisfy      | `allMatch()`                  |
| None satisfy     | `noneMatch()`                 |
| Combine values   | `reduce()`                    |
| Join Strings     | `joining()`                   |
| List → Map       | `toMap()`                     |
| Maybe result     | `Optional`                    |

# ⭐ Most Important Logic Development Rule

Har pipeline ke baad khud se pucho:

> **"Ab mere Stream ke andar kya hai?"**

Example:

```java
employees.stream()
```

Now:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.filter(Employee::isActive)
```

Still:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.map(Employee::getDepartment)
```

Now:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.distinct()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.sorted()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toList())
```

Finally:

```text
List<String>
```

So visualize:

```text
List<Employee>
      ↓ stream
Employee
      ↓ filter active
Employee
      ↓ map department
String
      ↓ distinct
String
      ↓ sorted
String
      ↓ collect
List<String>
```

**Agar tum har operation ke baad current data type/value mentally track karna seekh gaye, to naye Java 8 programs banana bahut easy ho jayega.**

=============================================================================================================================
# MODULE 18 : Map Programs — Java 8

Ab **Map Programs** important hain because real projects aur interviews me bahut baar data:

```java
Map<K, V>
```

form me hota hai.

Example:

```java
Map<String, Integer> students = new HashMap<>();

students.put("Amit", 80);
students.put("Rahul", 65);
students.put("Sneha", 90);
students.put("Vishnu", 75);
```

Data mentally:

```text
Key       Value
--------------
Amit       80
Rahul      65
Sneha      90
Vishnu     75
```

## ⭐ Sabse Pehle Map Ka Master Concept

List me:

```java
list.stream()
```

Map me direct:

```java
map.stream()   // ❌
```

nahi hota.

Map ke paas 3 main views hain:

```java
map.keySet()      // only keys
map.values()      // only values
map.entrySet()    // key + value
```

Isliye:

```text
Only Key chahiye?
→ map.keySet().stream()

Only Value chahiye?
→ map.values().stream()

Key + Value dono chahiye?
→ map.entrySet().stream()
```

Ye **Map programs ka sabse important decision** hai.

---

# 1. Print All Keys

```java
students.keySet()
        .stream()
        .forEach(System.out::println);
```

Output:

```text
Amit
Rahul
Sneha
Vishnu
```

---

# 2. Print All Values

```java
students.values()
        .stream()
        .forEach(System.out::println);
```

---

# 3. Print Key and Value

```java
students.entrySet()
        .stream()
        .forEach(e ->
                System.out.println(
                        e.getKey() + " = " + e.getValue()
                ));
```

### Remember

```text
Map.Entry
   ↓
getKey()
getValue()
```

---

# 4. `entrySet()` Kya Return Karta Hai?

For:

```java
Map<String, Integer> students;
```

this:

```java
students.entrySet()
```

returns conceptually:

```text
Set<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>
```

Then:

```java
students.entrySet().stream()
```

returns:

```text
Stream<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>
```

So stream ke andar ab:

```text
Entry
```

hai.

Not String.

Not Integer.

---

# 5. Filter Map By Value

Question:

> Marks greater than 70 wale students find karo.

Requirement:

```text
Key + Value dono chahiye
       ↓
entrySet()

Condition on Value
       ↓
filter()
```

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue
                ));
```

---

# 6. Filter Map By Key

Question:

> Name `"A"` se start hone wale students.

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e ->
                        e.getKey().startsWith("A"))
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue
                ));
```

---

# 7. Get Only Names With Marks > 70

Final answer Map nahi, names ki List hai.

Think:

```text
Need marks also for condition
      ↓
entrySet()

marks > 70
      ↓
filter()

Only names
      ↓
map(getKey)

List
      ↓
collect()
```

```java
List<String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 Important:

```text
Entry
 ↓ map(getKey)
String
```

---

# 8. Get Marks Greater Than 70 Only

```java
List<Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
                .map(Map.Entry::getValue)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 9. Count Entries

Simple:

```java
int count = students.size();
```

Stream:

```java
long count =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .count();
```

Usually `size()` better hai.

---

# 10. Count Students Marks > 70

```java
long count =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
                .count();
```

---

# 11. Sum All Values

```java
int total =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
                .sum();
```

Think:

```text
Only marks needed
      ↓
values()

Need sum
      ↓
mapToInt()
      ↓
sum()
```

---

# 12. Average of Values

```java
double average =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
                .average()
                .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 13. Maximum Value

```java
Integer max =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .max(Integer::compareTo)
                .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
int max =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
                .max()
                .orElse(0);
```

---

# 14. Minimum Value

```java
Integer min =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .min(Integer::compareTo)
                .orElse(null);
```

---

# 15. Find Entry With Maximum Value

Question:

> Highest marks kis student ke hain?

Ab sirf max marks nahi chahiye.

Student + marks dono chahiye.

So:

```text
entrySet()
```

Code:

```java
Map.Entry<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                .orElse(null);
```

Example:

```text
Sneha = 90
```

---

# 16. Highest Marks Student Name Only

```java
String name =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .orElse(null);
```

Pipeline:

```text
Entry
 ↓
max by value
 ↓
Optional<Entry>
 ↓
map(getKey)
 ↓
Optional<String>
 ↓
orElse
 ↓
String
```

---

# 17. Lowest Value Entry

```java
Map.Entry<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                .orElse(null);
```

---

# 18. Lowest Marks Student Name

```java
String name =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .orElse(null);
```

---

# 19. Sort Map By Key Ascending

Important: `HashMap` order maintain nahi karta.

So sorted result ko:

```java
LinkedHashMap
```

me collect karenge.

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(Map.Entry.comparingByKey())
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        (oldValue, newValue) -> oldValue,
                        LinkedHashMap::new
                ));
```

---

# 20. Why `LinkedHashMap`?

Agar:

```java
.sorted(...)
```

ke baad normal:

```java
Collectors.toMap(...)
```

use kiya, default result generally `HashMap` ho sakta hai.

HashMap sorted encounter order preserve karne ki guarantee nahi deta.

So:

```text
sorted stream
     ↓
LinkedHashMap
     ↓
order preserved
```

---

# 21. Sort Map By Key Descending

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByKey()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        (a, b) -> a,
                        LinkedHashMap::new
                ));
```

---

# 22. Sort Map By Value Ascending

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        (a, b) -> a,
                        LinkedHashMap::new
                ));
```

---

# 23. Sort Map By Value Descending

🔥 Very common interview question.

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        (a, b) -> a,
                        LinkedHashMap::new
                ));
```

Mental pattern:

```text
Need key + value
     ↓
entrySet

Sort based on value
     ↓
comparingByValue

Descending
     ↓
reversed

Preserve sorted order
     ↓
LinkedHashMap
```

---

# 24. Top 3 Entries By Value

```java
List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .limit(3)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Notice final output `List<Entry>` rakha hai.

Har problem me Map banana necessary nahi.

---

# 25. Top 3 Keys By Value

Question:

> Top 3 marks wale student names.

```java
List<String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .limit(3)
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 26. Second Highest Value

Only value chahiye:

```java
Integer result =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                .skip(1)
                .findFirst()
                .orElse(null);
```

---

# 27. Second Distinct Highest Value

```java
Integer result =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .distinct()
                .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                .skip(1)
                .findFirst()
                .orElse(null);
```

---

# 28. Second Highest Entry

```java
Map.Entry<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .skip(1)
                .findFirst()
                .orElse(null);
```

But careful:

```text
A = 90
B = 90
C = 80
```

Ye second entry:

```text
B = 90
```

de sakta hai.

Agar interviewer bole:

> Second **distinct** highest marks wala student

to alag logic lagega.

---

# 29. Check Key Exists

Normal Map method best:

```java
boolean result =
        students.containsKey("Amit");
```

Stream ki zarurat nahi.

---

# 30. Check Value Exists

```java
boolean result =
        students.containsValue(90);
```

Again Stream unnecessary.

---

# 31. Stream Using AnyMatch

Possible:

```java
boolean result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .anyMatch(e ->
                        e.getValue() > 90);
```

Useful jab simple exact `containsValue()` nahi, condition ho.

---

# 32. Check All Values Greater Than 50

```java
boolean result =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .allMatch(mark -> mark > 50);
```

---

# 33. Check No Value Is Negative

```java
boolean result =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .noneMatch(mark -> mark < 0);
```

Memory:

```text
At least one?
→ anyMatch

All?
→ allMatch

None?
→ noneMatch
```

---

# 34. Convert Map Keys To List

```java
List<String> keys =
        students.keySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Simpler:

```java
List<String> keys =
        new ArrayList<>(students.keySet());
```

---

# 35. Convert Map Values To List

```java
List<Integer> values =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 36. Convert Map Entries To List

```java
List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>> entries =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 37. Transform Map Values

Suppose:

```text
Amit = 80
Rahul = 65
```

10 marks bonus:

```text
Amit = 90
Rahul = 75
```

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        e -> e.getValue() + 10
                ));
```

Think:

```text
Key same
Value changed
```

So:

```java
key   → getKey()
value → getValue() + 10
```

---

# 38. Transform Map Keys

Question:

> All names uppercase karo.

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        e -> e.getKey().toUpperCase(),
                        Map.Entry::getValue
                ));
```

---

# 39. Transform Both Key and Value

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        e -> e.getKey().toUpperCase(),
                        e -> e.getValue() + 5
                ));
```

Mental model:

```text
Old Entry
   ↓
new key function
new value function
   ↓
New Map
```

---

# 40. Merge Two Maps

```java
Map<String, Integer> map1 = new HashMap<>();
Map<String, Integer> map2 = new HashMap<>();
```

Using streams:

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                map1.entrySet().stream(),
                map2.entrySet().stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                Map.Entry::getKey,
                Map.Entry::getValue,
                (value1, value2) -> value2
        ));
```

Why merge function?

Because same key dono maps me ho sakti hai.

---

# 41. Merge Maps — Keep First Value

```java
(value1, value2) -> value1
```

Complete:

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                map1.entrySet().stream(),
                map2.entrySet().stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                Map.Entry::getKey,
                Map.Entry::getValue,
                (value1, value2) -> value1
        ));
```

---

# 42. Merge Maps — Keep Second Value

```java
(value1, value2) -> value2
```

---

# 43. Merge Maps — Sum Duplicate Values

Suppose:

```text
Map1:
Java → 10
AWS  → 20

Map2:
Java → 5
Kafka → 30
```

Expected:

```text
Java  → 15
AWS   → 20
Kafka → 30
```

Code:

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                map1.entrySet().stream(),
                map2.entrySet().stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                Map.Entry::getKey,
                Map.Entry::getValue,
                Integer::sum
        ));
```

🔥 `toMap()` merge function important hai.

---

# 44. `toMap()` Master Pattern

```java
Collectors.toMap(
        keyFunction,
        valueFunction,
        mergeFunction,
        mapSupplier
)
```

Meaning:

```text
1. Key kya hoga?
2. Value kya hoga?
3. Same key aaye to kya karna?
4. Kaunsa Map implementation banana?
```

Example:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    Map.Entry::getKey,
    Map.Entry::getValue,
    (a, b) -> a,
    LinkedHashMap::new
)
```

---

# 45. Frequency Map From List

Suppose:

```java
List<String> names =
        Arrays.asList(
                "Java",
                "Spring",
                "Java",
                "AWS",
                "Java",
                "AWS"
        );
```

Frequency:

```java
Map<String, Long> frequency =
        names.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ));
```

Result:

```text
Java   → 3
Spring → 1
AWS    → 2
```

Ye already Map program ban gaya.

---

# 46. Find Most Frequent Key From Frequency Map

```java
String result =
        frequency.entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 47. Find Least Frequent Key

```java
String result =
        frequency.entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 48. Find Keys Whose Frequency > 1

```java
List<String> duplicates =
        frequency.entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Notice:

```text
List
 ↓ grouping
Map
 ↓ entrySet stream
filter
 ↓
List
```

Streams List aur Map ke beech move kar sakte hain.

---

# 49. Partition Map Entries Based On Value

Question:

> Students ko pass/fail me divide karo. Passing marks = 40.

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                        e -> e.getValue() >= 40
                ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → passed entries
false → failed entries
```

---

# 50. Group Map Entries By Value Range

Suppose marks:

```text
>= 75 → DISTINCTION
>= 60 → FIRST
>= 40 → PASS
else  → FAIL
```

```java
Map<String, List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(e -> {

                    int mark = e.getValue();

                    if (mark >= 75)
                        return "DISTINCTION";

                    if (mark >= 60)
                        return "FIRST";

                    if (mark >= 40)
                        return "PASS";

                    return "FAIL";
                }));
```

This is important logic.

`groupingBy()` ke andar:

```java
e -> ...
```

jo String return karega:

```text
DISTINCTION
FIRST
PASS
FAIL
```

wahi Map key banegi.

---

# 51. Count Entries By Value Range

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                        e -> e.getValue() >= 40
                                ? "PASS"
                                : "FAIL",
                        Collectors.counting()
                ));
```

---

```java
IntSummaryStatistics stats =
        students.values()

                .summaryStatistics();
```

Now:

```java
stats.getCount();
stats.getSum();
stats.getMin();
stats.getMax();
stats.getAverage();
```

# 54. Find IT Employees From Map

> ID ki zarurat nahi thi.

```java
values().stream()
```

enough hai.

# 55. Find IDs of IT Employees

Ab ID bhi chahiye.

So:

```text
Key + Employee
     ↓
entrySet()
```

```java
List<Integer> ids =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .filter(e ->
                           "IT".equals(
                               e.getValue()
                                .getDepartment()))
                   .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                   .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 56. Highest Paid Employee

ID ki need nahi hai:

```java
Employee employee =
        employeeMap.values()
                   .stream()
                   .max(
                       Comparator.comparingDouble(
                           Employee::getSalary))
                   .orElse(null);
```

---

# 57. ID of Highest Paid Employee

Now key bhi chahiye.

```java
Integer id =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .max(
                       Map.Entry.comparingByValue(
                           Comparator.comparingDouble(
                               Employee::getSalary)
                       )
                   )
                   .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                   .orElse(null);
```

Ye thoda advanced hai.

Simple readable version:

```java
Integer id =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .max(
                       Comparator.comparingDouble(
                           e -> e.getValue()
                                 .getSalary())
                   )
                   .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                   .orElse(null);
```

Interview me second wala easier explain hoga.

---

# 58. Sort Employee Map By Salary

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .sorted(
                       Comparator.comparingDouble(
                           e -> e.getValue()
                                 .getSalary())
                   )
                   .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                           Map.Entry::getKey,
                           Map.Entry::getValue,
                           (a, b) -> a,
                           LinkedHashMap::new
                   ));
```

---

# 59. Sort Employee Map Salary Descending

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .sorted(
                       Comparator.<Map.Entry<Integer, Employee>>
                               comparingDouble(
                                   e -> e.getValue()
                                         .getSalary())
                               .reversed()
                   )
                   .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                           Map.Entry::getKey,
                           Map.Entry::getValue,
                           (a, b) -> a,
                           LinkedHashMap::new
                   ));
```

```text
        ↓
filter

Back to Map
        ↓
toMap
```

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()

                           e.getValue().isActive())
```

# 73. Nested Map

Suppose:

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> departmentEmployees;
```

Structure:

```text
IT
 ↓
[Employee1, Employee2]

HR
 ↓
[Employee3, Employee4]
```

---

# 74. Get All Employees From Nested Map

Only values:

```text
Collection<List<Employee>>
```

Then flatten:

```java
List<Employee> result =
        departmentEmployees.values()
                           .stream()
                           .flatMap(List::stream)
                           .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Flow:

```text
Map<String, List<Employee>>
          ↓
values()
          ↓
Collection<List<Employee>>
          ↓
stream()
          ↓
Stream<List<Employee>>
          ↓
flatMap()
          ↓
Stream<Employee>
```

---

# 75. Find All Active Employees From Nested Map

```java
List<Employee> result =
        departmentEmployees.values()
                           .stream()
                           .flatMap(List::stream)
                           .filter(Employee::isActive)
                           .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 76. Highest Paid Employee From Nested Map

```java
Employee result =
        departmentEmployees.values()
                           .stream()
                           .flatMap(List::stream)
                           .max(
                               Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                   Employee::getSalary))
                           .orElse(null);
```

---

# 77. Unique Employee Names From Nested Map

```java
List<String> result =
        departmentEmployees.values()
                           .stream()
                           .flatMap(List::stream)
                           .map(Employee::getName)
                           .distinct()
                           .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

This combines:

```text
Map
+
FlatMap
+
Map
+
Distinct
+
Collectors
```

# 80. Map<String, List<Integer>> → Sum Each List

# 85. Find Duplicate Values Across Map Lists

First flatten.

Then frequency.

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        marks.values()
             .stream()
             .flatMap(List::stream)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Don't memorize full code.

Decode:

```text
Nested values
    ↓
flatMap

Duplicates?
    ↓
frequency

frequency
    ↓
groupingBy + counting

duplicate
    ↓
count > 1
```

---

# 86. Invert Map — Key Becomes Value

Suppose:

```text
A → 10
B → 20
C → 30
```

Need:

```text
10 → A
20 → B
30 → C
```

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        Map.Entry::getKey
                ));
```

But important:

If original values duplicate:

```text
A → 10
B → 10
```

then inverted Map:

```text
10 → ?
```

conflict hoga.

So merge function required.

---

# 87. Invert Map With Duplicate Values

```java
Map<Integer, String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        (name1, name2) -> name1
                ));
```

But isme ek name lose ho jayega.

Better:

```text
Value → List<Keys>
```

# 88. Group Keys By Same Value

```java
Map<Integer, List<String>> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        Collectors.mapping(
                                Map.Entry::getKey,
                                Collectors.toList()
                        )
                ));
```

Input:

```text
Amit  → 80
Rahul → 80
Sneha → 90
```

Result:

```text
80 → [Amit, Rahul]
90 → [Sneha]
```

🔥 Great `groupingBy + mapping` question.

```java
Map<Integer, Long> result =

                        Map.Entry::getValue,
                        Collectors.counting()
                ));

```

# 90. Find Duplicate Values in Map

Duplicate value means:

```text
value frequency > 1
```

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                        Function.identity(),
                        Collectors.counting()
                ))
                .entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same duplicate pattern again!

```text
List duplicate
Array duplicate
String duplicate
Map value duplicate

All:
groupingBy + counting + count > 1
```

# 94. Remove Entries Where String Key Is Empty

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getKey() != null)
                .filter(e -> !e.getKey().trim().isEmpty())
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        Map.Entry::getValue
                ));
```

Java 8 me:

```java
isBlank()
```

nahi hai.

Use:

```java
trim().isEmpty()
```

# 95. Find First Entry Matching Condition

```java
Map.Entry<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 80)
                .findFirst()
                .orElse(null);
```

Important:

If `students` is a `HashMap`, "first" insertion order wala first guaranteed nahi hai.

If predictable insertion order matters, use `LinkedHashMap`.

# 96. `forEach()` Directly On Map

Har baar Stream necessary nahi.

Map ka direct `forEach`:

```java
students.forEach((key, value) ->
        System.out.println(key + " = " + value)
);
```

Ye:

```java
students.entrySet()
        .stream()
        .forEach(...)
```

se simpler hai for basic iteration.

# 97. `getOrDefault()`

```java
        students.getOrDefault(
                "Unknown",
                0
        );
```


otherwise:


Useful in frequency/counting logic too.
