# 98. `putIfAbsent()`

```java
students.putIfAbsent("Amit", 50);
```

Meaning:

```text
Amit already exists?
    ↓ YES
Don't replace

Doesn't exist?
    ↓
Insert
```

Java 8 Map useful method.

---

# 99. `computeIfAbsent()`

🔥 Real project useful.

Suppose:

```java
Map<String, List<String>> departmentEmployees =
        new HashMap<>();
```

Add employee:

```java
departmentEmployees
        .computeIfAbsent(
                "IT",
                key -> new ArrayList<>()
        )
        .add("Amit");
```

Logic:

```text
IT key exists?
   ↓
Yes → existing List

No
   ↓
Create new ArrayList

Then
   ↓
add employee
```

---

# 100. `computeIfAbsent()` Common Pattern

Without it:

```java
if (!map.containsKey(key)) {
    map.put(key, new ArrayList<>());
}

map.get(key).add(value);
```

With Java 8:

```java
map.computeIfAbsent(
        key,
        k -> new ArrayList<>()
).add(value);
```

🔥 This is actually useful Java 8 Map knowledge beyond Streams.

---

# 101. `computeIfPresent()`

```java
students.computeIfPresent(
        "Amit",
        (key, value) -> value + 5
);
```

Meaning:

```text
Amit exists?
   ↓ YES
Update value

Doesn't exist?
   ↓
Do nothing
```

---

# 102. `compute()`

```java
students.compute(
        "Amit",
        (key, value) ->
                value == null
                        ? 0
                        : value + 5
);
```

Can handle both existing/non-existing key.

---

# 103. `merge()` — Very Important

Frequency counting example:

```java
Map<String, Integer> frequency =
        new HashMap<>();

frequency.merge(
        "Java",
        1,
        Integer::sum
);
```

First Java:

```text
Java doesn't exist
→ Java = 1
```

Second:

```java
frequency.merge(
        "Java",
        1,
        Integer::sum
);
```

Now:

```text
existing = 1
new      = 1

sum
 ↓
Java = 2
```

---

# 104. Frequency Using Map.merge()

```java
List<String> words =
        Arrays.asList(
                "Java",
                "Spring",
                "Java",
                "AWS",
                "Java"
        );

Map<String, Integer> frequency =
        new HashMap<>();

words.forEach(word ->
        frequency.merge(
                word,
                1,
                Integer::sum
        )
);
```

Result:

```text
Java   → 3
Spring → 1
AWS    → 1
```

Compare with Stream:

```java
Map<String, Long> frequency =
        words.stream()
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ));
```

Both valid.

---

# 105. `replaceAll()`

Suppose sab marks me +5:

```java
students.replaceAll(
        (name, mark) -> mark + 5
);
```

This modifies existing Map.

Stream approach generally new Map create karta:

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                        Map.Entry::getKey,
                        e -> e.getValue() + 5
                ));
```

Difference:

```text
replaceAll()
→ original Map modify

stream + collect
→ new Map
```

---

# 106. Remove Entries Using `removeIf()`

```java
students.entrySet()
        .removeIf(
                e -> e.getValue() < 40
        );
```

This modifies original Map.

Meaning:

> Failed students remove.

Again:

```text
Stream filter
→ usually new result

removeIf
→ original collection modify
```

---

# 🔥 Map Scenario Questions

## Scenario 1

Question:

> Marks > 70 students ke names descending marks order me chahiye.

Decode:

```text
Need key + marks
      ↓
entrySet

marks > 70
      ↓
filter

highest marks first
      ↓
sorted by value reversed

names only
      ↓
map key

List
      ↓
collect
```

```java
List<String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# Scenario 2

> Top 3 students by marks.

```java
List<String> result =
        students.entrySet()
                .stream()
                .sorted(
                    Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                            comparingByValue()
                            .reversed()
                )
                .limit(3)
                .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# Scenario 3

> Passed students ka average marks.

Decode:

```text
Only marks eventually needed
       ↓
values()

Pass
       ↓
filter >= 40

Average
       ↓
mapToInt
average
```

```java
double average =
        students.values()
                .stream()
                .filter(mark -> mark >= 40)
                .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
                .average()
                .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# Scenario 4

> Active IT employees me highest salary wale employee ka name.

Suppose:

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> employeeMap;
```

ID needed nahi.

```text
Employee objects
      ↓
values()

Active
      ↓
filter

IT
      ↓
filter

Highest salary
      ↓
max Comparator

Employee → Name
      ↓
Optional.map
```

```java
String name =
        employeeMap.values()
                   .stream()
                   .filter(Employee::isActive)
                   .filter(e ->
                           "IT".equals(
                               e.getDepartment()))
                   .max(
                       Comparator.comparingDouble(
                           Employee::getSalary))
                   .map(Employee::getName)
                   .orElse(null);
```

---

# Scenario 5

> Active IT employee IDs sorted by salary descending.

Now ID required.

Therefore:

```text
entrySet()
```

```java
List<Integer> ids =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .filter(e ->
                           e.getValue().isActive())
                   .filter(e ->
                           "IT".equals(
                               e.getValue()
                                .getDepartment()))
                   .sorted(
                       Comparator.<Map.Entry<Integer, Employee>>
                               comparingDouble(
                                   e -> e.getValue()
                                         .getSalary())
                               .reversed()
                   )
                   .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                   .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# ⭐ Most Important Map Decision

Question dekhte hi sabse pehle decide karo:

```text
What information do I need?
```

### Only Key

```java
map.keySet().stream()
```

### Only Value

```java
map.values().stream()
```

### Key + Value

```java
map.entrySet().stream()
```

Example:

```text
"All employee names"
→ values()

"Highest salary"
→ values()

"Employee ID of highest salary"
→ entrySet()

"Print ID + Employee"
→ entrySet()

"All IDs"
→ keySet()
```

---

# 🧠 Map Stream Type Tracking

Suppose:

```java
Map<String, Integer> students;
```

Start:

```java
students.entrySet().stream()
```

Current stream:

```text
Stream<Map.Entry<String,Integer>>
```

Then:

```java
.filter(e -> e.getValue() > 70)
```

Still:

```text
Stream<Map.Entry<String,Integer>>
```

Then:

```java
.map(Map.Entry::getKey)
```

Now:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.sorted()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toList())
```

Finally:

```text
List<String>
```

Visual:

```text
Map<String,Integer>
       ↓
entrySet()
       ↓
Entry<String,Integer>
       ↓ filter
Entry<String,Integer>
       ↓ map(getKey)
String
       ↓ sorted
String
       ↓ collect
List<String>
```

🔥 Har operation ke baad ye pucho:

> **"Ab stream ke andar kya hai?"**

---

# Interview Questions

### Q1. Map par directly `stream()` laga sakte hain?

No.

```java
map.stream(); // ❌
```

Use:

```java
map.keySet().stream();
map.values().stream();
map.entrySet().stream();
```

---

### Q2. Key aur value dono chahiye to?

```java
map.entrySet().stream()
```

---

### Q3. Only values?

```java
map.values().stream()
```

---

### Q4. Only keys?

```java
map.keySet().stream()
```

---

### Q5. Map.Entry se key/value kaise?

```java
entry.getKey();
entry.getValue();
```

---

### Q6. Sort Map by value?

```text
entrySet
↓
sorted(comparingByValue)
↓
LinkedHashMap
```

---

### Q7. Why LinkedHashMap after sorting?

Because `LinkedHashMap` insertion/encounter order preserve karta hai. Plain `HashMap` sorted order guarantee nahi karta.

---

### Q8. Maximum value ki key kaise find karoge?

```java
map.entrySet()
   .stream()
   .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
   .map(Map.Entry::getKey);
```

---

### Q9. `toMap()` me duplicate key aaye to?

Without merge function exception aa sakti hai.

Use:

```java
Collectors.toMap(
    keyFunction,
    valueFunction,
    (oldValue, newValue) -> newValue
)
```

---

### Q10. `groupingBy()` aur `toMap()` difference?

Simple memory:

```text
groupingBy()
→ Same key/group ke multiple elements collect karne hain.

toMap()
→ Har element se key-value pair banana hai.
```

---

### Q11. `computeIfAbsent()` kab?

Key absent ho to value initialize/create karne ke liye.

Common:

```java
map.computeIfAbsent(
    key,
    k -> new ArrayList<>()
).add(value);
```

---

### Q12. `merge()` kab?

Existing aur new value combine karne ke liye.

```java
map.merge(key, 1, Integer::sum);
```

Frequency counting me useful.

---

# ⭐ MODULE 18 — Master Cheat Sheet

| Question says          | Think                          |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------ |
| Only keys              | `keySet()`                     |
| Only values            | `values()`                     |
| Key + value            | `entrySet()`                   |
| Select entries         | `filter()`                     |
| Transform              | `map()` / `toMap()`            |
| Sort key               | `comparingByKey()`             |
| Sort value             | `comparingByValue()`           |
| Preserve sorted Map    | `LinkedHashMap`                |
| Highest                | `max()`                        |
| Lowest                 | `min()`                        |
| Top N                  | `sorted + limit()`             |
| Second                 | `sorted + skip(1)`             |
| Exists                 | `anyMatch()` / `containsKey()` |
| Group                  | `groupingBy()`                 |
| Two groups             | `partitioningBy()`             |
| Count groups           | `counting()`                   |
| Merge Maps             | `Stream.concat()` + `toMap()`  |
| Duplicate key handling | merge function                 |
| Nested values          | `flatMap()`                    |
| Key absent initialize  | `computeIfAbsent()`            |
| Existing value update  | `computeIfPresent()`           |
| Combine values         | `merge()`                      |
| Modify all values      | `replaceAll()`                 |

# 🧠 Map Logic Formula

Jab bhi Map ka naya program aaye, ye **5 questions** pucho:

```text
1. Mujhe KEY chahiye, VALUE chahiye,
   ya DONO?
             ↓
 keySet / values / entrySet


2. Koi condition hai?
             ↓
          filter()


3. Output change karna hai?
             ↓
            map()


4. Sorting / grouping / highest?
             ↓
 sorted / groupingBy / max


5. Final result kya hai?
             ↓
 List / Map / count / sum / single value
```

Example interviewer bolta hai:

> **"Salary 50,000 se jyada wale top 3 employees ke IDs chahiye."**

Tum code immediately mat socho.

```text
ID + Employee dono initially chahiye
              ↓
          entrySet()

Salary > 50000
              ↓
           filter()

Top employees
              ↓
      salary descending

Only 3
              ↓
          limit(3)

Finally ID only
              ↓
         map(getKey)

Result
              ↓
           List
```

Then code naturally banega:

```java
List<Integer> ids =
        employeeMap.entrySet()
                   .stream()
                   .filter(e ->
                           e.getValue().getSalary() > 50000)
                   .sorted(
                       Comparator.<Map.Entry<Integer, Employee>>
                               comparingDouble(
                                   e -> e.getValue()
                                         .getSalary())
                               .reversed()
                   )
                   .limit(3)
                   .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                   .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

**Agar ye requirement → operation conversion tum karne lag gaye, tab tum programs rat nahi rahe hoge; tum actual Java 8 Stream logic build kar rahe hoge.**

# MODULE 19: Employee Programs — Most Important Java 8 Interview Module

Ye module **bahut important** hai because Java 8 interview me `Employee` object ke upar questions se ek saath ye sab test hote hain:

```text
filter
map
sorted
Comparator
max / min
distinct
groupingBy
partitioningBy
Collectors
Optional
counting
summing
averaging
mapping
```

Is module ka target:

> **Question dekhkar pipeline khud banana.**

---

# 1. Employee Class

Hum poore module me ye class use karenge:

```java
class Employee {

    private int id;
    private String name;
    private String department;
    private double salary;
    private int age;
    private String gender;
    private boolean active;

    public Employee(int id,
                    String name,
                    String department,
                    double salary,
                    int age,
                    String gender,
                    boolean active) {

        this.id = id;
        this.name = name;
        this.department = department;
        this.salary = salary;
        this.age = age;
        this.gender = gender;
        this.active = active;
    }

    public int getId() {
        return id;
    }

    public String getName() {
        return name;
    }

    public String getDepartment() {
        return department;
    }

    public double getSalary() {
        return salary;
    }

    public int getAge() {
        return age;
    }

    public String getGender() {
        return gender;
    }

    public boolean isActive() {
        return active;
    }
}
```

Sample data:

```java
List<Employee> employees = Arrays.asList(

    new Employee(
        101, "Amit", "IT",
        70000, 28, "Male", true),

    new Employee(
        102, "Sneha", "HR",
        50000, 32, "Female", true),

    new Employee(
        103, "Rahul", "IT",
        90000, 35, "Male", true),

    new Employee(
        104, "Priya", "Finance",
        65000, 30, "Female", false),

    new Employee(
        105, "Vijay", "IT",
        90000, 40, "Male", true),

    new Employee(
        106, "Neha", "HR",
        55000, 27, "Female", true),

    new Employee(
        107, "Karan", "Finance",
        80000, 38, "Male", false)
);
```

---

# 2. Employee Question Ka Master Pattern

Question padhte hi keywords nikalo.

Example:

> Active IT employees ke names salary descending order me chahiye.

Break:

```text
Active
  ↓
filter()

IT
  ↓
filter()

Salary descending
  ↓
sorted() + Comparator

Employee → Name
  ↓
map()

List
  ↓
collect()
```

Code:

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

**Pehle requirement break karo, baad me code.**

---

# PART 1 — FILTER EMPLOYEE PROGRAMS

# 3. Find All IT Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Logic:

```text
Condition?
→ Department = IT

Therefore
→ filter()
```

---

# 4. Find HR Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "HR".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 5. Find Employees Salary > 60000

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 60000)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 6. Find Employees Age > 30

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getAge() > 30)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 7. Find Active Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same as:

```java
.filter(e -> e.isActive())
```

---

# 8. Find Inactive Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e -> !e.isActive())
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 9. Find Female Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "Female".equals(e.getGender()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 10. Find Male IT Employees

Two conditions:

```text
Male
+
IT
```

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "Male".equals(e.getGender()))
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Could also:

```java
.filter(e ->
        "Male".equals(e.getGender())
        &&
        "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
```

Both correct.

---

# 11. Active IT Employees Salary > 60000

Break:

```text
Active
  ↓ filter

IT
  ↓ filter

Salary > 60000
  ↓ filter
```

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 60000)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# PART 2 — MAP / TRANSFORMATION

# 12. Get All Employee Names

Input:

```text
Employee
```

Output:

```text
String
```

Transformation = `map()`.

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Flow:

```text
Stream<Employee>
      ↓ map
Stream<String>
```

---

# 13. Get All Salaries

```java
List<Double> salaries =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 14. Get All Departments

```java
List<String> departments =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 15. Get Unique Departments

```java
List<String> departments =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .distinct()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Pattern:

```text
Employee → Department
        ↓
       map

Remove duplicates
        ↓
    distinct
```

---

# 16. Get Unique Departments Sorted

```java
List<String> departments =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getDepartment)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted()
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 17. Get Names of IT Employees

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Remember:

```text
Select Employee
→ filter

Employee → Name
→ map
```

---

# 18. Active Employee Names

```java
List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# PART 3 — SORTING EMPLOYEES

# 19. Sort Employees By Salary Ascending

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 20. Sort Employees By Salary Descending

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Memory:

```text
Object sorting
     ↓
Comparator.comparing...

Descending
     ↓
reversed()
```

---

# 21. Sort Employees By Age

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingInt(
                         Employee::getAge)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 22. Sort Employees By Age Descending

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingInt(
                         Employee::getAge)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 23. Sort Employees By Name

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparing(
                         Employee::getName)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 24. Sort By Department

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()

                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 25. Sort Department Then Salary

Question:

> First department alphabetical, same department me salary ascending.

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparing(
                             Employee::getDepartment)
                         .thenComparingDouble(
                             Employee::getSalary)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Mental model:

```text
First condition
→ comparing()

Tie?
→ thenComparing()
```

---

# 26. Department Ascending + Salary Descending

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparing(
                             Employee::getDepartment)
                         .thenComparing(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                                 .reversed()
                         )
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 31. Highest Paid Employee Name

This is important.

First:

```text
Employee stream
    ↓
max salary
    ↓
Optional<Employee>
```

Then:

```text
Employee → Name
```

Use Optional `map()`:

```java
String name =
        employees.stream()
                 .max(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                 )
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 32. Highest Salary Only

```java
Double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .max(Double::compareTo)
                 .orElse(null);
```

Or:

```java
double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .mapToDouble(Employee::getSalary)
                 .max()
                 .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 33. Highest Paid IT Employee

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))

                         Employee::getSalary)
                 )
                 .orElse(null);
```

Decode:

```text
IT
↓
filter

Highest
↓
max

Based on salary
↓
Comparator
```

# PART 5 — SECOND HIGHEST / TOP N

# 35. Second Highest Paid Employee

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Pattern:

```text
Highest first
↓
sort descending

Highest nahi chahiye
↓
skip(1)

Next
↓
findFirst
```

⚠️ Ye **second employee** hai, second distinct salary nahi.

---

# 36. Second Distinct Highest Salary

Sample salaries:

```text
90000
90000
80000
70000
```

Second distinct highest:

```text
80000
```

Code:

```java
Double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 37. Third Highest Distinct Salary

```java
Double salary =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .skip(2)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

General:

```text
Nth highest
→ skip(n - 1)
```

after sorting.

---

# 38. Top 3 Highest Paid Employees

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 39. Top 3 Highest Salaries

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 40. Top 3 Distinct Salaries

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 41. Top 3 Active IT Employees By Salary

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .sorted(

                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 52. Employee Salary Statistics

```java
                 .mapToDouble(Employee::getSalary)
                 .summaryStatistics();
```

```java
stats.getCount();
stats.getSum();
stats.getMin();
stats.getMax();
stats.getAverage();
```

One operation se:

```text
Count
Sum
Min
Max
Average
```

mil gaya.

# PART 8 — GROUPING BY

🔥 **Employee questions ka sabse important part.**

# 53. Group Employees By Department

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment
                 ));
```

Think:

```text
Group kis basis par?
→ Department

getDepartment() returns?
→ String

Therefore key?
→ String
```

Result:

```text
IT      → [Amit, Rahul, Vijay]
HR      → [Sneha, Neha]
Finance → [Priya, Karan]
```

---

# 54. Group Employees By Gender

```java
Map<String, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getGender
                 ));
```

---

# 55. Group Employees By Active Status

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::isActive
                 ));
```

Though two groups ke liye `partitioningBy()` semantically better hai.

---

# 56. Count Employees By Department

🔥 Very common.

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
IT      → 3
HR      → 2
Finance → 2
```

Pattern:

```text
Department-wise
       ↓
groupingBy

Count
       ↓
counting()
```

---

# 57. Count Employees By Gender

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getGender,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 58. Department-Wise Employee Names

Need:

```text
IT → [Amit, Rahul, Vijay]
HR → [Sneha, Neha]
```

Not:

```text
IT → [Employee, Employee]
```

Use `mapping()`:

```java
Map<String, List<String>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getName,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

Pattern:

```text
groupingBy(
    groupKey,
    mapping(
        whatYouNeed,
        toList()
    )
)
```

---

# 59. Department-Wise Salaries

```java
Map<String, List<Double>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                                 Employee::getSalary,
                                 Collectors.toList()
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# PART 9 — DEPARTMENT-WISE SALARY

# 60. Department-Wise Total Salary

🔥 Very common.

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.summingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                 ));
```

Pattern:

```text
Department-wise
→ groupingBy

Total
→ summingDouble
```

---

# 61. Department-Wise Average Salary

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.averagingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                 ));
```

---

# 62. Department-Wise Highest Paid Employee

🔥🔥 One of the most asked questions.

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                         )
                 ));
```

Break:

```text
Department-wise
       ↓
groupingBy

Highest
       ↓
maxBy

Based on?
       ↓
salary
```

---

# 63. Department-Wise Lowest Paid Employee

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.minBy(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 64. Department-Wise Highest Salary Only

If Employee object nahi, salary chahiye:

```java
Map<String, Optional<Double>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.mapping(
                             Employee::getSalary,
                             Collectors.maxBy(
                                 Double::compareTo
                             )
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 65. Department-Wise Salary Statistics

```java
Map<String, DoubleSummaryStatistics> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.summarizingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                 ));
```

Then each department has:

```text
count
sum
min
max
average
```

---

# PART 10 — PARTITIONING

# 66. Active and Inactive Employees

Exactly two groups:

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         Employee::isActive
                 ));
```

Result:

```text
true  → Active
false → Inactive
```

---

# 67. Male and Female — Can We Partition?

Technically condition based:

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         e -> "Male".equals(e.getGender())
                 ));
```

But:

```text
true  → Male
false → Not Male
```

So if gender has more than two possible values, `groupingBy(Employee::getGender)` is better.

---

# 68. Salary > 60000 vs Others

```java
Map<Boolean, List<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         e -> e.getSalary() > 60000
                 ));
```

---

# 69. Count Active vs Inactive

```java
Map<Boolean, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.partitioningBy(
                         Employee::isActive,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 70. GroupingBy vs PartitioningBy

Remember:

```text
How many possible groups?

Exactly 2 based on condition
→ partitioningBy()

Multiple categories
→ groupingBy()
```

Examples:

```text
Active / Inactive
→ partitioningBy

Pass / Fail
→ partitioningBy

Salary > 50k / not > 50k
→ partitioningBy

IT / HR / Finance / Sales
→ groupingBy

Male / Female / Other
→ groupingBy
```

---

# PART 11 — MATCH OPERATIONS

# 71. Any Employee Salary > 1 Lakh?

```java
boolean result =
        employees.stream()
                 .anyMatch(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 100000);
```

---

# 72. Are All Employees Active?

```java
boolean result =
        employees.stream()
                 .allMatch(Employee::isActive);
```

---

# 73. No Employee Has Negative Salary?

```java
boolean result =
        employees.stream()
                 .noneMatch(e ->
                         e.getSalary() < 0);
```

Memory:

```text
At least one?
→ anyMatch()

All?
→ allMatch()

None?
→ noneMatch()
```

---

🔥 Real project useful.

```java
Map<Integer, Employee> result =
```

```text
Key?
→ ID

Value?
→ Employee itself
```

```java
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getName
                 ));
```

# 78. Employee ID → Salary

```java
Map<Integer, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getId,
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

---

# 79. Employee Name → Salary

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

But if duplicate names possible:

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Employee::getSalary,
                         (oldSalary, newSalary) ->
                                 newSalary
                 ));
```

---

# 80. Employee Name → Employee

```java
Map<String, Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                         Employee::getName,
                         Function.identity()
                 ));
```

# PART 14 — MULTI-LEVEL GROUPING

🔥 Advanced interview area.

# 81. Department → Gender → Employees

```java
Map<String, Map<String, List<Employee>>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.groupingBy(
                                 Employee::getGender)
                 ));
```

Structure:

```text
IT
 ├─ Male   → [...]
 └─ Female → [...]

HR
 ├─ Male   → [...]
 └─ Female → [...]
```

Logic:

```text
First group
→ Department

Inside every department
→ Gender
```

---

# 82. Department → Gender → Count

```java
Map<String, Map<String, Long>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.groupingBy(
                             Employee::getGender,
                             Collectors.counting()
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 83. Department → Active Status → Employees

```java
Map<String, Map<Boolean, List<Employee>>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.partitioningBy(
                                 Employee::isActive)
                 ));
```

This combines:

```text
groupingBy
+
partitioningBy
```

# 84. Active IT Employee Names Sorted Salary Descending

```text
Active
↓ filter

IT
↓ filter

Salary descending
↓ sorted

Only names
↓ map

List
↓ collect
```

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 89. Department With Highest Number of Employees

First:

```text
Department → Count
```

Then:

```text
Maximum count
```

```java
Map.Entry<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ))
                 .entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .orElse(null);
```

🔥 Notice pipeline changes:

```text
List<Employee>
      ↓
groupingBy
      ↓
Map<String,Long>
      ↓
entrySet().stream()
      ↓
Stream<Entry<String,Long>>
      ↓
max
```

---

# 90. Department Name With Most Employees

Only department name:

```java
String department =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ))
                 .entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

```java
Map.Entry<String, Double> result =
```

# 92. Department With Highest Average Salary

```java
String department =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.averagingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)

                 .entrySet()

                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 93. Employees Earning More Than Average Salary

🔥🔥 Very important logic question.

First average calculate karo:

```java
double average =
        employees.stream()
                 .mapToDouble(Employee::getSalary)
                 .average()
                 .orElse(0.0);
```

Then:

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > average)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Don't force everything into one Stream.

Readable solution better hai.

---

# 94. Names of Employees Earning Above Average

```java
double average =
        employees.stream()
                 .mapToDouble(Employee::getSalary)
                 .average()
                 .orElse(0.0);

List<String> names =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > average)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 95. Employees With Same Salary

Sample:

```text
Rahul → 90000
Vijay → 90000
```

First group:

```java
Map<Double, List<Employee>> salaryGroups =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ));
```

Then duplicate salary groups:

```java
Map<Double, List<Employee>> result =
        salaryGroups.entrySet()
                    .stream()
                    .filter(e ->
                            e.getValue().size() > 1)
                    .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                            Map.Entry::getKey,
                            Map.Entry::getValue
                    ));
```

Logic:

```text
Same salary?
    ↓
Group by salary

Duplicate group?
    ↓
List size > 1
```

---

# 96. Duplicate Salaries Only

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getSalary,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ))
                 .entrySet()
                 .stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getValue() > 1)
                 .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 97. Employee Names Having Same Salary

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getSalary
                 ))
                 .values()
                 .stream()
                 .filter(list ->
                         list.size() > 1)
                 .flatMap(List::stream)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 Combines:

```text
groupingBy
+
filter
+
flatMap
+
map
```

---

# 98. Highest Salary Employees — Handle Tie

Suppose:

```text
Rahul → 90000
Vijay → 90000
```

If you use:

```java
.max(...)
```

you only get one employee.

If interviewer asks:

> **All employees having maximum salary**

First max salary:

```java
double maxSalary =
        employees.stream()
                 .mapToDouble(Employee::getSalary)
                 .max()
                 .orElse(0.0);
```

Then:

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() == maxSalary)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Result:

```text
Rahul
Vijay
```

---

# 99. Employees Having Second Distinct Highest Salary

🔥 Very important.

First find second distinct salary:

```java
Double secondHighest =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .skip(1)
                 .findFirst()
                 .orElse(null);
```

Then:

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         Double.compare(
                             e.getSalary(),
                             secondHighest
                         ) == 0)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

For production code, handle `secondHighest == null` before filtering.

Logic:

```text
Find salary level first
        ↓
Then employees with that salary
```

---

# 100. Top 3 Distinct Salaries of Active IT Employees

🔥 Great combined question.

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(3)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Decode:

```text
Active
↓ filter

IT
↓ filter

Only salary
↓ map

Unique salary
↓ distinct

Highest first
↓ sorted reverse

Top 3
↓ limit
```

---

# PART 16 — AGE SCENARIOS

# 101. Employees Age Between 25 and 35

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getAge() >= 25 &&
                         e.getAge() <= 35)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 102. Names of Employees Older Than 30

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getAge() > 30)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 103. Department-Wise Average Age

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.averagingInt(
                                 Employee::getAge)
                 ));
```

---

# 104. Department-Wise Oldest Employee

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                             Comparator.comparingInt(
                                 Employee::getAge)
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# PART 17 — GENDER SCENARIOS

# 105. Male/Female Count

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getGender,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# 106. Average Salary By Gender

```java
Map<String, Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getGender,
                         Collectors.averagingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                 ));
```

---

# 107. Highest Paid Employee By Gender

```java
Map<String, Optional<Employee>> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getGender,
                         Collectors.maxBy(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                 Employee::getSalary)
                         )
                 ));
```

---

# 108. Department-Wise Female Employee Count

First female:

```text
filter
```

then department count:

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         "Female".equals(e.getGender()))
                 .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                         Employee::getDepartment,
                         Collectors.counting()
                 ));
```

---

# PART 18 — STRING OPERATIONS WITH EMPLOYEE

# 109. Employee Names Starting With A

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .filter(name ->
                         name.startsWith("A"))
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Could also filter Employee first:

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getName().startsWith("A"))
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 110. Employee With Longest Name

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .max(
                     Comparator.comparingInt(
                         e -> e.getName().length())
                 )
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 111. Longest Employee Name Only

```java
String name =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .max(
                     Comparator.comparingInt(
                         String::length)
                 )
                 .orElse(null);
```

---

# 112. Sort Employees By Name Length

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingInt(
                         e -> e.getName().length())
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# PART 19 — ADVANCED COMPARATOR

# 113. Salary Descending, Same Salary Name Ascending

🔥 Very common tie-breaker.

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                             Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                         .thenComparing(
                             Employee::getName)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Think:

```text
First priority
→ Salary descending

Tie?
→ Name ascending
```

---

# 114. Department → Salary Desc → Name Asc

```java
List<Employee> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparing(
                             Employee::getDepartment)
                         .thenComparing(
                             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                                     Employee::getSalary)
                                 .reversed()
                         )
                         .thenComparing(
                             Employee::getName)
                 )
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# PART 20 — REDUCE WITH EMPLOYEE

# 115. Total Salary Using Reduce

Normally:

```java
mapToDouble().sum()
```

better hai.

But reduce practice:

```java
double total =
        employees.stream()
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .reduce(
                     0.0,
                     Double::sum
                 );
```

---

# 116. Employee With Maximum Salary Using Reduce

```java
Employee employee =
        employees.stream()
                 .reduce((e1, e2) ->
                         e1.getSalary() >= e2.getSalary()
                                 ? e1
                                 : e2)
                 .orElse(null);
```

But interview me normally prefer:

```java
.max(Comparator.comparingDouble(
        Employee::getSalary))
```

because intention clearer hai.

---

# 🔥 PART 21 — MOST IMPORTANT SCENARIO LOGIC

Interviewer:

> Find names of top 3 active female employees from IT department having salary greater than 50,000, sorted by salary descending.

Code ko mat dekho.

Pehle English break karo:

```text
Employees
   ↓

Active
   ↓
filter

Female
   ↓
filter

IT
   ↓
filter

Salary > 50000
   ↓
filter

Highest salary first
   ↓
sorted descending

Top 3
   ↓
limit(3)

Names only
   ↓
map

List
   ↓
collect
```

Now code:

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "Female".equals(e.getGender()))
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getSalary() > 50000)
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                         .reversed()
                 )
                 .limit(3)
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Ye hai actual **logic building**.

---

# 🧠 Most Important: Current Stream Type Track Karo

Start:

```java
employees.stream()
```

Current:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.filter(Employee::isActive)
```

Still:

```text
Stream<Employee>
```

Then:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

Now:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.sorted()
```

Still:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Then:

```java
.collect(Collectors.toList())
```

Final:

```text
List<String>
```

---

# ⭐ Why Operation Order Matters

Suppose requirement:

> Employee names salary descending order me.

Wrong thought:

```java
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .sorted(???)
```

Problem:

After:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

stream becomes:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Salary gone!

So salary se sort nahi kar sakte.

Correct:

```java
employees.stream()
         .sorted(
             Comparator.comparingDouble(
                 Employee::getSalary)
                 .reversed()
         )
         .map(Employee::getName)
```

Visual:

```text
Employee
   ↓
sort using salary
   ↓
Employee
   ↓
extract name
   ↓
String
```

### Golden rule:

> **Jis field ki information baad me chahiye, us information ko `map()` karke pehle mat hatao.**

---

# 🔥 Employee Question → Operation Cheat Sheet

| Requirement             | Pattern                          |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| IT employees            | `filter()`                       |
| Active employees        | `filter()`                       |
| Salary > X              | `filter()`                       |
| Employee → Name         | `map()`                          |
| Employee → Salary       | `map()`                          |
| Unique departments      | `map + distinct`                 |
| Salary ascending        | `sorted + comparingDouble`       |
| Salary descending       | `sorted + reversed`              |
| Highest salary employee | `max()`                          |
| Lowest salary employee  | `min()`                          |
| Second highest employee | `sorted + skip(1)`               |
| Second distinct salary  | `map + distinct + sorted + skip` |
| Top 3                   | `sorted + limit(3)`              |
| Count                   | `count()`                        |
| Total salary            | `mapToDouble + sum`              |
| Average salary          | `mapToDouble + average`          |
| Department-wise         | `groupingBy()`                   |
| Department count        | `groupingBy + counting`          |
| Department total        | `groupingBy + summingDouble`     |
| Department average      | `groupingBy + averagingDouble`   |
| Department highest      | `groupingBy + maxBy`             |
| Two groups              | `partitioningBy()`               |
| ID → Employee           | `toMap()`                        |
| Department → Names      | `groupingBy + mapping`           |
| Multiple grouping       | nested `groupingBy()`            |
| Exists                  | `anyMatch()`                     |
| All satisfy             | `allMatch()`                     |
| None satisfy            | `noneMatch()`                    |

---

# 🧠 Employee Program Banane Ka 7-Step Formula

Har naye Employee question me ye karo:

```text
STEP 1
Final output kya hai?
Employee / Name / Salary / List / Map / Count?


STEP 2
Kisko select karna hai?
        ↓
filter()


STEP 3
Koi field extract karna hai?
Employee → Name/Salary/Department
        ↓
map()


STEP 4
Duplicate remove?
        ↓
distinct()


STEP 5
Order chahiye?
        ↓
sorted() + Comparator


STEP 6
Highest / Group / Top N?
        ↓
max / groupingBy / limit


STEP 7
Final result?
        ↓
collect / count / sum / findFirst
```

For example:

> **"Top 2 unique salaries of active employees older than 30 from IT."**

Don't code immediately:

```text
Active
→ filter

Age > 30
→ filter

IT
→ filter

Employee → Salary
→ map

Unique
→ distinct

Highest first
→ sorted(reverseOrder)

Top 2
→ limit(2)

List
→ collect
```

Then:

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e -> e.getAge() > 30)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(2)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Yahi skill tumhe develop karni hai: **question → small requirements → Java 8 operations → pipeline**. Iske baad Employee ka completely naya question aaye, tab bhi tumhe pura program ratne ki zarurat nahi padegi.
============================================================================================================================================
# MODULE 20: Java 8 Interview Programs

Ab tak tumne individual patterns cover kiye:

```text
Stream Basics
Filter
Map
Sorting
Distinct
Max / Min
Count
Grouping
Partitioning
Reduce
FlatMap
Optional
Collectors
Comparator
String
Array
List
Map
Employee
```

**Module 20 ka goal:** interviewer mixed/scenario-based question dega aur tumhe identify karna hai ki **kaunsa pattern lagana hai**.

Main har important question me pehle **Logic → Pattern → Answer** dikha raha hoon.

---

# PART 1 — Numbers: Most Asked

Assume:

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(10, 5, 20, 10, 30, 5, 40, 25, 20);
```

## 1. Find Even Numbers

### Logic

```text
Selection/condition
→ filter()

Condition
→ n % 2 == 0
```

### Answer

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 2. Find Odd Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 != 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 3. Numbers Greater Than 20

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 20)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 4. Square Every Number

### Logic

```text
Value change
→ map()

n → n*n
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 5. Square Only Even Numbers

Think:

```text
Even select
→ filter

Square
→ map
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 6. Remove Duplicate Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 7. Sort Ascending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 8. Sort Descending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 9. Maximum Number

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .max(Integer::compareTo)
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 10. Minimum Number

```java
Integer min =
        numbers.stream()
               .min(Integer::compareTo)
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 11. Sum of Numbers

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .sum();
```

Using reduce:

```java
int sum =
        numbers.stream()
               .reduce(0, Integer::sum);
```

---

# 12. Average of Numbers

```java
double average =
        numbers.stream()
               .mapToInt(Integer::intValue)
               .average()
               .orElse(0.0);
```

---

# 13. Count Even Numbers

```java
long count =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .count();
```

---

# 14. Find First Number Greater Than 20

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n > 20)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 15. Second Highest Number

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

⚠️ This means **second element after sorting**.

---

# 16. Second Distinct Highest Number

🔥 Very common distinction.

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(1)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

Pattern:

```text
Unique
→ distinct

Highest first
→ reverse sorting

Ignore highest
→ skip(1)

Next
→ findFirst
```

---

# 17. Third Highest Distinct Number

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .skip(2)
               .findFirst()
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# 18. Top 3 Highest Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 19. Top 3 Distinct Highest Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 20. Bottom 3 Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .sorted()
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Important:

Agar interviewer bole:

> List ko sort nahi karna, original list ke first 3 elements do.

Then:

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

So question wording samajhna important hai.

---

# PART 2 — Duplicate / Frequency Programs

Assume:

```java
List<Integer> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3, 2, 4, 1, 5, 2);
```

# 21. Find Duplicate Numbers

### Master duplicate pattern

```text
Element
 ↓
groupingBy

Frequency
 ↓
counting

count > 1
 ↓
filter

Element
 ↓
getKey
```

```java
List<Integer> duplicates =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 22. Find Unique Elements Appearing Exactly Once

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Notice only condition changed:

```text
Duplicate
→ count > 1

Unique occurrence
→ count == 1
```

---

# 23. Frequency of Every Number

```java
Map<Integer, Long> frequency =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ));
```

---

# 24. Most Frequent Number

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .orElse(null);
```

Pipeline changes:

```text
List<Integer>
    ↓
groupingBy + counting
    ↓
Map<Integer,Long>
    ↓
entrySet
    ↓
max by value
```

---

# 25. Least Frequent Number

```java
Integer result =
        numbers.stream()
               .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                       Function.identity(),
                       Collectors.counting()
               ))
               .entrySet()
               .stream()
               .min(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
               .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
               .orElse(null);
```

---

# PART 3 — String Interview Programs

Assume:

```java
String input = "programming";
```

# 26. Character Frequency

```java
Map<Character, Long> frequency =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ));
```

---

# 27. Find Duplicate Characters

```java
List<Character> result =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Same duplicate pattern!

```text
Numbers duplicate
String character duplicate
Words duplicate

→ groupingBy + counting
```

---

# 28. Find Non-Repeated Characters

```java
List<Character> result =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     LinkedHashMap::new,
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Why `LinkedHashMap`?

Because original character order preserve karna hai.

---

# 29. First Non-Repeated Character

🔥 Very common.

```java
Character result =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     LinkedHashMap::new,
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .findFirst()
             .orElse(null);
```

Logic:

```text
Frequency
→ groupingBy + counting

Original order
→ LinkedHashMap

Non repeated
→ count == 1

First
→ findFirst
```

---

# 30. First Repeated Character

```java
Character result =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                     Function.identity(),
                     LinkedHashMap::new,
                     Collectors.counting()
             ))
             .entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .findFirst()
             .orElse(null);
```

---

# 31. Reverse String Using Java 8

Simple and practical:

```java
String reversed =
        new StringBuilder(input)
                .reverse()
                .toString();
```

Java 8 Stream force karna necessary nahi.

---

# 32. Palindrome Check

```java
String reversed =
        new StringBuilder(input)
                .reverse()
                .toString();

boolean palindrome =
        input.equals(reversed);
```

---

# 33. Count Vowels

```java
long count =
        input.toLowerCase()
             .chars()
             .filter(c ->
                     c == 'a' ||
                     c == 'e' ||
                     c == 'i' ||
                     c == 'o' ||
                     c == 'u')
             .count();
```

---

# 34. Extract Vowels

```java
List<Character> vowels =
        input.toLowerCase()
             .chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> (char) c)
             .filter(c ->
                     "aeiou".indexOf(c) >= 0)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 35. Remove Duplicate Characters

```java
String result =
        input.chars()
             .mapToObj(c -> String.valueOf((char) c))
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.joining());
```

Example:

```text
programming
→ progamin
```

# PART 4 — Sentence / Word Programs

Assume:

```java
String sentence =
        "java spring java kafka spring java";
```

# 36. Word Frequency

```java
Map<String, Long> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ));
```

---

# 37. Duplicate Words

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 38. Unique Words

If meaning is "remove duplicates":

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .distinct()
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

If meaning is "words occurring exactly once":

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                      Function.identity(),
                      Collectors.counting()
              ))
              .entrySet()
              .stream()
              .filter(e -> e.getValue() == 1)
              .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

🔥 Interview me clarify karo:

> "By unique, do you mean distinct words or words appearing exactly once?"

---

# 39. Longest Word

```java
String result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .max(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length)
              )
              .orElse(null);
```

---

# 40. Shortest Word

```java
String result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .min(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length)
              )
              .orElse(null);
```

---

# 41. Sort Words By Length

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .sorted(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length)
              )
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 42. Sort Words By Length Descending

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .sorted(
                  Comparator.comparingInt(
                      String::length)
                      .reversed()
              )
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 43. Find Words Starting With "J"

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .filter(word ->
                      word.toLowerCase().startsWith("j"))
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 44. Convert Every Word To Uppercase

```java
List<String> result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .map(String::toUpperCase)
              .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 45. Join Words With Comma

```java
String result =
        Arrays.stream(sentence.split("\\s+"))
              .collect(Collectors.joining(", "));
```

---

```java
              .sorted()
```

```java
              .distinct()

```

# 53. Second Highest Distinct From Array

```java
Integer result =
        Arrays.stream(arr)
              .boxed()
              .distinct()
              .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
              .skip(1)
              .findFirst()
              .orElse(null);
```

Why `boxed()`?

Because:

```text
Arrays.stream(int[])
→ IntStream

Comparator.reverseOrder()
→ Objects ke saath

int
 ↓ boxed
Integer
```

# PART 6 — Two Lists

Assume:

```java
List<Integer> list1 =
        Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3, 4, 5);

List<Integer> list2 =
        Arrays.asList(4, 5, 6, 7, 8);
```

# 54. Merge Two Lists

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 55. Merge Two Lists and Remove Duplicates

```java
List<Integer> result =
        Stream.concat(
                list1.stream(),
                list2.stream()
        )
        .distinct()
        .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 56. Common Elements Between Two Lists

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(list2::contains)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 57. Common Distinct Elements

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(list2::contains)
             .distinct()
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 58. Elements Present in List1 But Not List2

```java
List<Integer> result =
        list1.stream()
             .filter(n -> !list2.contains(n))
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# PART 7 — FlatMap Interview Questions

Assume:

```java
List<List<Integer>> numbers =
        Arrays.asList(
            Arrays.asList(1, 2, 3),
            Arrays.asList(4, 5),
            Arrays.asList(6, 7, 8)
        );
```

# 59. Flatten Nested List

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Think:

```text
Stream<List<Integer>>
        ↓ flatMap
Stream<Integer>
```

---

# 60. Flatten + Even Numbers

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 61. Flatten + Unique + Sorted

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .distinct()
               .sorted()
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 62. Maximum From Nested List

```java
Integer max =
        numbers.stream()
               .flatMap(List::stream)
               .max(Integer::compareTo)
               .orElse(null);
```

# PART 8 — Map Interview Questions

Assume:

```java
Map<String, Integer> marks = new HashMap<>();

marks.put("Amit", 80);
marks.put("Rahul", 95);
marks.put("Sneha", 70);
marks.put("Neha", 95);
```

# 63. Highest Value in Map

Only value required:

```java
Integer max =
        marks.values()
             .stream()
             .max(Integer::compareTo)
             .orElse(null);
```

---

# 64. Key Having Highest Value

Need key + value:

```java
String name =
        marks.entrySet()
             .stream()
             .max(Map.Entry.comparingByValue())
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .orElse(null);
```

---

# 65. Sort Map By Value Descending

```java
Map<String, Integer> result =
        marks.entrySet()
             .stream()
             .sorted(
                 Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                         comparingByValue()
                         .reversed()
             )
             .collect(Collectors.toMap(
                     Map.Entry::getKey,
                     Map.Entry::getValue,
                     (a, b) -> a,
                     LinkedHashMap::new
             ));
```

---

# 66. Top 2 Entries By Value

```java
List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>> result =
        marks.entrySet()
             .stream()
             .sorted(
                 Map.Entry.<String, Integer>
                         comparingByValue()
                         .reversed()
             )
             .limit(2)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 67. Keys Having Value > 75

```java
List<String> result =
        marks.entrySet()
             .stream()
             .filter(e -> e.getValue() > 75)
             .map(Map.Entry::getKey)
             .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

```text
and
```

# PART 12 — Advanced Mixed Interview Programs

# 87. Top 3 Distinct Even Numbers

Decode:

```text
Even
→ filter

Unique
→ distinct

Highest
→ descending

Top 3
→ limit
```

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .distinct()
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .limit(3)
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

---

# 88. Square Distinct Even Numbers and Sort Descending

```java
List<Integer> result =
        numbers.stream()
               .filter(n -> n % 2 == 0)
               .distinct()
               .map(n -> n * n)
               .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
               .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

# 94. Gender Count Inside Each Department

🔥 Nested grouping.

```text
Department
   ↓
groupingBy

Inside department
   ↓
gender grouping

Inside gender
   ↓
```
