# PART 13 — Questions Where People Make Mistakes

# 95. Employee Names Sorted By Salary

❌ Wrong:

```java
employees.stream()
         .map(Employee::getName)
         .sorted();
```

Why?

After:

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

stream is:

```text
Stream<String>
```

Salary information gone.

Correct:

```java
List<String> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .sorted(
                     Comparator.comparingDouble(
                         Employee::getSalary)
                 )
                 .map(Employee::getName)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

Golden rule:

> **Jis information ki sorting/filtering ke liye zarurat hai, use `map()` se pehle mat hatao.**

---

# 96. `distinct()` on Employee

This:

```java
employees.stream()
         .distinct()
```

duplicate Employee remove tabhi correctly karega jab `Employee` me appropriate:

```java
equals()
hashCode()
```

implemented hain.

Important interview point.

---

# 97. `findFirst()` vs `findAny()`

```java
stream.findFirst()
```

encounter order ka first element.

```java
stream.findAny()
```

koi bhi matching element de sakta hai; parallel streams me useful ho sakta hai.

---

# 98. `map()` vs `flatMap()`

```text
Employee → Name
→ map

String → Uppercase String
→ map

List<List<Integer>> → List<Integer>
→ flatMap

Employee → List<String> skills,
all skills ek flat stream me chahiye
→ flatMap
```

---

# 99. `map()` vs `filter()`

```text
filter()
→ element select/remove

map()
→ element transform
```

Example:

```java
.filter(e -> e.getSalary() > 50000)
```

Employee remains Employee.

```java
.map(Employee::getName)
```

Employee becomes String.

---

# 100. `groupingBy()` vs `partitioningBy()`

```text
Department:
IT / HR / Finance / Sales
→ groupingBy

Gender categories
→ groupingBy

Active / Inactive
→ partitioningBy

Pass / Fail
→ partitioningBy

Even / Odd
→ partitioningBy
```

---

# 🔥 PART 14 — Interviewer Gives Unknown Question

Suppose interviewer asks:

> Find top 2 unique salaries of active IT employees older than 30.

Tumne exact program kabhi nahi dekha.

No problem.

Break it:

```text
Employees
    ↓

Active
    ↓
filter

IT
    ↓
filter

Age > 30
    ↓
filter

Only salary needed
    ↓
map

Unique
    ↓
distinct

Highest first
    ↓
sorted(reverse)

Top 2
    ↓
limit(2)

List
    ↓
collect
```

Now code:

```java
List<Double> result =
        employees.stream()
                 .filter(Employee::isActive)
                 .filter(e ->
                         "IT".equals(e.getDepartment()))
                 .filter(e ->
                         e.getAge() > 30)
                 .map(Employee::getSalary)
                 .distinct()
                 .sorted(Comparator.reverseOrder())
                 .limit(2)
                 .collect(Collectors.toList());
```

**Yehi skill chahiye. Program ratna nahi.**

---

# 🧠 Interview Keyword → Java 8 Operation

| Interviewer says     | Think                    |
| -------------------- | ------------------------ |
| whose / where / only | `filter()`               |
| convert / get names  | `map()`                  |
| remove duplicates    | `distinct()`             |
| ascending            | `sorted()`               |
| descending           | `sorted(reverseOrder)`   |
| object sorting       | `Comparator.comparing()` |
| highest              | `max()`                  |
| lowest               | `min()`                  |
| second               | `skip(1)`                |
| third                | `skip(2)`                |
| top N                | `limit(n)`               |
| total                | `sum()`                  |
| average              | `average()`              |
| how many             | `count()`                |
| department-wise      | `groupingBy()`           |
| frequency            | `groupingBy + counting`  |
| two groups           | `partitioningBy()`       |
| nested collection    | `flatMap()`              |
| key/value result     | `toMap()`                |
| combine all          | `reduce()`               |
| exists               | `anyMatch()`             |
| all satisfy          | `allMatch()`             |
| none satisfy         | `noneMatch()`            |
| first                | `findFirst()`            |
| null safety          | `Optional`               |

---

# 🔥 PART 15 — 50 Questions For You To Solve Without Seeing Answer

Ab ye **actual practice** hai. Pehle khud operation pattern likhna, phir code.

1. Find odd numbers greater than 20.
2. Find distinct even numbers.
3. Find top 5 distinct numbers.
4. Find bottom 3 distinct numbers.
5. Find second smallest distinct number.
6. Find third highest distinct number.
7. Find duplicate numbers.
8. Find numbers occurring exactly once.
9. Find frequency of numbers.
10. Find most frequent number.
11. Find duplicate characters in String.
12. Find first non-repeated character.
13. Find first repeated character.
14. Find character frequency.
15. Find duplicate words.
16. Find word frequency.
17. Find longest word.
18. Find second longest distinct word by length.
19. Sort words by length descending.
20. Find words starting with vowel.
21. Find common elements in two Lists.
22. Find elements only in first List.
23. Merge two Lists and remove duplicates.
24. Flatten nested List.
25. Flatten nested List and find duplicates.
26. Find highest Map value.
27. Find key of highest Map value.
28. Sort Map by value descending.
29. Find top 3 Map entries.
30. Find duplicate Map values.
31. Find highest paid employee.
32. Find second distinct highest salary.
33. Find employees having second distinct highest salary.
34. Find top 3 highest-paid employees.
35. Find active IT employees.
36. Find active IT employee names.
37. Sort employees salary descending.
38. Sort salary descending, same salary name ascending.
39. Group employees by department.
40. Count employees by department.
41. Department-wise total salary.
42. Department-wise average salary.
43. Department-wise highest paid employee.
44. Department-wise active employee count.
45. Department-wise employee names.
46. Department with maximum employees.
47. Employees earning above average.
48. Employees with duplicate salaries.
49. Top 3 active female employees by salary.
50. Department → Gender → Employee count.

---

# ⭐ Most Important 20 Programs Before Interview

Agar interview kal hai, minimum ye **20 patterns** strong hone chahiye:

```text
1. Even / Odd
2. Max / Min
3. Second highest
4. Second distinct highest
5. Top N
6. Duplicate elements
7. Frequency
8. First non-repeated character
9. Duplicate characters
10. Word frequency
11. Longest word
12. Common elements between Lists
13. Flatten nested List
14. Sort Map by value
15. Highest-paid Employee
16. Top N Employees
17. Group Employee by department
18. Department-wise count
19. Department-wise average/total salary
20. Department-wise highest-paid Employee
```

---

# 🧠 Final Master Formula — Don't Write Code First

Interviewer question:

> **"Top 3 active IT employee names with unique salaries greater than 50k, highest salary first."**

First write mentally:

```text
Active
→ filter

IT
→ filter

Salary > 50k
→ filter

Unique salary?
→ careful! distinct Employee won't solve this automatically

Highest salary first
→ sort

Top 3
→ limit

Names
→ map
```

Notice **"unique salary"** made the problem harder. This is exactly why blindly memorizing:

```text
filter → map → distinct → sorted
```

is dangerous.

Instead ask:

```text
What exactly needs to be unique?
Employee?
Name?
Salary?
Department?
```

That's the main skill.

---

```text
                    JAVA 8 QUESTION

          │                              │
       filter()                        map()
          │                              │
          └───────────────┬──────────────┘
                          │
                     DUPLICATE?
                          │
                      distinct()
                          │
                       ORDER?
                          │
                       sorted()
                          │
                 ┌────────┴────────┐
                 │                 │
              HIGHEST?           TOP N?
                 │                 │
               max()             limit()
                 │

                 │

                 │
             groupingBy()
                 │
        ┌────────┼──────────┐
        │        │          │
      COUNT     SUM       AVERAGE
        │        │          │
    counting  summing    averaging
        │

        │
 ┌──────┼──────────┬─────────┐
 │      │          │         │
List   Map       Single     Count
 │      │          │         │
collect toMap   Optional    count
```

Tumhare **Modules 1–20** ke baad focus ab aur programs collect karne par nahi hona chahiye. Ab best next step hai **mixed scenario practice without hints**, jahan pehle tum khud solution try karo aur phir answer compare karo. Ye memorization se actual logic-building ki taraf shift karega.
